# Lootpath - the central brain

This document is the single source of truth for what Lootpath is, how it works, every decision made and why, what is verified and what is not, and where the work stands. **Every session updates this file before it ends, after anything is learned, discussed, decided, created or worked on** - not only when a PR changes code. A conversation that settles a question, refutes a premise, or moves the plan is recorded here in the same session, through a docs-only PR if nothing else is in flight. If this file and the code disagree, say so in the PR and fix whichever is wrong; never leave them disagreeing. **A new session reads §0 first**, then the sections §0 points at.

Last updated: 2026-09-15, night (**R-3d built, PR open** (WKE-584): a vault reward the rating never imported no longer reads `not in your best set`, and is no longer told to be skipped. C-8's premise - every vault option is active from the moment it is imported - is about a reward the run IMPORTED, and the run imports nothing the profile did not carry; on reset day before the player opens the Great Vault the profile has no vault section at all. So the verdict file now carries `profileVaultCount` (C-13, a number, because ZERO is the case), `ns.Roads.VaultConsidered` answers true / false / nil over that count and over every pass's recorded pool, and a `false` puts the road in the no-rating group with `not rated - new since the last refresh` and the sentence "The plan hasn't rated this yet. Refresh, then look again." A run with no vault at all also takes the Vault tab's whole headline: "The plan was rated before your vault was generated. Refresh." A document that recorded neither a pool nor a count is left exactly where 558 left it. Seven guards proven red on the addon side and two on the companion's; the owner's own reset-day block asserted line for line, before and after, off snapshot 12 of his 14:27 pull. Earlier that night: **V-4 built, PR open** (WKE-589): the hour is off the clocks. `ns.EpochFromISO` paired its two `mktime` calls at last - the offset table said `isdst = false` and the stamp table said nothing, so during daylight time the offset came out an hour too large and every age and every wall clock the addon printed was an hour too old. Both tables now say `false`, which is what `Lootpath/UI/VaultPanel.lua`'s own reader has always done and which was measured exact against musl's `mktime` under `TZ=America/Chicago` for a two-minute-old stamp in summer and in winter, across the November fall-back, and for a stamp half a year old. The stub grew a MODELLED clock (`world.setClock`), because CI's container is UTC and carries no timezone database at all, so no daylight fault could be made red against the real `os.date`. Six guards proven red, and one of them answers a question §11 left open: R-6's wait could NEVER have shown on the owner's client during daylight time - the elapsed time it computed was 3600 s too large, cleared the ten-minute give-up bound on the first check, and deleted itself before anything drew it. Earlier that night: **M3-16b built, PR open** (WKE-583): the reset-day vault answered off the owner's 14:27 pull. **The Great Vault WINDOW generates the week's rewards and `OnUIInteract` does not** - four refreshes asked and were answered in 66.7-130.9 ms, and every one of them read back the 10 activities and 0 reward links it already had; the capture the owner took with the window open carried 11 activities, 5 with rewards and 9 links. So no second exception was built: the Vault tab says `open the Great Vault, then refresh` when rewards are waiting and none are generated. What did change: the `vault` capture reads on EVERY `WEEKLY_REWARDS_UPDATE` inside the bound and settles on the read that carries rewards, recording each update's stamp and contents; the flush no longer captures the vault at all, because the owner's 19:09:31Z plain read shadowed his 19:09:29Z asked one and was what the companion built `0 vault` from; the companion CHOOSES its vault snapshot - the newest in the reward period that carries links, a read that asked winning among those - and logs which; and the wait indicator says itself where it can be seen, as a chat line at the first load after a refresh, a minimap badge while it stands, and the FIRST clause on the status strip, with the four facts it displaces moved into the tooltip. Eleven guards proven red, five gates green, PR #138 open and not merged.)

Before that: 2026-09-15, evening (**R-7a built, PR open** (WKE-582): two faults in R-7, both measured on the owner's 13:58 pull, neither a fault in what R-7 set out to do. **`PLAYER_LOGOUT` is an unload event, not a logout event** - it fires on `/reload` too, so R-7's four captures ran on every reload and stamped each one `logout`; the pull carried five such `env` snapshots for at most one logout, and the companion printed `run after logout (gear captured at logout)` for a plain reload. Nothing at the moment the event fires can tell the two apart, so the sequence keeps running on both - it is why the second reload of the refresh loop carries fresh gear - and everything it writes now says `flush`: the label, `capturedOn`, the vault's `interact.skipped`, `logoutMs` renamed `flushMs`, `CaptureAtLogout` renamed `CaptureAtFlush`, and the companion's line `run after a logout or reload (gear captured at the flush)`. **And nothing trimmed a capture list**, so SavedVariables went 6.1 -> 7.1 -> 11.0 MB in two days, 35 `inventory` snapshots worth 5.85 MB of it, read and written whole at every login and every reload; `storeSnapshot` now keeps the newest `ns.CAPTURE_HISTORY` (4) per name. **The sequence's real cost is measured for the first time: 19.5-33.1 ms on the owner's client**, five runs, against the 250 ms the issue offered as a budget. Five guards proven red; 1152 busted and 219 companion tests green. Nothing of it has been on a real screen. See §0, §5, §6, §7, §9, §11.)

Before that: 2026-09-14 (**V-1 built, PR open, not merged** (WKE-569): the source-free voice applied to the three tabs that predate the 2026-09-11 decision. Seventy-three player-facing string literals named the engine; all seventy-three are swept, and `spec/voice_spec.lua` is the guard that keeps them swept - a rendered half that drives the real window over the committed fixtures and reads back every line, cell, refusal and slash message, and a constant half that walks the panels' string tables so a branch no fixture reaches is caught too. **The sweep found one hole that was not in the issue:** four of the Upgrade Map's badge sentences live in branches the fixtures never reach - a zero percent, a rating with no delta - and as inline literals neither half of the guard could see them, so they were lifted into named `BADGE_*` constants like every other string in the file. **Two strings were looked at and deliberately left, and both are named in the PR:** the Vault tab's `thisWeek` scenario label, which names no source and whose parenthetical is the only place the tab says what that scenario assumed, and `ValueBadge`'s `prefix`, through which the Vault tab passes a scenario name. Nothing on screen moved but the words: no number, no ordering and no count changed. 948 busted tests (`main` had 943), five gates green. Before that: 2026-09-13 (**T-1 built, PR open, not merged** (WKE-560): the headless stub now models each Blizzard widget's OWN mixin chain and nothing more, which closes the 2026-09-10 risk in §11. Every kind and every template `spec/stubs/wow.lua` recognises was diffed, method by method, against Ketho's annotations under `.luals/`; the audit found the dropdown fix of PR #72 was only the visible half. **The real finding was structural, not per-template:** `newRegion` handed EVERY widget the union of Region + FontString + Texture, and `newFrame` handed every frame ScrollFrame's five scroll setters, so a bare `Frame` answered `SetText`, `SetTexture`, `SetTexCoord` and `SetScrollChild`, a `FontString` answered `SetAtlas`, and a `Texture` answered `SetText`. **And the dropdown fix itself still leaked:** it handed `SetDefaultText` to the KIND and subtracted it inside `attachTemplate`, so a `CreateFrame("DropdownButton")` with no template never reached the subtraction and answered it headlessly. Nineteen method names left the widgets they do not belong on; seven stub-only helpers (`Enter`, `Leave`, `Pick`, `SelectedIndex`, `SelectByText`, `Acquire`, `ReleaseAll`, and the tooltip's `Text`) moved off Blizzard's namespace onto a `widget.stub` shelf. One gap the audit EXPOSED was real: `DropdownButton` is a `Button` (`Intrinsic/DropdownButton.lua:9`) and the Upgrade Map calls `SetText` on it, which 31 tests caught the moment the union came apart. New `spec/stubs_spec.lua`, 18 tests, locks every removal; 894 busted tests (876 on `main`), 16 mutations proven red one at a time, five gates green locally. **Nothing on screen changed and no client was involved.** See §0, §6, §7, §11.)

Before that: 2026-09-13 (**C-9 built, PR open, not merged**: the companion is visible from inside the game. It now writes two files beside the verdict - `Lootpath/Data/companion.log`, every line it prints with a full date stamp, rotated at 200 KB keeping one `.1`; and `Lootpath/Data/CompanionStatus.lua`, a data-only chunk carrying the last run's `state` (`idle` / `running` / `skipped` / `failed`), `stage`, stamps and exit code, rewritten at EVERY stage change - and the addon loads the second from its `.toc` and says one clause of it on the status strip: `companion: wrote 3 minute(s) ago`, `run started 22:48`, `profile unchanged, no run (23:06)`, `FAILED at profile (21:06) - see companion.log`, `never seen`. `skipped` is its own state because "nothing to do" and "nothing happened" are exactly the two the owner could not tell apart (§11, 2026-09-09). **Start with Windows** is answered: `tools/companion/install-startup.ps1` registers an at-logon Task Scheduler task running `start-companion.ps1` (the fork if its port is dead, then `node companion.js --watch`), `uninstall-startup.ps1` removes it, and "never two watchers" is enforced by a lock file - a second watcher exits 7 naming the pid, a dead one's lock is taken over. 898 busted tests (`main` was 876) and 186 companion tests (`main` was 155), 37 guards proven red one at a time, five of the six gates green locally. **Two things nothing here claims:** the Task Scheduler task has never been run - there is no Windows or logon in CI, so the owner registers it once and reboots - and the LuaLS gate could not be run in this environment (its binary is a GitHub release download the session cannot reach); CI runs it. See §0, §5, §6, §7, §11.)

## 0. Where we are (read this first; keep it current)

**As of 2026-09-15, night (M3-16b built, PR open: the reset-day vault answered, the flush's vault read dropped, the companion's vault snapshot chosen rather than assumed, and the wait indicator on three surfaces in the owner's own words). As of 2026-09-09, night (`main` at 074e511 after PR #59; every `ai-ready` issue 514-548 and 555 is Done; the M5 lane 549-554 is filed, 551 M5-2 is merged, 550 M5-1 is merged, and 552 M5-3 is built with its PR open).** Everything below is a pointer; the detail is in the numbered sections.

**Merged to `main`:** PR #1 this document; PR #2 WKE-514 skeleton, five CI gates, captures `env`/`inventory`/`vault`; PR #3 WKE-515 first in-client transcript, branch protection; PR #4 WKE-516 Inventory module and `ns.ParseItemLink`; PR #5 WKE-518 QEImport parser (its real-fixture test is `pending` until 519); PR #6 WKE-527 QE Live fork branch and the finding that the live site has no Download JSON control; PR #7 WKE-522 part 1, the Journal adapter, async captures and `/lootpath capture journal` (merged 2026-09-06 20:56 UTC); PR #8 this section and the one-worktree-per-issue rule; PR #9 the pre-Tuesday plan (docs); PR #10 WKE-523 first visit, the 2026-09-06 journal transcript and before-reset vault pair; PR #11 WKE-519 the first genuine QE Live export; PR #12 WKE-522 part 2, `ns.Journal` and the walk's second read (merged 22:16 UTC); PR #13 the brain sync and the client re-sync; **PR #14 WKE-520, `ns.Match`, the paste editbox, the Equip Now panel and the Settings page**; PR #16 `tools\check.ps1` failing its gate when StyLua or LuaLS is missing instead of passing it; **PR #15 WKE-524 (M3-3), `ns.Vault`, the Upgrade Map and Vault panels, and their tabs in the window**; **PR #21 WKE-530 (M3-5), the five UI findings from the first in-game run**; PR #24 WKE-531 (S-1), the fork driven headless.

**Merged since:** PR #24 (S-1, WKE-531): the headless fork driver, the `/simc` fixture and the two Upgrade Finder fixtures. PR #26 (S-2, WKE-532): the SimC profile builder, its SavedVariables reader, 32 Node guards, and four new reads in `capture env` (`UnitLevel`, `UnitRace`, `GetCurrentRegionName`, `GetCurrentRegion`). PR #25 (C-2, WKE-534): the addon half - `Lootpath/Data/QEVerdict.lua` (a committed placeholder that sets nothing) in the `.toc` after `Core.lua`, `ns.Companion` validating and importing it at load through `ns.QEImport.Parse`, per-content-type storage with the later of file and paste winning, the source on the window's verdict line and in `/lootpath status`, `/lootpath refresh` (out of combat only), and `tools\sync.ps1` keeping the companion's file unless `-IncludeData`. **PR #27 (C-1, WKE-533): the companion itself** - `tools/companion/`, which lifts S-1's fork driver and S-2's profile builder into one program and writes C-2's contract. PR #31 (C-3, WKE-536): `/lootpath refresh` takes the `env`, `inventory` and `vault` captures before it reloads. **PR #28 (M3-6, WKE-535): the Upgrade Finder import** - `Modules/UFImport.lua`, the paste box routing on schema, `ns.Companion` handing `qe-live-upgradefinder` documents to it instead of refusing them, and the Upgrade Map's second path to a number (itemID + item level, exact or nothing).

**Merged 2026-09-09:** PR #45 (WKE-545, M3-10, the cross-level join) and PR #46 (WKE-540, C-6, the named scenarios).

**Open:** **584 R-3d BUILT, PR open, not merged (2026-09-15 night): the vault reward nothing rated.** The companion writes `profileVaultCount` into the verdict file, the addon carries it onto every Top Gear verdict, and `ns.Roads.VaultConsidered` decides a vault road's phrase off the document's record of its POOL rather than off the document's existence. Nine guards proven red; nothing of it has been on a real screen, and the owner's next refresh with an unclaimed vault is the first look. **585 R-3e edits the same vault road - whichever of the two lands second rebases onto the first.** **589 V-4 merged** (PR #141, `3391827`, synced): both tables say `isdst = false` so the standard-time reading cancels; measured under a real timezone database; R-6's wait could never have appeared during daylight time and now can. **590 R-6a is unblocked.** **589 V-4 BUILT, PR open, not merged (2026-09-15 night): the hour is off every age and clock.** `ns.EpochFromISO` built its UTC offset from a table carrying `isdst = false` and read the stamp from a table carrying nothing, and `mktime` reads that field, so during daylight time the offset was an hour too large. Both tables now say `false` and the two interpretations cancel. Six guards proven red - including the owner's own screen, `companion, written 62 minutes ago` and `(16:20)` where it should read 2 minutes and `(17:20)` - and R-6's wait, which the same hour had been deleting on its first check. The stub gained a modelled clock (`world.setClock`, §7) because CI's container is UTC. Nothing of it has been on a real screen; the owner's next refresh is the look. **590 R-6a (ready, after 589):** the load after a refresh always says one line; today a refresh the companion skipped as unchanged said nothing, which was correct and silent at the moment the player asked. **583 M3-16b merged** (PR #138, `69d4607`, synced): the vault capture reads on every update; the flush no longer captures the vault; the companion prefers the read that carries links, then the one that asked; the Vault tab says open the Great Vault then refresh on reset day; the wait indicator on three surfaces (chat line, minimap badge, the strip's first clause). **What the product does today (all merged and in the owner's client):**
- **The loop:** `/lootpath refresh` takes the `env`, `inventory`, `vault` and `currencies` snapshots and reloads - **and since R-7 (WKE-579) the same four are taken at `PLAYER_LOGOUT` too**, so logging out leaves a current plan waiting at the next login and refresh is for catching up mid-session; **since R-7a (WKE-582, PR open) that event is understood as what it is - an unload, which a `/reload` is too - so the captures run on both and are labelled `flush` rather than `logout`, and no capture list keeps more than `ns.CAPTURE_HISTORY` (4) snapshots** - since M3-16 it waits for the vault capture's own question to the client before it reloads, and says `waiting for the vault` on the strip while it does - **and since M3-16a (WKE-581, PR open) that question is asked once at login instead, so the wait is the exception and not the rule; when it does happen the refresh asks for the reload with a box rather than calling `ReloadUI` from the wait, which the client refuses**; the companion (`tools/companion`, `node companion.js --watch`) reads the SavedVariables, builds the SimC profile, drives the owner's local QE Live fork headless and writes `Lootpath/Data/QEVerdict.lua`; a second `/lootpath refresh` loads it. No `/simc`, no browser, no paste. A refresh with unchanged gear costs nothing (C-4). Twelve Top Gear documents come back per run - `asOffered` / `catalyzed` / `maxed` x Dungeon / Raid (C-6) - plus the Upgrade Finder at +2, +4, +6, +8, +10 and Raid (C-7); all three QE Live import boxes are set explicitly, both upgrade boxes off by default (C-5). **Since R-6 (WKE-578, PR open) the loop announces itself:** a second row under the status strip reads `your gear changed since this plan (2 items) · click to refresh` whenever a gear key has appeared, gone or changed since the plan was written, the launcher wears a small gold badge while it is up, and the click runs `ns.Companion.Refresh` - the same function `/lootpath refresh` runs. After that reload the WAIT takes the strip's own first clause - `rating your gear, started 30 seconds ago, usually ready in about 45 seconds · click to load it`, counting, with the four facts it displaces in the strip's tooltip and the click on the strip's own row - the launcher keeps its badge while it stands, and the chat frame says `your gear is sent; the rating usually takes about 3 minutes. The window says when it's ready.` once at that load (M3-16b, WKE-583, PR open; R-6 had it on the second row, where the owner could not find it).
- **Equip Now:** QE Live's `asOffered` top set joined to the scan by exact item key; Equip and Equip all work in game (521); a vault option in his best set is a pointer to the Vault tab, not a "not owned" swap (541). **Since 550 (M5-1, PR open) a row is an item line** - the item's own icon, a quality-tinted `WhiteIconFrame` border, the item level in the icon's corner, the name in quality colour, a grey second line and a status badge - a swap is drawn as worn icon → arrow → best icon with the Equip button, the five counts are chips above the list, and the list scrolls.
- **Upgrade Map:** by slot (every drop by item level, his `upgradePercent` where a key-level document carries the item at the client's level - the cross-level join, 545) and by run (best upgrade / most upgrades, never probability-weighted - 542); dungeon runs rank since 545. **Since 552 (M5-3, PR open) both views are a `WowScrollBoxList` over a data provider**: by slot is a collapsible section per slot, headed by the icon and level of what that slot wears, over M5-1 item lines whose second line is `boss - instance, difficulty`, with `[owned 305]` as the Owned tag and his verdict as the badge with `(at +6)` in grey after it; by run is a card per run - instance art, name, difficulty, `best +1.83%` as the badge, the drop count in grey - that opens onto its rated drops. One difficulty dropdown replaces the row of buttons. Which sections are shut and which cards are open is remembered per character.

- **Vault:** the real reward shape (538); one line per scenario under each gear option (540, and since 548 there are **four** scenarios, `asOffered` / `catalyzed` / `thisWeek` / `maxed`, with `thisWeek` the default highlight); a headline block leading with his pick under the owner's chosen scenario, "none - nothing beats your set; closest: …" when that is the truth, the five Mistcrest counts from the client, and the Catalyst charge (544/548), which 546 now reads by currency ID - Venomblight Manaflux 3465 - so the line says "needs a Catalyst charge (you have 1 of 8)" off the live client and keeps saying "not readable" over the 2026-09-08 transcript, which genuinely does not know. **547 (M3-12, PR open):** a reward the client has not loaded yet reads `name pending (item 275547) - level pending` - or `Preyhunter's Lantern (from the last capture) - level pending` when a stored vault snapshot names the same reward - never `[] (nil)`; the client is asked for the item and the tab redraws once when it answers, and its counts, its scenario lines and the pick are unchanged because the key was never lost. **555 (M3-14):** a **fifth** headline line under the four scenario lines - `one charge (this week, Catalyst used once): catalyze your Hide of Pestilence (302) into the tier chest - 1.73% behind` - read off the `thisWeek` document's own alternatives, and `not in QE Live's export - no set he ranked spends the charge just once` when none of his sets qualifies. It is absent entirely when the question does not arise (no `thisWeek` document, the Catalyst box off in the run that made one, or no inventory scan). **556 (M3-15, PR open) corrects what "the charge" counts:** converting a Great Vault reward spends one too, so every clone is counted and on the owner's own documents that fifth line is the absence sentence; the scenario lines gained the vault half of the catalyze sentence - `and catalyze your Hide of Pestilence (302) into the tier chest and the vault's Scavenger's Spaulders (308) into the tier shoulder` - which is said even with no bags read.
- **The Vault tab is a grid since 553 (M5-4, PR open).** Three rows of three cells - Raids, Dungeons, World, in Blizzard's order and under `RAIDS`/`DUNGEONS`/`WORLD` when the client has them - each cell the reward the client offers there drawn as an M5-1 item line, with the slot and M3-7's level wording under the name and the highlighted scenario's own line below that. A cell with no reward says Blizzard's own threshold sentence (the activity's `raidString` on a Raid row, `WEEKLY_REWARDS_THRESHOLD_*` otherwise), falling back to the progress wording. The pick wears the Great Vault's own `evergreen-weeklyrewards-reward-selected`; when the headline says `none - nothing in the vault beats your set`, nothing glows and the closest option is labelled `closest`. Every scenario's line is in the cell's tooltip, verbatim from `Panel.ScenarioLine`, so the hover and the text panel cannot disagree. The scenario dropdown sits on the tab. Under the grid: a currency strip with the client's own icons and names, and every option Blizzard's frame does not draw (Concession, "Also receive") named rather than dropped. `Panel.Lines` did not change, so `/lootpath status` and every text test read exactly what they did.

**Hazards a new session must know:** **The companion reads capture snapshots, not live gear** - which is why `/lootpath refresh` takes them (C-3, merged in PR #31). `simc-profile.js` builds the profile from the newest `env`, `inventory` and `vault` snapshots in `db.global.captures`; on 2026-09-07, when refresh only reloaded, the first companion run produced a profile with `spec=guardian` from the 09-05 capture, missing `region`/`level`/`race` because that `env` capture predates S-2's reads. **Until the client is synced to `main` at or past 85dcacc**, the real loop is still `/lootpath capture env`, `/lootpath capture inventory` (bank open if wanted), `/lootpath capture vault`, `/lootpath refresh`, wait for the companion's "wrote" line, `/lootpath refresh`; after it, the two refreshes are the whole loop, and opening the bank first is the only thing left to remember. **Linear closes an issue when a merged PR's title or body names it** - docs-only PR #20, titled "Brain: WKE-530 UI polish filed", marked WKE-530 Done before a line of it was built; the 530 agent found the issue closed and worked it anyway. Name an issue in a PR title only when that PR completes it; in a docs PR write "the 530 issue" or put the ID in the body after "Closes WKE-" is filled or removed. Also: an agent's mis-quoted path left an empty `C:\Code\lootpath-530;C` directory on disk (deleted 2026-09-06 21:5x); check `ls C:\Code` after a session ends. `tools\sync.ps1` is a manual step, and the client silently runs whatever was pushed last - check the AddOns folder's timestamps against `main` before asking the owner to run a capture (found 2026-09-06: the client was one day and five files behind). Several sessions run at once, one issue each, so **create your own worktree before the first edit** (CLAUDE.md, §12); two sessions collided in the shared checkout on 2026-09-06. A PR that conflicts with `main` gets no CI run at all, so zero checks means rebase, not broken CI. Ketho's annotations are one patch behind the client (§11). `jq` is not on this machine. **`stylua` and `lua-language-server` are winget installs that are not on the default PATH of a fresh shell**, so a session prepends `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\WinGet\Packages\JohnnyMorganz.StyLua_*` and `...\LuaLS.lua-language-server_*\bin` (or refreshes `$env:Path` from the User scope) before running the gates. Until PR #16 a missing tool was reported as PASS, because the command-not-found error left `$LASTEXITCODE` at 0; `tools\check.ps1` now fails that gate and names the tool, so a green means the gate ran. **Proving a guard red destroys uncommitted work unless you commit first**: on 2026-09-15 the R-7 session broke a guard, ran the spec, and restored with `git checkout -- Lootpath/` - which reverted not the one broken line but every uncommitted addon change it had made that session, and the next two guards silently found no pattern to break. Commit the build, THEN break and restore; a `git status` between guards is what catches it. **A cached fixture is shared mutable state**: `spec/helpers/replay.lua` memoises transcripts, so a test that edits one must copy it first (§7).

**Where things live:** the fork at `c:\Code\qe-live-fork`; transcripts under `spec/fixtures/captures/`; expected outputs under `spec/fixtures/expected/`; every gate locally via `.\tools\check.ps1`; the memory copy of this file that Claude Code sessions carry must be regenerated from this file whenever it changes.

## 1. What Lootpath is

**One sentence:** Lootpath puts QE Live's gear answers inside World of Warcraft, next to the loot they are about.

**The three promises, on one in-game frame:**
1. **Equip Now** - what you should be wearing right now, from what you own, with one-click equip.
2. **Upgrade Map** - for each gear slot, which dungeon boss or raid boss drops a candidate, and (where QE Live has ranked it) how much better it is.
3. **Vault** - which Great Vault option to take this week.

**The product rule, above every other rule:** **Lootpath never computes a healer value.** Every healing number on screen is QE Live's, transported unchanged from its export. Lootpath's own work is the scanner (gear, bags, bank, vault), the loot map (Encounter Journal walk), matching, and display. No network, no backend, no behaviour in combat, no estimate of its own to fill a gap.

**Who it is for, in order:** the owner (MVP = the owner alone, on one character); then healers generally; DPS via Raidbots/MythicSim output is a later extension of the same import interface, not an MVP goal.

**Why this exists:** the audience's whole gearing loop today is alt-tab: `/simc` in game, paste into QE Live in a browser, read the answer there, alt-tab back and try to remember it at the vault or when a drop appears. QE Live is right about the math; everything around the math happens where the game cannot see it. Mr. Mythical solves this in-game for DPS with a SimulationCraft-trained model; SimulationCraft cannot model healing, so no healer equivalent could be built that way. Lootpath is "Mr. Mythical for healers" with QE Live as the brain.

- Owner: wkernan. Senior engineer (TS/Node, Python, AWS); **no WoW healing expertise** - every mechanics claim traces to a source, never intuition.
- Repo: https://github.com/wkernan/lootpath - public, **MIT**, created 2026-09-05. Local clone `c:\Code\lootpath`, owner's alone (not a shared tree).
- Linear project **Lootpath** (team Wkernan): https://linear.app/wkernan/project/lootpath-fb6687ed82e3/overview. Labels: `ai-ready` (an outside Claude Code session can build it) and `human-required` (the owner must act, usually in game).
- Game client: Retail, Midnight expansion, patch 12.1, at `C:\World of Warcraft\_retail_`. Test character **hotornot**, Restoration Druid. Interface number **120100**, build **69587** (12.1.0), **measured**: `GetBuildInfo()` captured on the live client 2026-09-01 by the Healper spike (`HealperSpike.lua` SavedVariables, `environment.build` = `"12.1.0", "69587", "Aug 27 2026", 120100`), and `C:\World of Warcraft\.build.info` reads `12.1.0.69587` (read 2026-09-05). The earlier "build 69465" was the 08-24 build. M0-2's `capture env` re-measures; the `.toc` follows the client.

Inventory and vault data are spec-independent; the QE Live export (WKE-519) and the journal capture (WKE-523) must be run in Restoration (105), because QE Live values a spec and `EJ_SetLootFilter` takes one.
- Sister project: Healper (`c:\Code\Healper`), a log-analysis web app, parked 2026-09-02 and kept alive cheaply. **Not a dependency.** What transferred: the addon spike's secret-value serializer pattern, `sync.ps1`, `FINDINGS.md`/`README.md` client facts, and the working discipline (evidence rules, prove-red guards, premise checks - 48 of 71 Healper issues had a wrong premise, including ones written the same day by the same author).

## 3. The ecosystem, stated precisely (premise-checked 2026-09-03/05)

| Tool | What it does | Relation to Lootpath |
|---|---|---|
| **QE Live** (questionablyepic.com/live, Voulk) | Healer gearing brain: Top Gear (best set from what you own + vault), Upgrade Finder (values drops you do not own, by boss), Vault, trinket analysis. Web only. | **The brain.** Lootpath is the in-game reader of its output. |
| **Mr. Mythical** (addon) | In-game equip/loot/vault/crest recommendations from a neural-net DPS model. `/mrdps`. | The shape Lootpath copies, for healers. DPS only; cannot do healers. Open-source reference for journal walking and loadout ranking (respect its licence; mirror patterns, copy nothing). |
| **Pawn** | Same-slot item comparison in tooltips from imported stat weights (QE Live and WoWAnalyzer export weights for it). | Already owns tooltip comparison. **Lootpath does not do tooltips.** |
| **GearingWise, GearWish** | Tooltip upgrade advisor; loot wishlist. | More reasons tooltips are not a gap. |
| **Healer Stat Weights** | Computed healer weights live in-game; 234K downloads; appears unmaintained. | Proof the in-game healer audience exists. |
| **Raidbots / MythicSim** | DPS sims; Droptimizer output consumed by addons. | The DPS import path later; not MVP. |
| **WowCoach.gg** | AI log coaching, $5.99-12.99/mo, no visible traction. | The rejected product shape. AI is never the pitch. |

**The gap, exactly:** not "stat comparison for healers" (Pawn), not "loot source lists" (the Adventure Guide), but **QE Live's verdicts shown in-game next to the loot map and the vault.** That combination exists nowhere.

**Economics:** side project that pays for itself at most. Free addon, tip jar later. Comparable free-plus-Patreon tools: QE Live, Mr. Mythical, wowaudit (patron count NOT public; the "3,400" figure from an early draft was unverified and is withdrawn).

---

## 4. QE Live: everything we know (all read from its public source, branch `dev`)

- Repo: github.com/Voulk/QuestionablyEpic. **No licence file** = all rights reserved. Actively pushed (2026-09-02). We read it for understanding and schemas; we copy no code and no data tables.
- **Top Gear exports.** `src/General/Modules/TopGear/Report/TopGearJSONExport.ts`, present since at least 2026-05-20 (last touched by "handle duplicates"). Schema string `"qe-live-droptimizer"`, `version: 1`. Payload:
  ```
  { schema, version, exportedAt (ISO), player: {name, realm, region, spec, gameType: "Retail"|"Classic"},
    contentType, reportId,
    topSet: { score (hardScore), stats (setStats), items: [Item] },
    differentials: [ { scorePercent, hpsDifference, items: [Item], gems: [int] } ] }
  Item = { slot, id, level, bonusIDs: [int], gems: [int], enchant: string, tertiary: string, setId: int, isVault: bool, isExclusive: bool, source: {} }
  ```
  Sign conventions: `scoreDifference` (exported as `scorePercent`) is a percent where **positive means the alternative is worse**; `rawDifference` (exported as `hpsDifference`) is a raw score delta where **negative means the alternative is worse**. Stated in the exporter's comment *and* confirmed by the arithmetic (§9, 2026-09-06; **corrected 2026-09-08 - the Retail path is `TopGearEngine.ts`, not `TopGearEngineShared.js`, and it DOES scale `rawDifference` by the player's HPS**). The SIGNS are unchanged by that correction. Pinned as named constants in `QEImport` and again in its spec.
  `topSet.items` are the chosen items of the best set (falls back to the full list if none flagged). `differentials` = alternative sets: only the items that differ from the top set, with the delta.
  It is a **file download** (`qe-droptimizer-<name>-<id>.json`), not a clipboard copy. **Nothing on GitHub consumes this schema** (code search 2026-09-04).
  **Refuted 2026-09-06 (WKE-527): there is no Download JSON control.** `downloadTopGearJSON` has no caller anywhere in `src`, in any of his 20 branches (`git grep` across `upstream/*`); the exporter has one commit and is dead code. The live site cannot produce the file today. The owner's fork branch `lootpath/upgrade-finder-export` wires it (Export menu: Download JSON, Copy JSON). Until that lands upstream, the owner gets an export by running the fork locally (`npm start` in `c:\Code\qe-live-fork`, import `/simc`, Top Gear, Export > Download JSON); the engine is the same code as the site. **Done once, 2026-09-06:** the control produced a well-formed `version: 1` file on the first try (§9).
  **Upgrade Finder report shape (read 2026-09-06):** the report page holds the *shortened* object `shortenReport` stores via `addUpgradeReport.php`: `{ id, playername, realm, region, spec, contentType, gameType, ufSettings, equippedItems: Item[], results[] }` where each `results` entry is `processItem`'s `{ item (id), level, dropLoc, dropType, dropDifficulty, score, rawDiff, percDiff }` plus `source {instanceId, encounterId}` and `slot` added by the page. **`percDiff` and `rawDiff` are `new - base`: positive means an upgrade**, the opposite sign to the Top Gear differentials. The fork's `qe-live-upgradefinder` v1 export names them `upgradePercent` / `hpsGain` (see `docs/qe-live-pr.md`).
  **His checks:** `.eslintignore` ends with a bare `src/`, so his ESLint config lints nothing in the source tree and has no TypeScript parser; `tsc --noEmit` on `dev` reports 205 errors; `react-scripts test` is the runnable check.
- **`dropLoc` is four words, and two of them are not a boss (read off the eight committed exports 2026-09-13/14, R-4/WKE-565; the rows are `ns.Roads`' craft and delve roads by slot and the by-run view's two cards).** `Dungeon` and `Raid` are the drops the Encounter Journal walk can find; `Delves` and `Crafted` are the two the walk cannot, and every committed export carries **33 delve rows and 18 crafted ones** beside its drops - the same 51 rows, at the same item levels, in all eight. On both, `dropType` is null and `dropDifficulty` is the empty string, so neither the drop/max/bonus tie-break nor a difficulty means anything for them. Their `source` is a sentinel rather than an instance: `{ instanceId: -98, encounterId: -98 }` on every delve row, `{ instanceId: -4, encounterId: <n> }` on a crafted one, where `n` is an index into his profession table (1, 2, 3, 4, 5 and 7 appear) and is carried, never turned into a profession name. Delve rows arrive at **321** and crafted rows at **331**; `settings.craftedStats` (`Crit / Haste`) is the stats line those crafted rows assume and **`settings.craftedLevel` is an INDEX, not an item level** - it is 2 in every export while the rows it produced are 331, the same trap `settings.dungeon` is. Measured: the five key-level dungeon documents agree about all 51 rows to the last digit, and the Raid document differs on 33 of them, so **what moves these numbers is the content type, not the Mythic+ key**.

- **Upgrade Finder does NOT export.** `UpgradeFinderReport.js` has no export/copy/download path; `UpgradeItemData.js` is marked "Currently unimplemented". The in-memory result (`runUpgradeFinder`, `UpgradeFinderEngine.js` ~line 105) is `UpgradeFinderResult { itemSet, differentials, contentType, id }` where each item possibility carries `source[0].instanceId` / `encounterId` (used by `getSetItemLevel`) and differentials carry score deltas. An export mirroring Top Gear's is roughly 50 lines - in his repo.
- **Vault reaches QE Live via the SimulationCraft addon**: `simc-addon/core.lua` ~line 1294 reads `C_WeeklyRewards:HasAvailableRewards()`, `GetActivities()`, `GetItemHyperlink(rewardInfo.itemDBID)` and exports the options. QE Live marks them `vaultItem`/`isVault`.
- **Relationship history:** Voulk reviewed Healper's advice layer harshly and correctly (2026-09-01, Discord). Lootpath must read as the opposite: computes nothing, defers to his engine, drives users to his site. Outreach is the owner's (QE-2), after there is a working addon to show, leading with "your export has no consumer; here is one, plus two buttons" - a copy-to-clipboard control and the Upgrade Finder export, offered as a PR from the owner's fork. Never mention Healper unless he does.
