## 5. Data flow (the round-trip)

1. In game: SimulationCraft addon `/simc` (includes equipped, bags, and vault when available) - copy.
2. Browser: run **Top Gear** (M+ by default), Export > **Download JSON**. **On the companion's path this is driven headlessly, and since C-5 the two upgrade checkboxes are set explicitly before Submit** (both off by default), so a vault option and the gear it would replace are valued at the same point on their upgrade tracks; the pair is recorded in `Data/QEVerdict.lua` as `qeSettings`. **Until the fork's PR lands upstream that control exists only on the fork**, so the owner runs it locally (`npm start` in `c:\Code\qe-live-fork`) rather than on questionablyepic.com/live.
3. In game: `/lootpath`, paste the JSON text into the editbox. **The box takes either export** (M3-6): `UI.ImportAny` reads the `schema` string out of the text and hands it to `QEImport` (Top Gear) or `UFImport` (Upgrade Finder); each validates schema, version 1 and Retail for itself and stores its verdict in SavedVariables with its `exportedAt`. A third schema is refused by name, naming both the ones Lootpath reads.
4. `Inventory` scans; `Journal` walks (cached); `Vault` reads; `Match` joins the Top Gear verdict to the inventory by item key, and the Upgrade Map joins the Upgrade Finder verdict to the journal by itemID + item level.
5. Three panels render. Equip Now can equip out of combat.

Constraints that shape it: addons have **no network** and **cannot read files** (hence paste); SavedVariables flush **only on `/reload` or logout**; secret values apply in combat/encounters/M+/PvP (spike-verified they lift out of combat; the guard stays on every read regardless); the bank is readable only while the bank frame is open.

**Since C-6 (WKE-540) step 2 is not one Top Gear run but one per named scenario, and each is its own IMPORT.** QE Live's three import checkboxes act at import (`runSimC` is handed their state at Submit), so asking "what is this vault option now" and "what is it through the Catalyst" means pasting the same profile again with different boxes. The four are fixed and named: `asOffered` (all off), `catalyzed` (catalyze on), **`thisWeek` (vault upgrade + catalyze, ALL off - M3-13)**, `maxed` (all three on). Top Gear only; the Upgrade Finder is not a scenario question and rides in whichever pass already asks for the configured `qeAutoUpgradeVault`/`qeAutoUpgradeAll` pair with catalyze off - the `asOffered` pass under the default, an import of its own otherwise. **Since C-12 (WKE-577) each what-if is asked when ITS OWN question has an answer**, not when the vault has gear: `catalyzed` when QE Live made a Catalyst clone out of what the character holds, `maxed` when something came back above the level the `asOffered` import valued it at, `thisWeek` when either. The gate is settled inside the run, after that pass's import, off the pool QE Live built - his `Item.canBeCatalyzed` and his `CONSTANTS.itemLevelCaps` are what decide, and Lootpath restates neither - so a skipped pass costs its Top Gear documents and not its import. A vault section with gear in it, and `--force`, waive the gate outright; the gate fails open when there is no pool to read. Each Top Gear document records its `scenario` and the three boxes that produced it; the addon files verdicts under `(content type, scenario)` and **Equip Now and the Upgrade Map read `asOffered` and nothing else**.

**Since C-7 (WKE-543) step 2 is not one Upgrade Finder run but one per Mythic+ key level.** His Upgrade Finder values every dungeon drop at the single key `ufSettings.dungeon` names, so the dungeon Upgrade Finder is run once per level in `upgradeFinderKeyLevels` (default `+2, +4, +6, +8, +10`) and each document records `keyLevel`; the addon files an Upgrade Finder verdict under `(contentType, keyLevel)` and the Upgrade Map reads the one matching the level its walk previewed.

**Since C-11 (WKE-572) step 2 is not one Top Gear run per scenario but a SEQUENCE of runs inside each one.** A non-patron's Top Gear answers a question about thirty items (`TopGear.tsx:177`) and the owner's page draws 63 cards, so one pass leaves the rest unrated: on the 2026-09-14 11:42 run, 33 cards left out of every Catalyst pass, 22 of them trinket rows, every one of them reading `not rated · beyond the rating's item limit` in game. Each later pass keeps the cards QE Live made ACTIVE at import - the equipped set, the vault options and their clones, his own `SimCImportEngine.ts:712` and `Item.ts:174` - deselects the bag items the previous pass clicked, and spends the room on cards no pass has asked about yet. **Every pass is its own document over its own pool**, carrying `pass` and the `considered` list beside C-8's `excluded`; nothing is merged and no two numbers are combined. Pass 1 is the plan and nothing else is: an item's RATING comes from the pass that considered it, while the best set, the plan sentence, Equip Now and the Upgrade Map read pass 1 alone. The bound is `topGearPasses` (default 4) and is in the fingerprint.

**The companion path (decision 2026-09-07; S-1/S-2 spikes, C-1/C-2 build it):** the same round-trip with no human in the middle. (1) **`/lootpath refresh` takes the `env`, `inventory` and `vault` captures, in that order, and then reloads**, so what reaches disk is the gear the character is wearing now rather than whatever was last captured by hand (C-3, WKE-536; before it refresh only reloaded and the companion valued the newest stored snapshot, which on 2026-09-07 was two days and one spec old). A plain `/reload` still flushes, and still flushes only what was already captured. (2) A Node companion on the owner's PC (`tools/companion/`) watches that file, builds the SimC text profile from Lootpath's own records (mirroring the SimC addon's Unlicense exporter), drives the owner's local fork at localhost:3000 headless to run Top Gear and Upgrade Finder for Dungeon and Raid, and writes the four JSON documents as Lua strings into `Interface\AddOns\Lootpath\Data\QEVerdict.lua` (temp file + rename). (3) `/reload` again: the addon loads that file - it is in the `.toc`, a committed placeholder ships with the addon - and runs each document through the same `QEImport.Parse` as a paste, pins and refusals included. The paste box stays as the manual path. **Both halves are built.** C-1 (WKE-533, PR #27) is `tools/companion/`: it finds the SavedVariables by glob under `WTF\Account\*` (the account name is never written into the repo and never printed), reads them with S-2's reader and builds the profile with S-2's builder (both lifted into `tools/companion/lib/`, so there is one copy of each and not two), drives the fork through Playwright with a persistent browser profile, and writes the chunk temp-file-then-rename. **The addon half (C-2, WKE-534, PR #25):** `ns.Companion` at load, `/lootpath refresh` for both reloads, the source named on screen, and a repo sync that no longer overwrites the companion's file. The file shape it reads is §6's contract, and it is what C-1 must write. This is the Raider.IO pattern: their desktop client writes score databases into the addon folder the same way. Floor: two reloads, zero alt-tabs; nothing in the client can go lower, because SavedVariables reach disk only on reload and addon files load only on reload. **The second reload is free (C-4, WKE-537, PR #32):** every `/reload` writes SavedVariables and the watcher fires on every write, so step (3) used to start a QE Live run of its own over the same gear - 17.7 s and four byte-identical documents, measured on the owner's machine 2026-09-07 21:07. The companion now fingerprints the profile it is about to ask QE Live about (SHA-256 over the profile text with its four volatile header lines removed), remembers in `.state/last-profile.json` the fingerprint of the last verdict it actually wrote, and skips the run when the fingerprint agrees, the remembered path is the file this run would write, and that file is still on disk; it logs one line instead. `--force` runs regardless. The debounce (1500 ms) is untouched and was never the answer: two reloads twenty seconds apart really are two writes and the second must be READ, because the owner may have opened the bank and captured between them - it is the profile, not the write, that decides. **The loop now says what it is doing (C-9, WKE-559):** the companion writes `Data/companion.log` (every line it prints, rotated at 200 KB) and `Data/CompanionStatus.lua` (`state`, `stage`, the stamps, the exit code) beside the verdict at every stage change, the addon loads that second chunk from its `.toc` like any other file, and the window's status strip carries one clause of it - so step (3) can tell a run that died from a run that had nothing to do, which from inside the game it could not (§11, 2026-09-09). A logon Task Scheduler task (`tools/companion/install-startup.ps1`) starts the fork and the watcher, and a lock file makes a second watcher exit 7 rather than race the first.

**Since R-7a (WKE-582) step 1's own occasion is named for what it is: the flush.** `PLAYER_LOGOUT` fires when the interface is unloaded, which a `/reload` does as surely as a logout, and nothing at that moment can tell the two apart - only the next load can (`PLAYER_ENTERING_WORLD` carries `isInitialLogin, isReloadingUi`), and by then the snapshot is written. So the sequence runs on both, which is the reason the second reload of the loop carries the gear the player is in rather than the gear of two steps ago, and everything it stores says `flush`. The companion's line follows: `run after a logout or reload (gear captured at the flush)`. **And no capture list grows without limit any more:** `storeSnapshot` keeps the newest `ns.CAPTURE_HISTORY` (4) per name, because the loop writes four snapshots per reload and two reloads per refresh and nothing ever removed one (§7).

**Since R-7 (WKE-579) step 1 also happens on its own, at the flush.** `PLAYER_LOGOUT` runs the same four captures `/lootpath refresh` runs - `env`, `inventory`, `vault`, `currencies` - with no reload, nothing asynchronous and no vault interaction, so the SavedVariables the client flushes on the way out carry the gear the player logged out in. The companion wakes on that write, rates it while the player is away, and the next login loads a current plan. The floor has not moved and the two reloads are unchanged; what changed is that the loop no longer needs a refresh before a logout to be true, and `/lootpath refresh` is for catching up mid-session. A forced logout in combat captures nothing, because nothing in Lootpath runs in combat.

**Since M3-16a (WKE-581) the vault's question is asked at login, not inside step 1, and a reload that cannot be immediate is a click.** M3-16 put `OnUIInteract` where its answer was wanted - inside the `vault` capture, which the refresh then had to wait for - and that wait is what broke the reload: the client refuses a `ReloadUI` it cannot attribute to the player's own hardware event, and one called from an event continuation is not that (§7, §11). So the first `PLAYER_ENTERING_WORLD` of a session asks the same question - the same `OnUIInteract`, the same bounded wait for `WEEKLY_REWARDS_UPDATE`, the same `CloseInteraction` on every path out - with **no capture and no reload**, and simply leaves the client holding its own data. By the time the player types `/lootpath refresh` the activities carry their rewards, the vault capture has nothing to ask for, all four captures finish inside the slash command's own execution, and `ReloadUI` is the player's action exactly as it was before M3-16. When the login ask failed, timed out or never ran, the chain still captures and still waits, but it **never calls `ReloadUI`**: it shows `Gear captured. Reload to send it?` with Reload and Not now, and the click on Reload is the hardware event. R-6's `refreshStartedAt` stamp is written on that path too, so the strip's wait line stands with the same reload behind its click and a player who chose Not now is not stranded. The login ask is never in combat; a login that lands in a fight asks at `PLAYER_REGEN_ENABLED`.

**Since M3-16b (WKE-583) the vault's question is asked properly and is known not to be enough.** Three changes to step 1 and one to step 2, all off the owner's reset day (2026-09-15, §9). (a) The `vault` capture reads on EVERY `WEEKLY_REWARDS_UPDATE` inside the bound and settles when a read carries rewards or the bound ends, the way Blizzard's own frame re-reads while it is shown; `interact.updates` records each update's stamp and what it carried, and `CloseInteraction` still runs once at the end. (b) The flush no longer captures the vault at all - `Companion.FLUSH_CAPTURES` is `env`, `inventory`, `currencies` - because a read that cannot ask can only shadow one that did, which is what the owner's 19:09:31Z flush did to his 19:09:29Z refresh. (c) The companion CHOOSES its vault snapshot instead of taking the newest: of the reads for the same reward period, the newest that carries reward links, and among those the newest that asked; the log says which. And the finding the three sit under: **on reset day the client has not been given the week's rewards at all, and `OnUIInteract` does not fetch them** - the Great Vault window is what generates them, so the Vault tab says `open the Great Vault, then refresh` rather than sending the player round the loop (§7).

**Since R-6 (WKE-578) the player is told when step 1 is due, and told again while step 5 is happening.** The round trip above is unchanged; what changed is that nothing in it has to be remembered. `ns.Drift` keeps the key set the plan was written over, re-reads it on `BAG_UPDATE_DELAYED`, `PLAYER_EQUIPMENT_CHANGED`, `WEEKLY_REWARDS_UPDATE` and `ITEM_UPGRADE_MASTER_UPDATE` (debounced 0.5 s, never in combat), and the window says `your gear changed since this plan (n items) · click to refresh`. After the reload, `db.global.drift.refreshStartedAt` - written immediately before `ReloadUI`, which is what puts it on disk - keeps the row up as `rating your gear, started …, usually ready in about … · click to load it` until a plan newer than that stamp loads, C-9 reports a FAILED run, or ten minutes pass.

---

## 6. Architecture

One shipped package `Lootpath/` (what the packager zips) plus `spec/` (tests) and `tools/` (dev scripts). One namespace table `ns` shared via `.toc` load order; modules register on `ns`; no globals except the slash handler.

| Module | Responsibility | Interface (contract) | Testable headless? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Core.lua` (shipped M0-1) | lifecycle, events, `/lootpath` dispatcher (`capture <name>`, `capture`, `capture wipe`, `status`, `refresh`, `spike` (R-0, temporary), `help`), AceDB SavedVariables, `ns.Safe(value)` secret guard, `ns.CopyRaw(table)` guarded deep copy (secret, cycle, depth 10, 50k nodes, UI objects), `ns.Probe(fn, ...)` pcall-to-positional-table, `ns.ItemKey(id, bonusIDs)`, `ns.BonusIDsFromKey(key)`, `ns.EpochFromISO(iso, now)`, `ns.RegisterCapture/RunCapture`; **since M3-16a the login half of the vault question** | `ItemKey` = `"<itemID>:<sorted bonusIDs joined by :>"`, bare `"<itemID>"` with none, `nil` for garbage; ONE definition used by QEImport and Match, with `BonusIDsFromKey` (C-6) as its inverse beside it so no caller has to know the format carries colons. `RunCapture(name, onComplete, args)` refuses in combat (`{ok=false, reason="combat"}`), before the DB exists, for unknown names, and while another capture is running. A capture registered `{ async = true }` is handed `finish(data, failure)` instead of returning data, answers `{ok=true, pending=true}` at once, and is abandoned after `ns.CAPTURE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (180) if it never calls back; `finish` counts only once (M3-1). **`storeSnapshot` keeps the newest `ns.CAPTURE_HISTORY` (4) snapshots per capture name since R-7a (WKE-582)** and drops the oldest past it, `table.remove(list, 1)` on the list AceDB already holds rather than a replacement put in its place. Before it nothing trimmed anything: the owner's SavedVariables went 6.1 -> 7.1 -> 11.0 MB across two days, 35 `inventory` snapshots worth 5.85 MB of it, and the client reads and writes the whole file at every login and every reload. Four is the refresh loop's two flushes plus two to compare against; the companion reads only the newest of each name, and a pull kept as evidence is committed under `spec/fixtures/captures/` rather than left in the live file. `ns.captureTrigger` is `"refresh"`, `"flush"` or nil (stored as `"command"`) - **`"logout"` was retired by R-7a, because the event it named fires on a `/reload` too**. `EpochFromISO` (C-2) is the ONE reader of an ISO 8601 UTC stamp - QE Live's `exportedAt` and the companion's `writtenAt` - so an age on screen and a freshness decision can never disagree about what a timestamp means; `UI.AgeSeconds` is a subtraction on top of it. **M3-16a (WKE-581) adds one lifecycle event and nothing else to this file:** `PLAYER_ENTERING_WORLD` (registered beside `PLAYER_LOGOUT`, unregistered inside the first handler, so it is once per session and not once per loading screen) calls `ns.Companion.AskVaultAtLogin` inside a `pcall`, the way the logout's capture is called, and registers `PLAYER_REGEN_ENABLED` only when that answered nil - which it does for exactly one reason, a login that landed in combat. A question that threw is not retried. What is asked and why lives in `Modules/Companion.lua`; this file owns only the lifecycle half. **R-7a (WKE-582) renames what `PLAYER_LOGOUT` calls to `ns.Companion.CaptureAtFlush` and says in the comment what the event is:** an unload, which a `/reload` performs as surely as a logout, and which only the NEXT load could tell apart (`PLAYER_ENTERING_WORLD` carries `isInitialLogin, isReloadingUi`) - by which time the snapshot is written. The sequence therefore runs on both on purpose | yes (`spec/core_spec.lua`) |
| `Captures.lua` (shipped M0-1; `currencies` added M3-9, its by-ID probe M3-11; the keystone and bag-frame reads R-0) | `/lootpath capture env`, `inventory`, `vault`, `currencies` raw dumps | snapshot `{ name, capturedAt, capturedAtLocal, build, addonVersion, sawSecret, durationMs, data }` appended to `db.global.captures[name]`. Reads only; every client function called is named in the file (bank predicates are an allow-list) - never a namespace walk, because `C_Bank` also moves items and money. **`currencies` (M3-9, WKE-544)** walks `C_CurrencyInfo.GetCurrencyListSize()` and `GetCurrencyListInfo(i)` over every index - **headers kept**, because the client's own grouping is the evidence for which entries are the season's upgrade currencies - and adds `GetCurrencyInfo(currencyID)` for every non-header entry. All three are in Ketho's `CurrencyInfoDocumentation.lua` (`CurrencyInfo`: `name`, `description`, `currencyID`, `isHeader`, `quantity`, `maxQuantity`, `quantityEarnedThisWeek`, `iconFileID`, ...). Guard before coerce: a secret `CurrencyInfo` comes back from `ns.Safe` as a marker string and is recorded rather than indexed, and it produces no second call. **M3-11 (WKE-546) adds the by-ID half**, because the list walk is the currency TAB and the tab shows only the rows of EXPANDED headers: the capture also calls `GetCurrencyInfo(id)` for every ID in `ns.Currencies.KNOWN_IDS` (read at capture time - `Modules/` loads after this file) and stores the packs under `data.byID[id]`, with `data.probedIDs` naming what it asked. `isHeaderExpanded` keeps being recorded; it is the field that proved the list is a scroll state. `ExpandCurrencyList` would make the hidden rows appear and is **not** called. **R-0 (WKE-561) adds two blocks to `env`, both recorded raw and shown nowhere:** `data.keystone` - `C_MythicPlus.GetOwnedKeystoneLevel()` / `GetOwnedKeystoneChallengeMapID()` / `GetOwnedKeystoneMapID()` (MythicPlusInfoDocumentation.lua:38/34/42) and `C_ChallengeMode.GetMapUIInfo(challengeMapID)` (ChallengeModeInfoDocumentation.lua:81) for the name, asked with the CHALLENGE map ID and not the other one - and `data.bagAddons`, `IsAddOnLoaded` for Baganator, Syndicator, ElvUI, Pawn, `Blizzard_UIPanels_Game` (where Blizzard's container frames live; there is no `Blizzard_Bags`) and `Blizzard_BankUI`, plus the `type()` of six bag globals read through `rawget`, so nothing indexes or calls another addon. **M3-16 (WKE-557) makes `vault` the second capture that is not purely a read** and the only one that asks the server a question: when `HasAvailableRewards()` is true and not one activity carries a reward - the state measured in §9 - it calls `C_WeeklyRewards.OnUIInteract()`, waits up to `ns.VAULT_INTERACT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (5) for `WEEKLY_REWARDS_UPDATE`, reads `GetActivities()` and the links again, and calls `CloseInteraction()` on every path out, timeout included. It is therefore `{ async = true }`. The snapshot keeps both reads and merges nothing: the top-level `activities` / `rewardLinks` / `exampleLinks` are the before read, `data.interact.after` holds the after one, and `data.interact` also carries `attempted`, `reason`, `updateFired`, `timedOut`, `waitedMs` and the raw probes of both new calls. Two new reads ride along whether or not the interaction happens - `AreRewardsForCurrentRewardPeriod()` and `HasGeneratedRewards()` (WeeklyRewardsDocumentation.lua:6 and :84), the pair Blizzard's own `UpdatePreviousClaim` reads. `data.functionNames` is the file's own list of the nine `C_WeeklyRewards` functions the capture may call; `ClaimReward` and `SelectReward` are not on it and a test asserts so. **M3-16a (WKE-581) lifts the interaction itself out into `ns.VaultInteract(record, onFired, onDone)`** - `OnUIInteract`, the listener, the bound, and `CloseInteraction` on every path out - so the capture and `ns.Companion.AskVaultAtLogin` run one copy of the exception rather than two that could drift; `record` is filled in place and `onFired` is where the capture reads its lists a second time, while the interaction is still open. `env` also carries `data.vaultLoginAsk`, the login ask's own record copied through `ns.CopyRaw` (`askedAtLogin`, `reason`, `updateFired`, `timedOut`, `waitedMs`), because that ask captures nothing itself and this is the only place it leaves a transcript; `{ absent = true }` before any login event has run. **M3-17 (WKE-574) adds `upgrade`, the third capture that is not purely a read** (`/lootpath capture upgrade`, `{ async = true }`): the client answers `C_ItemUpgrade.GetItemUpgradeItemInfo()` only for the item currently in the open upgrade vendor's window, so for every equipped slot and bag slot whose item `CanUpgradeItem(location)` accepts it calls `SetItemUpgradeFromLocation(location)`, waits up to `ns.UPGRADE_SET_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (2) for `ITEM_UPGRADE_MASTER_SET_ITEM`, reads `GetItemUpgradeItemInfo()`, `GetItemUpgradeCurrentLevel()`, `GetHighWatermarkForItem(link)` and `GetItemHyperlink()`, and calls `ClearItemUpgrade()` - after every item and once more at the end, on every path out including a client that errors on one item. It refuses when `ItemUpgradeFrame` is not shown (`open the crest vendor first`) and, like every capture, in combat. Each item's record is FLAT - `key`, `itemID`, `link`, `source`/`invSlot`/`bag`/`slotIndex`, `canUpgrade`, `set`, `eventFired`, `timedOut`, `waitedMs`, then the four probes - because `ns.CopyRaw`'s depth 10 truncates a cost's `discountInfo` if anything sits between the record and the probes (§9). `data.itemInWindowAtStart` records what the owner had in the window; the capture clears it and does NOT put it back. `data.functionNames` lists the seven `C_ItemUpgrade` functions it may call; **`UpgradeItem`, `SetItemUpgradeFromCursorItem` and `CloseItemUpgrade` are not on it, are never called, and a test reads this file's own source and asserts it contains none of them** | yes (`spec/captures_spec.lua`, over the stub; the `currencies` half is Blizzard-documented placeholders until the owner's transcript lands) |

| `Modules/Inventory.lua` (shipped M1-1) | equipped + owned bags 0..5 + bank tabs (only while `C_Bank.CanViewBank` says so) -> records | `ns.Inventory:Scan()` -> `{ ok = true, records, bankAvailable, secretsSeen }` or `{ ok = false, reason = "combat" }`; record `{ key, itemID, link, itemLevel, bonusIDs (sorted), slot, equipLoc, location = equipped/bag/bank, bag, slotIndex, name, quality, icon }`. **`slot` uses QE Live's vocabulary** (`Head`, `Neck`, `Shoulder`, `Back`, `Chest`, `Wrist`, `Hands`, `Waist`, `Legs`, `Feet`, `Finger`, `Trinket`, `1H Weapon`, `2H Weapon`, `Offhand`, `Shield`, read from `TopGearExports.ts`), not the issue's `HEAD` style, because Match groups per slot against the export. Non-gear (consumables, bags, shirts, profession tools, keystones) yields no record. `itemLevel` = `C_Item.GetDetailedItemLevelInfo(link)[1]`; quality falls back to `ContainerItemInfo.quality` when `GetItemInfo` is uncached. **`icon` (M5-1) is the FIFTH return of `GetItemInfoInstant`** - static data, so it answers for an item whose full data has not arrived - with `ContainerItemInfo.iconFileID` as the fallback, exactly as quality has one. Every read passes `ns.Safe`; a secret is dropped and counted. `ns.ParseItemLink` (Core) is the one link parser | yes: `spec/inventory_spec.lua` replays the transcript through the stub and compares to golden fixtures under `spec/fixtures/expected/` |
| `Modules/Journal.lua` (M3-1, both PRs shipped) | `ns.JournalAdapter` (every EJ call, raw, secret-guarded, plus the async two-stage walk and `capture journal`) + `ns.Journal` (pure aggregator) | Every adapter entry point hands back `ns.Probe` packs unchanged (`{n=..., ...}`, `{absent=true}`, `{error=...}`) with each value through `ns.CopyRaw`; secrets are masked and counted in `Adapter.secretsSeen`. `Adapter.FUNCTION_NAMES` is the literal list of every client function the file calls (28 as of PR 2), and `Availability()` reports which of them this client lacks. `Walk(opts, onDone)` selects instance / difficulty / loot filter / preview level per target, waits for the loot LIST (`LOOT_MAX_ATTEMPTS` x `LOOT_WAIT_SECONDS`, 8 x 0.25s), reads it, and **then reads it again** if any row's ITEM data is missing (`ITEM_DATA_MAX_ATTEMPTS` x `ITEM_DATA_WAIT_SECONDS`, 8 x 0.25s) - `record.loot` is the first read, `record.reread` the second, both kept so a capture shows what the second filled. `Adapter.RowIsPending(row)` is Blizzard's own gate (`itemInfo.name` missing = RETRIEVING_ITEM_INFO); `Adapter.EncounterNames(read)` resolves each row's boss with `EJ_GetEncounterInfo`, as Blizzard's loot button does. **`ns.Journal:Build(opts)` -> `sources, summary, fromCache`**, `sources` being the contract map `{ [itemID] = { {instanceID, instanceName, encounterID, encounterName, difficultyID, itemLevel, slot, isRaid, pending, itemKey, name} ... } }` (`itemKey` and `name` added by M3-3; `summary.previewMythicPlusLevel` with them), cached in `db.global.journalCache` keyed `(build, seasonID, specID, difficultyIDs)` - e.g. `69587|18|105|2:8:15:16:23` - and every entry from another build dropped on the way in. `slot` is QE Live's vocabulary, taken from the row's `GetItemInfoInstant` equipLoc through **`ns.Inventory.SlotForEquipLoc`**, not from `EncounterJournalItemInfo.slot` (that field is the client's localised UI text). A row whose item data had not arrived is carried with `pending = true` and a nil `itemLevel` and `slot`: **`pending` is "not known yet", never "no item level"**. **`itemLevel` is not a field of `EncounterJournalItemInfo`** (Blizzard docs, 2026-09-05): it comes from `C_Item.GetDetailedItemLevelInfo` on the loot row's `link`, as the SimC addon does for vault links. Build is pure Lua over a walk result, so like `QEImport.Parse` it carries no combat guard; the walk in front of it does | aggregator yes, over the committed transcript (`spec/journal_spec.lua`: 613 rows -> 558 sources over 375 items, slot vocabulary, a raid item and a dungeon item, the cache key, the second read, plus a golden under `spec/fixtures/expected/journal-20260906-161213.lua`); adapter only via captures, with the stub proving the behaviour around the client - probing, missing functions, secret masking, both waits, both bounds, combat, restore |
| `Modules/Vault.lua` (shipped M3-3; pending rewards and the second read M3-12) | `C_WeeklyRewards` adapter: the week's options, their progress, and the item behind each generated reward - and, since M3-12, the request and the second read for a reward the client has not loaded yet | `ns.Vault.Options()` -> `{ ok = true, options, hasAvailableRewards, canClaimRewards, secondsUntilWeeklyReset, secretsSeen }` or `{ ok = false, reason = "combat"\|"no vault API" }`; option `{ type, typeLabel, index, id, threshold, progress, level, activityTierID, raidString, unlocked, rewards }` (`raidString` added M5-4: the client's own threshold sentence for a Raid row, recorded verbatim - `%d` and plural marker and all - because Blizzard's own vault frame prefers it over `WEEKLY_REWARDS_THRESHOLD_RAID` and the drawn grid has to say the same thing; nothing formats it here); reward `{ itemDBID, itemID, key, link, itemLevel, slot, equipLoc, name, quality, quantity, icon }` (`icon` added M5-1, from the same fifth return, so a pending row still draws its own icon). The same four calls the SimulationCraft addon makes, all named in `Vault.FUNCTION_NAMES`, never discovered by walking `C_WeeklyRewards` (it also carries `ClaimReward`). **Guard before coerce**: `tonumber()` of a secret is `nil`, so every field passes `ns.Safe` first and is coerced after, or the secret would be swallowed uncounted. A currency or quest reward (`Enum.CachedRewardType`) is skipped rather than shown as an item with no level; a reward whose link never arrives keeps its row and carries no key rather than a wrong one. **M3-12 (WKE-547): a reward whose link ARRIVES with an empty name is `pending`** - measured 2026-09-09 08:59, right after a client restart, on four of five rewards: `|h[]|h`, `GetItemInfo` and `GetDetailedItemLevelInfo` nil, `GetItemInfoInstant` still answering the slot, itemID / bonus IDs / key intact. `Vault.RewardIsPending` is Blizzard's own gate (`itemInfo.name` missing = RETRIEVING_ITEM_INFO); a pending record carries `name = nil`, `itemLevel = nil`, its slot when the client's static data gives one, and its key. `Options(opts)` stays synchronous and returns the pending records; unless `opts.request == false` it calls `Vault.RequestItemData` on each, which asks `C_Item.RequestLoadItemDataByID(itemID)` once per itemID per episode, registers `ITEM_DATA_LOAD_RESULT` and `GET_ITEM_INFO_RECEIVED` on the module's own frame, and arms a timer (`ITEM_DATA_WAIT_SECONDS` x `ITEM_DATA_MAX_ATTEMPTS`, 8 x 0.25 s, the journal's bound). `Vault.OnItemData` (out of combat only; a `success == false` load is forgotten, not guessed at) and the timer both run `Vault.ResolvePending`, which runs `Vault.Reread` - `GetItemHyperlink` again, then the same item reads as the first pass - on the SAME record objects the panel was handed, and asks for ONE redraw per batch through `ns.UI.RefreshVault` (`C_Timer.After(0)`-coalesced). A record the client never describes stays `pending` in words, is dropped from `Vault.pendingItems` when the bound runs out, and is asked for again on the next `Options()`. `pendingRewards` and `requestedItems` ride on the result. **M5-1 (WKE-550) moved the ASKING into `ns.ItemData`** and left every rule about what is pending here: `Vault.pendingItems`, `Vault.PendingCount`, `Vault.OnItemData`, `Vault.ResolvePending`, `Vault.Reread`, `Vault.Forget` and the coalesced `ns.UI.RefreshVault` are unchanged in behaviour, `ITEM_DATA_WAIT_SECONDS` / `ITEM_DATA_MAX_ATTEMPTS` / `ITEM_DATA_EVENTS` are now aliases of ItemData's, and `C_Item.RequestLoadItemDataByID` LEFT `Vault.FUNCTION_NAMES` with the call itself - a list that claims a call the file does not make is worth nothing. `FUNCTION_NAMES` now names the C_Item reads too (they were always called). **The activity shape is measured** (four snapshots, 2026-09-05 and 2026-09-06); **the reward shape is Blizzard's exported `WeeklyRewardActivityRewardInfo`, not measured** - `rewards` was empty in every committed snapshot bar the Concession currency - and WKE-523's after-reset capture is what confirms it | yes (`spec/vault_spec.lua`, three transcripts replayed through the stub: 09-06, 09-08 12:45, and the 09-09 08:59 fresh-login one, with the 09-08 23:26 snapshot of the same itemDBIDs replayed over it as the client's late answer) |
| `Modules/Currencies.lua` (shipped M3-9, read by ID in M3-11) | how many Catalyst charges and season crests the player has, for the two Vault-tab lines whose scenarios assumed them | `ns.Currencies.Read(opts)` -> `{ ok = true, source = "live"\|"capture", entries, byID, crests, crestsKnown, catalystKnown, catalystCharges, catalystMax, catalyst, secretsSeen }` or `{ ok = false, reason }`. The live client wins when it can be asked; in combat, or on a client with no `C_CurrencyInfo`, the newest stored `currencies` snapshot answers instead and `source` says so. `Currencies.FUNCTION_NAMES` lists the three calls, literally, because `C_CurrencyInfo` also carries the currency-transfer functions. **M3-11 (WKE-546) makes the currency ID the primary key**, because a by-name read of the list is a read of the currency TAB and the tab lists only the rows of expanded headers - which is how M3-9 concluded, from a transcript with 8 of 10 headers collapsed, that the Catalyst charge is not a currency. `Currencies.KNOWN_IDS = { crests = { 3442, 3443, 3444, 3445, 3446 }, catalyst = { 3465 } }`, every ID MEASURED (the crests from the 23:04 transcript's "Crests" header; 3465 from the owner's own Catalyst tooltip, "Venomblight Manaflux ... Total Maximum: 1/8, CurrencyID 3465") and none remembered from a wiki. `Read()` resolves each configured ID through the direct `GetCurrencyInfo` probe, then the list's info half, then - only when no ID answered - the list by name through `CREST_NAMES` / `CATALYST_NAMES`, which survive as that fallback and as the crest order; the name shown is always the client's own `CurrencyInfo.name`. `crestsKnown` / `catalystKnown` mean "an ID or a name is configured". `catalystCharges` and `catalystMax` are the client's `quantity` and `maxQuantity`, numbers or nil, and nil is never shown as 0. **`CATALYST_NOT_A_CURRENCY` is gone** - it was true of one night's scroll state and false about the client. **M5-4 (WKE-553) keeps the client's own `iconFileID`** on every record `Currencies.record` builds, and hands the whole Catalyst record on as `read.catalyst` beside the two numbers that were already there, because the Vault tab now draws each crest and the charge with its own icon and the client's own name. The issue's premise that M3-11 "already read" the icon was half true: the call was made, the field was dropped. Nothing is derived from an icon; it is a number the client handed over. **No arithmetic of any kind** - no cost, no total, no "enough for N upgrades"; an item's upgrade cost is not readable outside the upgrade UI and is not estimated | yes (`spec/currencies_spec.lua`; the `world.currencies` tests use labelled placeholders in Blizzard's documented `CurrencyInfo` shape, the fixture tests use the owner's real client. The one that carries the issue: a list whose headers are ALL collapsed, showing nothing, still yields the crests and the charge out of the by-ID probe) |
| `Modules/QEImport.lua` (shipped M2-1) | parse pasted JSON -> verdict; store it | `ns.QEImport.Parse(text)` -> `{ ok = true, verdict, warnings }` or `{ ok = false, reason }`. Verdict `{ exportedAt, spec, contentType, reportId, player, topSet = { score, stats, items = [itemKey], order = {itemKey...} }, alternatives = { {scorePercent, hpsDifference, items (a list), gems} }, vault = [itemKey], skippedItems }`; item `{ key, itemID, slot, level, bonusIDs (sorted), gems, enchant, tertiary, setId, isVault, isExclusive, source, count }`. Refuses: nothing pasted, non-JSON, non-object JSON, `schema ~= "qe-live-droptimizer"`, `version` that is not the number `1` (names both), missing `topSet`, `gameType ~= "Retail"` - each naming what it saw, strings clipped to 40 chars. Warns (never refuses) on a character-name mismatch, an empty topSet, and items skipped for a bad itemID. `Store`/`Current`/`Import` keep the last verdict in `db.char.qeImport` with `exportedAt` and `importedAt`. `AlternativeIsBetter(alt)` is the single reading of the sign convention. **C-6 (WKE-540) adds the named scenario as part of a Top Gear verdict's identity:** `SCENARIOS` is `asOffered`, `catalyzed`, `maxed` (the companion's strings verbatim), `DEFAULT_SCENARIO` is `asOffered` and `ScenarioKey(verdict)` reads a MISSING scenario as it - every paste and every file written before C-6 says nothing and every one of them is the character as it stands - while a name this build does not know gets `UNKNOWN_SCENARIO` rather than the default's shelf. `Store` files every verdict under `db.char.qeImportsByScenario[contentType][scenario]` and **writes `qeImport` / `qeImports[contentType]` only for `asOffered`**, which is the whole of "Equip Now and the Upgrade Map never change meaning"; `ForContentTypeAndScenario` and `Scenarios(contentType)` (ask-order, unknown names appended, a pre-C-6 verdict answered as `asOffered` when the shelf is empty) read them back, and `Existing(verdict)` compares like with like so three answers about one content type are not two repeats. **`CatalyzedCoverage(verdict, option)` is the second join, and the only reason the Catalyst question is answerable:** `autoCatalyze` does not change an item, it ADDS a clone (`SimCImportEngine.ts` ~253-265) whose `convertToTier` takes the tier piece's item ID and set ID and keeps the slot, level, bonus IDs and `vaultItem` flag, and his exporter carries neither `catalyzedID` nor `isCatalystItem` - so his clone is recognised by the fields he copied (same slot, same NON-EMPTY bonus IDs, still `isVault`, a different item ID) and by nothing else. It is a join, not a model: nothing here knows what can be catalyzed. **`CatalyzedOwned(verdict, inventory)` (M3-13) points that same join at the inventory scan** and lists every piece of his BEST SET that is his clone of an item the owner already owns, in his top-set order, guarded by the run's own `autoCatalyze`, by the exact key already being in the scan, and by there being a scan at all. **`OneChargeCandidates(verdict, inventory, vaultOptions)` (M3-14, WKE-555; the vault argument M3-15, WKE-556) answers the fifth question off his own alternatives:** the top set and each differential is a candidate SET - a differential names a slot completely (his `buildDifferential`, `TopGearEngine.ts:499-511`, pushes both rings or both trinkets the first time either differs), so applying one is "every item in a slot he names comes from him, every other slot keeps the top set's" - and the sets whose `CatalyzedOwned` count is exactly ONE come back ordered by `AlternativeRank`, each carrying his `scorePercent` / `hpsDifference` (0 for the top set, which is zero from itself by definition) and the single clone it spends the charge on. It chooses no item and adds no number. **M3-15 (WKE-556) fixes what "the charge" counts.** 555's issue said a one-charge set had exactly one catalyzed OWNED item and skipped every `isVault` item; the Catalyst spends a charge per item converted and does not care whose the item is. `conversionOf` is now the single place a set item is weighed: a tier item flagged `isVault` is a charge unless `ns.Vault.Options()` is offering that very item ID (a reward that arrives as a tier piece converts nothing), it is named through the same slot-and-bonus-IDs join `CatalyzedCoverage` uses, and with no vault snapshot it is counted unnamed - the conservative reading, because calling a conversion free is the only mistake worth avoiding. `CatalyzedOwned(verdict, inventory)` is unchanged and still owned-only by design; **`CatalyzedVault(verdict, vaultOptions)`** is its sibling for the other half, guarded by `autoCatalyze` alone because it reads no bags | yes, fully (`spec/qeimport_spec.lua`, 84 tests over real JSON text) |
| `Modules/UFImport.lua` (shipped M3-6) | parse a pasted Upgrade Finder export -> verdict; store it | `ns.UFImport.Parse(text)` -> `{ ok = true, verdict, warnings }` or `{ ok = false, reason }`. Verdict `{ schema, version, exportedAt, spec, contentType, reportId, player, settings (his `ufSettings`), items = ["<itemID>@<itemLevel>"], order, levelsByItemID, skippedItems }`; entry `{ key, itemID, level, slot, instanceID, encounterID, dropLoc, dropType, dropDifficulty, upgradePercent, hpsGain, score, count, sources }`. Refuses: nothing pasted, non-JSON, `schema ~= "qe-live-upgradefinder"` (naming both), `version` that is not the number `1`, missing `items`, `gameType ~= "Retail"`. Warns on a character-name mismatch, an export that ranks nothing, drops skipped for a bad itemID or level, and a drop listed twice with different values (the first is kept). **`UFImport.UPGRADE_BETTER_PERCENT_SIGN` and `UPGRADE_BETTER_HPS_GAIN_SIGN` are both `+1` and mean the OPPOSITE of `QEImport`'s `ALT_WORSE_*`** - the same number carrying the other meaning, which is why this is its own file (decision 2026-09-07). `Lookup(verdict, itemID, level)` answers only for the EXACT item level; `LevelsFor(verdict, itemID)` says which levels the export does carry, so a caller can tell silence from a level mismatch and never turn one into a number. A drop several drop types list is one entry with several `sources`, not several entries. `Store`/`Current`/`ForContentType`/`StoredContentTypes`/`Import` mirror `QEImport` over `db.char.ufImport` / `db.char.ufImports[contentType]`. **C-7 (WKE-543) adds the Mythic+ key level as part of a verdict's identity:** `KeyLevelOf(verdict)` reads `verdict.keyLevel` and insists on a whole non-negative NUMBER (the string "10" is not one - this decides a shelf, and a value that had to be converted is not one anybody wrote deliberately); `KeyLevelKey` is the lenient counterpart that normalises what a CALLER asks for, and answers `UNKNOWN_KEY_LEVEL` for anything that is not a level; `Store` also files under `db.char.ufImportsByLevel[contentType][keyLevel]`; `ForContentTypeAndLevel` and `StoredKeyLevels(contentType)` (ascending, plus whether one export does not say) read them back; `Existing(verdict)` is what `ns.Companion` asks instead of matching on content type. **M3-10 (WKE-545) replaced C-7's single-document pick with the cross-level join, and `PickForLevel` / `KeyLevelNote` / `PICK_*` are gone with it:** `Documents(contentType)` -> `{ { verdict, keyLevel } }` ascending with a document that names no level last (and a pre-C-7 `ufImports` entry as the whole list when the by-level shelf is empty) is **the one place the database is read**, and everything else is pure over that list; `LookupAcrossLevels(documents, itemID, level)` -> `entry, keyLevel, how, matches` keeps the EXACT `itemID@itemLevel` match wherever it is found and answers `nil` when no document carries it - never a neighbouring level, never an interpolation - with `MATCH_DROP` (QE Live's own `dropType` calls that level a `drop`, read off the entry and its `sources`, which is what `IsDropAtLevel` is for) beating `MATCH_LOWEST` (the lowest key level) when several carry it, and `MATCH_ONLY` when one does; `LevelsAcrossLevels` is `LevelsFor` over the whole set, so "QE Live has never seen this drop" stays distinguishable from "never at this level"; `KeyLabel(level)` is the only place a level becomes a word (`+6`, `+%d` and nothing else); `DocumentsNote` is the sentence the panel puts under its header (`QE Live's Upgrade Finder at +2, +4, +6, +8, +10 (5 documents).`). **Nothing here converts `settings.dungeon` into a key level**: that field is an index into QE Live's own table and turning it into a level would restate his data in Lua. **R-4 (WKE-565) makes the two non-drop `dropLoc` values first-class:** `SourceKind(dropLoc)` answers `SOURCE_KIND_CRAFT` for `"Crafted"` and `SOURCE_KIND_DELVE` for `"Delves"` and nil for everything else (a boss drop, and the number his own unit-test fixture uses); `UFImport.Item` puts that kind on the entry and carries a crafted row's `source.encounterId` as `professionIndex`, an index into his profession table that is never turned into a name; `CraftedSettings(verdict)` reads `settings.craftedStats` and `settings.craftedLevel` as `{ stats, levelIndex }`, **`levelIndex` being an index and never an item level** (2 in every export, whose rows are 331); `SourceRows(documents, kind, slot)` is the list a surface asks for - every row of that kind across the documents, deduplicated by `itemID@itemLevel` with the first document winning, ordered by the percentage HE gave and narrowed to one slot on request - and each entry carries `{ entry, keyLevel, verdict, exportedAt, documents, disagrees, crafted }`. **`disagrees` is what decides whether a row may name a key level at all**: measured over the committed run, the five dungeon documents value all 51 of these rows identically and only the Raid document differs, so a row names a key level only when two stored documents really disagree about it (`DELVE_INSTANCE_ID` -98 and `CRAFTED_INSTANCE_ID` -4 are recorded as facts about his files; the kind is decided by `dropLoc`, which is the field the exporter sets) | yes, fully (`spec/ufimport_spec.lua`, 62 tests over real JSON text: the hand-built sample, the two 2026-09-07 exports, the five 2026-09-08 key-level documents and the Top Gear export as a refusal) |
| `Modules/Companion.lua` (shipped C-2) | read the one file the local companion writes (`Lootpath/Data/QEVerdict.lua`, listed in the `.toc` after `Core.lua`), import each document it carries, and say where the verdict on screen came from | **The chunk's contract, which C-1 must write and nothing else may extend:** it assigns `ns.companionVerdict = { writtenAt = "<ISO 8601 UTC>", companionVersion = "<string>", qeSettings = { autoUpgradeVault = <bool>, autoUpgradeAll = <bool> }, exports = { { schema, contentType, json = "<QE Live's export text, verbatim>" }, ... } }` and does nothing else - Lua strings, numbers, booleans and tables only, no calls. **Since C-12 the chunk may also carry a file-level `scenarioNote = "<one sentence>"`** - what the run did not ask and why, in the companion's own words - which is carried onto every Top Gear verdict exactly as `excluded` is, and onto no Upgrade Finder one. `Companion.Validate(raw, now)` -> `{ok=true, writtenAt, writtenAtEpoch, companionVersion, qeSettings, excluded, scenarioNote, exports}`, or `{ok=false, absent=true}` for the untouched placeholder (silent, not a refusal), or a refusal naming what it read. `Companion.Entry(raw, index)` refuses a non-table entry, a non-string `json`, one past `MAX_JSON_BYTES` (4 MB), a missing schema, and a schema **neither importer owns** - naming both, and never guessing at one. **Since M3-6 `Companion.ImporterFor(schema)` picks `ns.QEImport` or `ns.UFImport`**; before it, a `qe-live-upgradefinder` document was refused by name rather than handed to the Top Gear parser. `Companion.ImportAll(raw, now)` -> `{ok, imported, skipped, unchanged}`: each `json` goes through **its own importer's `Parse` and `Store`, the same path as a paste**, so every schema / version 1 / `gameType` refusal applies verbatim, and a refusal is reported without disturbing what is stored. This file calls exactly four things of an importer - `Parse`, `ContentTypeKey`, `ForContentType`, `Store` - and reaches past none of them into a verdict's shape, because the two shapes disagree and their signs are opposites. The staleness check therefore compares **like with like**: a Top Gear import is never made stale by an Upgrade Finder one for the same content type. The verdict is filed under **the content type inside the JSON**, never the entry's advisory `contentType`, and carries `source = "companion"`, `companionWrittenAt`, `companionVersion` and, **since M3-7, `qeSettings`** (per document since C-6, falling back to the file's) - the pair C-5 records, carried onto the verdict rather than left in the file's result because the Vault panel reads it off whichever verdict is on screen, which may have come back from SavedVariables long after that file was replaced. `Companion.Settings(raw)` is the only reader of it: `ns.Safe` first, then **both** of `autoUpgradeVault` / `autoUpgradeAll` must really be booleans or the whole pair is nil, because half a stated pair is not a setting; `autoCatalyze` (C-6) rides along when it really is a boolean and is left out otherwise, so a file that never mentions the Catalyst claims nothing about it. **C-6 also reads each entry's `scenario`**, sets it on the verdict BEFORE asking the importer what that verdict replaces (without which three Top Gear answers for one content type read as three repeats of the first, exactly the failure C-7 hit with key levels), and passes the string through unrepaired - `QEImport.ScenarioKey` is the one place a name becomes a shelf. A file that does not say (anything written before C-5, and the committed placeholder) still imports; it simply says nothing about assumptions. **The later of the two wins:** a file older than the stored import's `companionWrittenAt`/`importedAt` is skipped and said so; the same file read again on the next `/reload` is `unchanged` and silent. Every field passes `ns.Safe` before its type is checked, nothing in the chunk is called, there is no `loadstring`. `Companion.SourceText(verdict, now)` is the one phrase the window and `/lootpath status` show (`"pasted"` / `"companion, written N minute(s) ago"`). **C-8 reads each entry's `excluded` list** (`Companion.Excluded`, the file's as the fallback for a document that carries none), and **C-10 (WKE-567) adds the identity to it**: an entry carries `itemID`, sorted `bonusIDs` and, on a Catalyst clone, the `originalItem` it was made from, all of them QE Live's own `data-wowhead` numbers carried unchanged. `Companion.ExcludedKey(entry)` BUILDS `ns.ItemKey` from the first two - the key format is Core.lua's one definition and is never read out of the file - and `Companion.IsExcluded(excluded, key, item)` answers "was this item left out" with the entry and with how it matched (`EXCLUDED_BY_KEY`, or `EXCLUDED_BY_NAME` for an entry that carries no identity, which is every file written before C-10). An entry with an identity is matched on identity ALONE, because a name match against an entry whose key says a different item is a wrong answer rather than a second chance; a `bonusIDs` field that is present and unreadable takes the item ID down with it, since `ns.ItemKey` over a shortened list is a valid key for an item nobody owns. **`Companion.Refresh()` is `/lootpath refresh`, and since C-3 it captures before it reloads**: `Companion.REFRESH_CAPTURES` is `{ "env", "inventory", "vault" }` and each is run through `ns.RunCapture` in that order, so the three snapshots the companion's profile is built from are this session's. Any refusal (`combat`, the DB not loaded, another capture still running) is repeated verbatim in chat and **stops the reload**; a client with no `ReloadUI` is refused before anything is captured, because three snapshots nobody can flush are three snapshots written for nothing. `journal` is never among them - it is the one asynchronous capture, the SimC profile reads none of it, and `db.global.journalCache` is untouched by a refresh. `Companion.RefreshSummary(snapshots)` is the chat line's `gear, bags, bank (open/closed), vault`, and it reads the bank half back out of **the snapshot just taken** (`C_Bank.CanViewBank` for the character's own bank) rather than asking the client a second time that could disagree with what the companion will read. It **refuses in combat** (`InCombatLockdown`), where `ReloadUI` is protected and the captures refuse anyway. **Since M3-16a (WKE-581) the chain never calls `ReloadUI` from a callback.** It tracks whether any capture answered `pending`; if none did, all four finished inside the slash command's own execution and it reloads immediately as it did before M3-16. If one did, it calls `Companion.AskForReload`, which shows `StaticPopupDialogs[Companion.RELOAD_POPUP]` - `Gear captured. Reload to send it?`, Reload / Not now, `timeout = 0`, `whileDead`, `hideOnEscape`, `ReloadUI` in `OnAccept` - and returns `{ ok = true, reloaded = false, reloadPending = "popup" }`; a client with no `StaticPopup_Show` gets the same thing said in chat and `reloadPending = "chat"`. R-6's `Drift.RefreshStarting()` stamp is written on BOTH paths, so the strip's wait line stands behind a click that reloads. **`Companion.AskVaultAtLogin(onDone)` is the login half:** once per session (`Companion.loginAsked`), never in combat (it answers nil and the login defers to `PLAYER_REGEN_ENABLED`), asked only when `HasAvailableRewards()` is true and not one activity carries a reward - read through `ns.Safe`, not iterated raw - and run through `ns.VaultInteract` with **no capture and no reload**. The record it leaves on `ns.vaultLoginAsk` is what the next `env` snapshot carries. **`Companion.CaptureAtFlush()` is R-7's sequence under R-7a's name (WKE-582):** the four `REFRESH_CAPTURES` at `PLAYER_LOGOUT`, no reload, nothing asynchronous, the vault handed `FLUSH_VAULT_ARGS` (`{ skipInteract = "flush" }`) so `OnUIInteract` is never called there, each capture in its own `pcall` so one that throws leaves the rest standing, and `ns.captureTrigger = Companion.FLUSH_TRIGGER` (`"flush"`) restored afterwards. The `env` snapshot is stamped `capturedOn = "flush"` and `flushMs` after the sequence, because its cost is not known until it is over; nothing in the addon reads either, they are the evidence. The panel draws a fixed list of at most 20 rows (a full set is 15-19) with the remainder counted, not dropped. **M5-1 (WKE-550): a row is a `UI.ItemLine`, not a font string.** The widget takes one shape - `{ itemID, link, name, quality, itemLevel, icon, second, badge = { text, tone, hex, atlas }, tags }` - and draws the icon (the caller's, else `ItemData.Instant`, else `INV_Misc_QuestionMark`), an `IconBorder` of `Interface\Common\WhiteIconFrame` tinted through `ColorManager.GetColorDataForItemQuality` then `C_Item.GetItemQualityColor` then `ITEM_QUALITY_COLORS` (each guarded; **no answer means no border, never a guessed one**), the item level bottom-right of the icon in `NumberFontNormalSmall`, the name in quality colour on one non-wrapping line, a grey second line, a text badge and tag words. A row the client cannot name yet says `RETRIEVING_ITEM_INFO` and asks `ns.ItemData.Request`, cancelling the handle whenever the line is handed another item, so a late answer never lands on a re-used row; a row whose data never arrives stays exactly as it is. Hovering the icon or the name opens the item's own tooltip - `SetHyperlink` with a link, `SetItemByID` without one - with `GameTooltip_ShowCompareItem` behind a type check. **The badge is text, never a bar**, because a bar's length would be arithmetic on QE Live's numbers; its tones are his (`better` `#FFDF14`, `worse` `#C16719` at 0.72 alpha, `none`/`neutral` grey) and its tag colours are his words resolved through the CSS named-colour table (Vault aqua `00FFFF`, Catalyst plum `DDA0DD`, Tier yellow `FFFF00`, Exclusive orange `FFA500`, an unknown tag in grey rather than dropped). Every atlas is validated at runtime with `C_Texture.GetAtlasInfo` and has a word to fall back on. **Equip Now binds it:** `Describe` keeps `text` and `note` word for word and gains `name`, `second`, `badge`, `tags`, `item` and `worn`; a swap is worn icon -> `common-icon-forwardarrow` -> best icon with the Equip button, an already-best row is one icon with `common-icon-checkmark` in the badge, a `best_in_vault` row carries the Vault tag and keeps its note; the five counts are chips above the list (`EquipPanel.Chips`, pure) and `EquipPanel.NoteText` keeps only what the chips do not say, while `SummaryText` stays the whole line `/lootpath status` reads. Twenty item lines are 760 points against the window's body, so **the list became a `UIPanelScrollFrameTemplate`** rather than the panel deciding the window's size for M5-2. **M2-4 (WKE-541): `EquipPanel.Describe` returns `note` as well as `text`, and every sentence a row has to say lives on the note.** The detail line is one line that does not wrap, so anything long appended to it used to run off the frame; the note is a second font string under it, anchored LEFT and RIGHT to the row (no `SetWidth`, so its width is the frame's), `SetWordWrap(true)`, and the row grows by `EquipPanel.NOTE_HEIGHT` when it has one. A `best_in_vault` row shows the item you wear now as **already equipped** in its own blue - not the gap's red, and never `X -> Y (not found)` - with `EquipPanel.VaultNoteText` underneath in the panel's note grey: `QE Live's best set has a Great Vault option in this slot: <name> (QE Live's level <n>) - see the Vault tab`. It quotes QE Live's level and points at the tab; it computes nothing. A `best_not_owned` row keeps `(not found)` on the first line and puts the reason on the note. The summary line counts the vault apart: `N already best, N to swap, N waiting in the Great Vault, N not owned, N without a verdict`. The Equip button is anchored to the row's top rather than its middle so a two-line row still puts it beside the first line, and the overflow line anchors under the last row drawn rather than under the list frame's nominal height. **Tabs (M3-3):** `UI.TABS` is the three promises in the order §1 states them; the buttons are `PanelTabButtonTemplate` (`parentArray="Tabs"`, so they land in `frame.Tabs`) and `PanelTemplates_SetNumTabs`/`SetTab` are called through a `type(...) == "function"` check for the selected artwork ONLY - which panel is visible is `UI.ShowTab`'s own state, so a client without those helpers still tabs. **Title (M3-5):** `ns.VERSION` is the `.toc` metadata unless it still starts with `@`, which is the BigWigs packager's unsubstituted token on a dev copy - then it is `"dev"`. **`UI.Refresh` redraws only the tab on screen**, because rebuilding the journal map on every `BAG_UPDATE_DELAYED` would walk 613 rows for a panel nobody is looking at; `UI.RefreshEquip` is the Equip tab's half, split out for that. **`UI.RefreshVault` (M3-12)** is the third: the Vault module's second read calls it when a pending reward resolves, and it redraws the Vault tab only when the window is open on it (`UI.VAULT_TAB`), touching nothing else. The sentence, the `Also stored:` line and why an age is amber live in the strip's tooltip, never on the line. **The Import dialog** is a `BasicFrameTemplateWithInset` frame (`LootpathImportDialog`, also in `UISpecialFrames`) built with the window and hidden: it holds the editbox, Import, Clear and the import status line, and `frame.pasteBox`/`importButton`/`clearButton`/`status` are aliases onto it, so `UI.Import`, `UI.ImportAny` and `UI.StatusText` are untouched. **The launcher** is `## AddonCompartmentFunc: LootpathToggle` in the `.toc` - the one global Lootpath defines, which Blizzard's `AddonCompartmentMixin` looks up in `_G` - plus a native minimap button (`UI.MinimapButton`, built in `onReady`) whose position is `UI.MinimapButtonOffset(angle)` at radius 80 and whose drag reads the angle back with `UI.MinimapAngleFrom`, saved in `db.profile.settings.minimapAngle`. **Two settings** join the two dropdowns: `Settings.CreateSlider` over `Settings.CreateSliderOptions(0.7, 1.3, 0.05)` for `settings.scale` (clamped reading AND writing, applied with `frame:SetScale` on the window alone) and `Settings.CreateCheckbox` for `settings.compactRows`, which is stored for M5-1's item line and draws nothing yet. It walks `sources` itself rather than regrouping `Model`'s output, because **the denominator has to be every drop the journal lists for that run** - the cosmetics the slot view hides and the rows whose item data never arrived included - and a filtered list cannot say that. `run.rated` counts only drops `ns.UFImport.IsUpgrade` calls upgrades, over the same exact itemID + item level join and no other; `run.best` is the largest of those and `run.bestText` carries HIS sign and magnitude with no direction word of its own. Two orders, `Panel.SORT_BEST` and `Panel.SORT_COUNT`, and a run with nothing rated sorts last under both as a consequence of them rather than as a clause of its own. `model.headline` restates the top row as one sentence; `Panel.RUN_NOTE` says on screen that neither ranking is weighted by drop chance; `model.keyLevelNote` names the key levels shown. the row's label and index, **never its progress**, which the client zeroes after a reset (finding 2, WKE-538). `Panel.HeadlineLine` then draws one line per stored scenario **in QE Live's own order**, each built from the SAME `reward.scenarioLines` the list below draws, so the block cannot claim a pick the list does not carry: the scenario's own best by `coverageRank`, said as `in your best set` when he put it in the top set and `nothing in the vault beats your set` when his answer leaves every option in an alternative, with the item named only when it differs from the headline's (`%s instead`) and `, as tier` carried through from `ScenarioCoverage` when the number is about his catalyzed clone. `Panel.NEEDS_TEXT` is a **fixed phrase per scenario naming what that answer ASSUMED** - `catalyzed` "needs a Catalyst charge", `maxed` "needs crests", `asOffered` nothing, because it assumed nothing - and `Panel.CountText` puts the client's count after it from `ns.Currencies.Read()`: the number - It sits in `model.headline.lines` (and on `model.headline.oneCharge`, `kind = "oneCharge"`) rather than in bespoke drawing, because M5-4 redraws this tab. The line is absent entirely - not even the absence sentence - when the question does not arise: no `thisWeek` document, the Catalyst box off in the run that made one, or no inventory scan, all three of which empty `CatalyzedOwned` first. **M3-15 (WKE-556)** hands the vault snapshot to `OneChargeCandidates`, asks for the line when `CatalyzedOwned` OR `CatalyzedVault` finds anything, and on the owner's own documents that makes the line the absence sentence (§9). **M5-4 (WKE-553) is the tab drawn as the Great Vault, and it adds no sentence to `Panel.Lines` and removes none - the text half is byte-for-byte what it was.** What is new on the model: `Panel.NoteLines(model)` (the notes alone, which the drawn rows print and `Panel.Lines` now begins with, so the two cannot drift), `model.grid` and `model.currencyChips`, `icon` and `quality` on every gear row, `raidString` carried through from the module, and `model.headline.closest`. `Panel.Grid(model, highlight, best, closest)` lays out **three rows of three cells in Blizzard's own order** - `Panel.ROW_ORDER = { Raid, Activities, World }`, read off `WeeklyRewardsFrame:SetUpActivities`, which calls SetUpActivity with RAIDS, then DUNGEONS, then WORLD and sets the PvP row up separately - places each option by its own `index`, and puts **every option those three rows do not hold** (Concession, "Also receive") in `grid.other` with its header and its extras, so nothing the client offers leaves the screen. `Panel.GridRowLabel` / `Panel.RowLabel` prefer the client's own `RAIDS` / `DUNGEONS` / `WORLD`; **their values are nowhere in this repo** - there is no GlobalStrings transcript under `.luals/` - so they are asked for at runtime and the labels measured off the owner's 2026-09-08 vault screen are the fallback, which is also what the headless stub exercises because it deliberately defines none of them. `Panel.ThresholdText(option, enumName)` is a locked cell's words, the same choice `WeeklyRewardsActivityMixin:Refresh` makes - the activity's own `raidString` on a Raid row, then `WEEKLY_REWARDS_THRESHOLD_RAID` / `_DUNGEONS` / `_WORLD` - formatted with the threshold inside a `pcall`, because a pattern this addon did not write must not be able to throw the panel; with no pattern at all the cell keeps the progress wording the text list prints. `Panel.Cell` binds one reward as an M5-1 item line (`icon`, `quality`, the level in the icon's corner), a grey second line of **slot + M3-7's own level wording unchanged**, the highlighted scenario's line as `verdictText` with `Panel.VerdictTone` picking QE Live's own two colours (top set or `isBetter` gold, anything else he ranked burnt orange, an option he never ranked grey and `not ranked by QE Live in any scenario`), `Panel.CellTags` saying **Catalyst / Tier only when his answer on that cell is about his catalyzed clone**, the extras in words, and `tooltipLines = reward.verdictLines` - the same strings `Panel.Lines` indents, which is the whole point of building the hover out of `ScenarioLine`'s output. **The pick is one cell and it is `model.best`**, the same reward that carries `<- QE Live's pick` in the text, drawn with Blizzard's own `evergreen-weeklyrewards-reward-selected` (the SelectedTexture of `WeeklyRewardsActivityTemplate`) validated at runtime through `ItemLine.Atlas`, with four edges tinted `FFDF14` - M5-1's `better` hex, defined once - when the client no longer has it; when `model.headline.closest` is true (the headline's own `none - nothing in the vault beats your set` test, computed once and shared) **nothing glows** and the closest option gets a grey `closest` label. `Panel.CurrencyChips(currencies)` is the strip under the grid: one chip per crest and one for the charge, each the client's own `name`, its own `iconFileID` and its own count (`1 of 8` is still two client numbers side by side), and an empty list whenever nothing has read the currencies - the tab then says so rather than drawing question marks. The scenario dropdown moved onto the tab's header row as a `DropdownButton` with `WowStyle1DropdownTemplate` (`SetupMenu` with a generator, `rootDescription:CreateRadio(text, isSelected, setSelected)`, `SetDefaultText` through a `pcall` because the generated annotations do not record that the template mixes `DropdownSelectionTextMixin` in), labelled with the Settings page's own `SCENARIO_CHOICE_LABEL` and writing through `ns.UI.Options.SetVaultScenario` - one stored setting, two ways to reach it, and the Settings page keeps its copy. The whole thing is a `pcall(CreateFrame, "DropdownButton", ...)`: a client without the template loses the dropdown on the tab and nothing else. **R-3a (WKE-570): the `the pick` label lives inside its cell.** It was anchored `BOTTOMLEFT` to the cell's `TOPLEFT`, which put all of it but one point above the cell and, on the owner's screen, on the bottom edge of the row above; it is `TOPLEFT` to `TOPLEFT` now, and every cell reserves a 12-point band above its item line for it - on every cell, not only the labelled one, so a cell's contents do not move as the pick moves. `CELL_HEIGHT` grew by the band and the row's own left label dropped with the item lines beside it | yes (`spec/vaultpanel_spec.lua`; the M3-12 block replays vault snapshot 13 of the 2026-09-09 transcript, asserts no line carries `[]` or `nil`, and drives the frame through the second read; the M3-7 block replays vault snapshot 9 of the 2026-09-08 transcript against the companion's own export) |

| `tools/companion/` (C-1, WKE-533, plus C-4; NOT shipped in the addon zip - `.pkgmeta` ignores `tools`) | the local companion: SavedVariables -> SimC profile -> QE Live in the owner's fork -> `Data/QEVerdict.lua` | `companion.js` is the CLI (`--watch`, `--profile-only`, `--config`, `--out`, `--force`) and owns the exit codes (0 ok, 1 usage/config, 2 no SavedVariables, 3 profile incomplete, 4 fork unreachable, 5 QE Live refused, 6 write failed). **`lib/lua-savedvariables.js` and `lib/simc-profile.js` are S-2's, lifted out of `tools/companion-spike/` rather than copied**: C-1 wrote its own pair first, in parallel, and threw them away when S-2 merged, because S-2's mirrors the SimulationCraft addon's own offsets and slot tables, reproduces the client's `# Checksum:` with adler32, and enforces QE Live's two line-index rules itself. `lib/profile.js` is the thin part C-1 adds: one `{ ok, text, warnings, counts }` shape so a bad transcript is a named exit code rather than a stack trace, and the spec check. `lib/fork.js` is S-1's driver with a persistent browser profile (the welcome dialog is answered once) and `npm start` when nothing answers; **since C-5 (WKE-539) it also sets QE Live's two upgrade checkboxes itself before Submit** - `CHECKBOX_LABELS` names all three by the JSX string literal each is rendered with (`SimCraftDialog.js` lines 122-133; they are NOT `translate.json` keys), the two it was asked about are clicked only when they are wrong, both are read back, a click that did not take or a label that is not on the page is a `DRIVE` failure (exit 4) rather than a guess, `autoCatalyze` is never touched, and `settingsFrom()` reduces the per-box detail the log wants to the two booleans the writer records. **Since C-6 (WKE-540) the driver's unit of work is a PASS, not a document**: `configLib.plannedPasses(config, { hasVaultGear, force })` hands it an import plus the documents that import can answer, and `run()` re-imports the profile at the head of every pass because the boxes act at import. `lib/luawriter.js` renders the chunk; `lib/output.js` writes it to a temp file beside the target and renames, so the client never reads half a file and a failed run leaves the last good verdict alone; `lib/watch.js` gives one run per `/reload` (debounce plus two stats that agree on the size). **`lib/fingerprint.js` is C-4's (WKE-537): the profile decides whether QE Live is asked at all.** Since C-6 it also hashes the named scenario list (adding a scenario is a new question and must cost a run; reordering one must not). Since C-5 it hashes **the profile and the two QE Live upgrade settings together** - a canonical `# qeSettings autoUpgradeAll=... autoUpgradeVault=...` line appended to the kept lines, keys sorted - because the same gear asked about with `autoUpgradeVault` flipped is a different answer. It hashes the built SimC profile with four volatile lines removed - the identity line and `# Inventory captured ...` (both carry the capture stamp), `# Built by ...` (it names the tool, not the character) and `# Checksum:` (adler32 over the body, so it follows the stamps) - and remembers `{ hash, writtenAt, verdict }` in `<stateDir>/last-profile.json` after each verdict is safely written, never before. `once()` skips the QE Live run when the hash matches, the remembered path is the file this run would write, and that file still exists; `--force` overrides, and a missing, unreadable or hash-less state file runs and says why, because forgetting must cost a run and never skip one. The profile is what is hashed, not the SavedVariables: anything the profile does not carry cannot change QE Live's answer. The fork driver is injectable through `once(config, log, args, deps)` for exactly one reason - so a guard can prove the browser was NOT opened. **The file it writes is C-2's contract, not its own:** `ns.companionVerdict = { writtenAt, companionVersion, profileCapturedAt, qeSettings = { autoUpgradeVault, autoUpgradeAll } (C-5, booleans; the file-level pair, and since C-6 the Upgrade Finder's), exports = { { schema = "qe-live-droptimizer"|"qe-live-upgradefinder", contentType = "Dungeon"|"Raid", keyLevel (C-7, Upgrade Finder only), scenario (C-6, Top Gear only, one of the three names), qeSettings = { autoUpgradeVault, autoUpgradeAll, autoCatalyze } (C-6, per document, read back off his page), bytes, json } ... } }` - the writer REFUSES a Top Gear document with no scenario and an Upgrade Finder document that claims one, for the reason it refuses a key level on the wrong kind: a document filed under the wrong question is a wrong answer that looks right - `json` is QE Live's export text verbatim, so `ns.Companion` feeds it to the same `ns.QEImport.Parse` a paste goes through and every pin and refusal still applies. **Since C-9 (WKE-559) it writes two more files, and only ever into that same `Data/` folder:** `Data/companion.log`, every line it prints with a full ISO stamp instead of the terminal's clock, rotated at 200 KB keeping one `.1` (`lib/log.js` gained `consoleSink` / `fileSink` / `tee`, and a sink that cannot write is reported ONCE and then goes quiet - a companion that dies over its own log is worse than one that says nothing about it); and `Data/CompanionStatus.lua` (`lib/status.js`), C-9's contract above, written at EVERY stage change through the verdict writer's own escaper and `output.writeAtomic`. `lib/output.js` now owns one directory rule for all three files (`dataDir`: the Data folder may be created, the AddOns folder never). `--profile-only` writes the log and NOT the status, and `--out` moves both beside the file it writes: a dry run must not overwrite what the game is about to read. `lib/lock.js` is "never two watchers" enforced rather than remembered - `<stateDir>/watch.lock` holds the pid, a second `--watch` exits **7** naming it, and a lock whose process is gone is taken over and said so, so a machine that lost power mid-run never needs a file deleted by hand. `start-companion.ps1` (the fork if nothing answers its port, then `node companion.js --watch`), `install-startup.ps1` and `uninstall-startup.ps1` register and remove the at-logon Task Scheduler task for the current user; all three are idempotent and none of them stops a watcher the owner started himself | yes: 186 tests on Node's own runner (`node --test` in `tools/companion`, a sixth CI job and a `check.ps1` gate), S-2's 32 profile guards among them (they were in no gate before), the profile measured against the owner's real `/simc` string, and the writer's golden loaded by a real Lua interpreter in `spec/companionfile_spec.lua`, C-4's 14 guards driving the real `once()` over a fake `_retail_` in the OS temp directory with an injected fork driver (nothing near the real game folder, no browser), and C-5's checkbox step against a fake page that records every click and answers `isChecked` the way his controlled MUI checkboxes do, and C-9's 31 guards over the log, the status chunk, the lock and the run that drives them - including two that run the real CLI in a child process, because the log file and the status recorder are `main()`'s to wire and no in-process test can say it did. The fork driver itself is not mocked - it is proven by a recorded run (§9); **neither is the Task Scheduler task, which is the owner's reboot (WKE-559)** |

| **V-2 (WKE-573), what the status strip carries** | **four facts, not five**: spec, then source and age, then the vault pick, then the companion. The content type and the export's name (`Dungeon Top Gear`) are NOT on the line; they are in the strip's tooltip, inside `UI.VerdictNoteText`'s own sentence - "Showing the Dungeon Top Gear export (companion, written 4 hours ago)." - which the Import dialog shows too. `UI.StatusStripModel` deliberately drops `ActiveVerdict`'s second return on the floor. Why: the window's width cut the line and C-9's companion clause was the half lost, and that clause is the one fact on it a player acts on (R-3a, WKE-570; owner's decision 2026-09-14 evening). What it does not fix: the FAILED clause is still longer than the line has room for (below, 7). |
| **R-6 (WKE-578), the second row** | the strip stays four facts; the nudge is its own row UNDER it, because R-3a's lesson (WKE-570) is that a fifth fact past the window's width is a fact nobody reads. `UI.RefreshNudge` shows or hides `frame.nudgeButton` and grows `frame.statusStrip` by `UI.NUDGE_HEIGHT`; every tab's panel is anchored to the strip's BOTTOMLEFT already, so the body moves with it and nothing is repositioned by hand. The launcher's badge is two `WHITE8X8` layers, R-2b's rule (a mark is drawn at the size it will be seen at), and it follows the NUDGE only - never the wait. | yes (`spec/ui_spec.lua`) |
| **V-1 (WKE-569), who owns a player-facing string** | every word a player can read is a named constant on the panel or module that draws it, and no constant names where a rating came from (decision 2026-09-11, 7). The owners, in full: `Core.lua`'s `HELP` list and its chat lines; `UI/MainFrame.lua` (`PASTE_INSTRUCTIONS`, `NO_VERDICT_STRIP`, `STALE_STRIP_TOOLTIP`, `SCENARIO_TAG`, `VerdictNoteText`, the strip's five facts and the Import dialog's title); `UI/Options.lua` (`LABEL`/`TOOLTIP`/`CHOICE_LABEL` and their `SCENARIO_` twins); `UI/EquipPanel.lua` (`STATUS_BADGE`, `CHIP_ORDER`, `SummaryText`, `NoteText`, `Describe`, `VaultNoteText`, `ExcludedText`); `UI/UpgradeMapPanel.lua` (`NOTE`, `PENDING_NOTE`, `EMPTY_NOTE`, `LEVEL_MISMATCH_NOTE`, `RUN_*`, `KEY_LEVEL_NOTE`, and since V-1 the ten `BADGE_*` sentences the two badge builders write); `UI/VaultPanel.lua` (`NOTE`, `PICK_LABEL`, `HEADLINE_*`, `QE_LEVEL_TEXT`, `SCENARIO_LABEL`, `ONE_CHARGE_*`, `CATALYZE_*`, the cell and currency strings, `NO_VERDICT_NOTE`, `STALE_NOTE`); `Modules/Roads.lua` (the four phrases, the five verbs, `PLAN_LABEL`, the group headers - written to the rule from the start); `Modules/QEImport.lua` and `Modules/UFImport.lua` (every refusal, plus `UFImport.DOCUMENTS_NOTE*`); `Modules/Match.lua` (its refusals and the key-fallback reason); `Modules/Companion.lua` (`SourceText`, `STATUS_*`, `ExcludedText`). **The badge sentences moved into constants for the guard's sake** (V-1): four of `UpgradeBadge`/`ValueBadge`'s branches - a zero percent, a rating with no delta - are unreachable from any committed fixture, so as inline literals they were the one place a source could return unwatched. | `spec/voice_spec.lua` (V-1, WKE-569) reads both halves: the RENDERED half drives the window over the committed fixtures with both genuine exports and reads back every tab's lines and elements, the Vault grid's cells, the strip and its tooltip, the Import dialog, every refusal the two importers and `Match` can produce, the slash help and `/lootpath status`; the CONSTANT half walks the panels' string tables so an unreached branch is still caught. Neither may say `QE Live`, `his`, `he`, `verdict` or "the addon has determined". Bare identifiers are skipped (internal names stay) and one string is allowed by name, `Data\QEVerdict.lua`, with its reason in the file. Each half asserts a measured floor on how many strings it read, so a surface that goes quiet fails rather than passes empty (1200 / 19 / 25 / 29 / 160 on 2026-09-14) |
