**SavedVariables (AceDB):** `db.char.qeImport` (the most recent Top Gear verdict + exportedAt), **`db.char.qeImports[contentType]`** (one verdict per QE Live content type, so a Raid paste does not lose the Dungeon one; since C-2 a verdict also carries `source`, and a companion one `companionWrittenAt` / `companionVersion`, so where it came from survives a reload), **`db.char.ufImport` and `db.char.ufImports[contentType]`** (the same pair for Upgrade Finder exports, M3-6 - separate keys, so neither kind can overwrite the other, and a companion one carries the same `source` fields), **`db.char.qeImportsByScenario[contentType][scenario]`** (C-6: one Top Gear verdict per named scenario, with `"unknown"` as the shelf for a name this build does not know; `qeImport` and `qeImports` are written for `asOffered` alone, so everything that read them before C-6 keeps reading what the character has now), **`db.char.ufImportsByLevel[contentType][keyLevel]`** (C-7: one Upgrade Finder verdict per Mythic+ key level, with the string `"unknown"` as the shelf for an export that does not say which key it was run at; `ufImports` is untouched beside it and still holds the most recent verdict for the content type, so everything written before C-7 keeps reading what it always read - **the cost is that a five-level refresh stores five dungeon verdicts rather than one**, which is the feature and not an accident), `db.global.journalCache[key]`, `db.profile.settings.vaultScenario` (C-6, one of `ns.QEImport.SCENARIOS`; **its default is `thisWeek` since M3-13**, and it moves the Vault tab's highlight only - `qeImport` / `qeImports` stay the `asOffered` shelves whatever it says), `db.profile.settings.contentType` (**`"Dungeon"` or `"Raid"` - QE Live's own strings; it read `"Mythic+"` until M2-2 and could never have matched an export**). Captures write raw returns under a `captures` table pulled by `tools/sync.ps1 -Pull`. **`db.global.drift` is R-6's two remembered facts (WKE-578):** `refreshStartedAt`, the ISO stamp written just before the first reload, and `runSeconds`, how long the last finished rating run took - global, because the companion is one per machine. **Every capture snapshot also carries `trigger`** (`"refresh"` while `ns.Companion.Refresh` drives the chain, `"command"` for one typed by hand), which is the only thing in the file that says how a capture was taken.

**Since C-11 (WKE-572) a Top Gear verdict is identified by content type, scenario AND pass, and only pass 1 is the plan.** `ns.QEImport.PassKey(verdict)` reads a missing or unusable `pass` as `FIRST_PASS` (1), so every paste and every file written before C-11 says exactly what it said. `Store` files a pass above 1 under **`db.char.qePassesByScenario[contentType][scenario][pass]`** and touches none of `qeImport`, `qeImports` or `qeImportsByScenario`; a new pass-1 import for a content type and scenario CLEARS that subtree first, because the later passes of the run before it rated a pool the new file has just replaced. `ForPass` and `Passes(contentType, scenario)` (pass order, pass 1 never among them) read them back, and `Existing` compares like with like so the second document of a run is not read as a repeat of the first. `Modules/Companion.lua` reads `pass` and `considered` off each export entry and carries both onto the verdict; `Companion.IsConsidered` is `IsExcluded` under another name, because a pool is one list of cards and "did this pass see it" must not be a second answer to "did this pass leave it out".

**Libraries (all licence-checked, recorded in `Lootpath/Libs/LICENSES.md`):** LibStub (public domain), CallbackHandler-1.0 and AceDB-3.0 (Ace3 BSD-style, clause quoted in LICENSES.md: embedding permitted, "redistribution of a stand alone version is strictly prohibited"), rxi/json.lua 0.1.2 (MIT; vendored verbatim plus a six-line footer that sets `ns.json`, because the WoW loader discards a chunk's return value). Ace3 arrives as packager externals from the wowace SVN trunks in CI and from the WoWUIDev/Ace3 GitHub mirror locally (`tools/fetch-libs.ps1`); both directories are gitignored. Nothing else without a licence line.

**Test harness (M0-1):** `spec/stubs/wow.lua` installs a fake client into `_G` (shapes from Blizzard's docs, build tuple from the spike capture; placeholders, never wiki) and `spec/helpers/addon.lua` loads the `.toc` file list in order with `(addonName, ns)` varargs; a fake `LibStub("AceDB-3.0")` deep-copies the defaults. Fixtures under `spec/fixtures/` win over the stub whenever they disagree.

**One widget carries one widget's methods (T-1, WKE-560, 2026-09-13).** The stub gives a kind or a template only what that class or that mixin chain has, read under `.luals/vscode-wow-api/Annotations/` and cited at the file and line in the block that models it. A `Frame` has no `SetText` and no `SetTexture`; text setters are a `FontString`'s (and four of them an `EditBox`'s), texture setters are a `Texture`'s, the five scroll setters are a `ScrollFrame`'s, and `SetDefaultText` is `DropdownSelectionTextMixin`'s, which only `WowStyle1DropdownTemplate` mixes in. **Missing the other way is deliberate and safe:** a method no panel calls is simply not modelled (`CheckButton:SetChecked`, `DropdownSelectionTextMixin:SetSelectionText`, `DropdownButtonMixin:Pick`), so a panel that starts calling one fails headlessly first - a gap turns a test red where the client would have been fine, while a superset turns a test green where the client breaks. **A template is ADDED to, never subtracted from:** the caption surface is attached to the one template whose mixin has it rather than handed to every dropdown and taken back, because a widget built without a template never reaches the subtraction. **Nothing that is not the client's sits in a Blizzard method slot:** the stub's own affordances - firing an `OnEnter`, picking a menu row by index, acquiring a pooled frame, reading a tooltip back - live on `widget.stub` (`button.stub:Enter()`, `dropdown.stub:Pick(2)`, `world.tooltip.stub:Text()`), so a panel that reached for one in the client would index a nil field on the first frame. `spec/stubs_spec.lua` locks all of it: every removal is asserted absent on the widget that does not have it and present on the one that does.

---

## 7. Decisions log (dated; do not reopen without a new entry here)

**2026-09-15 (C-11, WKE-572) - a Top Gear run is a sequence of passes, and an item's rating comes from the pass that saw it.** The owner's decision on WKE-558, taken 2026-09-14 evening, was option (c): not a patron tier, not living within thirty. The fork's cap is never edited (§7, 2026-09-07: the fork is read and driven, never changed in what it decides). What was built:

  (a) **The baseline is read at import, once, before anything is clicked.** It is the cards QE Live made active, which his own engine says are the equipped set, the vault options and any clone of one of those. It is in every pass's pool and nothing ever deselects one. After pass 1 "active" means "pass 1 clicked it", so a driver that read the baseline later would keep the wrong set.

  (b) **Each later pass deselects the previous pass's bag items and asks about cards nothing has asked about yet.** No card outside the baseline is asked twice; the grid is checked ident-for-ident between passes, because every click is by position and a grid that moved would choose a pool nobody named. The loop stops on the first of: nothing left out, no room past the baseline (a second document there would be the first one's pool again), or `topGearPasses`.

  (c) **Every pass is its own document. Nothing is merged.** `pass` and `considered` travel beside C-8's `excluded`, in the same shape, so the addon asks one identity question (C-10) of both lists.

  (d) **Pass 1 is the plan and the later passes cannot reach it.** They are filed on `qePassesByScenario`, which no surface that draws a week's plan reads. This is the same deliverable C-6 wrote for the scenarios and it fails the same way if it is broken: a later pass's pool holds no vault option and no Catalyst clone, so a best set drawn from one would tell the owner to skip his vault.

  (e) **No percent from a later pass is printed beside a pass-1 row, and the issue's step 3 is answered to that extent and no further.** The issue asked for a pass-2 item to be rated "against your best set" in the Upgrade Map's `+x%` / `behind` words. It cannot be, honestly: a later pass's differentials are against THAT pass's top set, which is built over the equipped baseline plus the leftovers and is not your best set. What IS honest is the membership question, so the road model says `rated · better than what you wear` when the pass put the item in its own top set, and the existing third phrase `not in your best set` when it did not - the second is sound because a pool that held everything the character is wearing and did not pick the item is a pool the fuller one could not have picked it out of either. The tooltip's sentence for the first case is `This beats what you've got on. The plan was built before anything had rated it.`, which takes the plan's position without inventing an imperative the plan has no grounds for.

  (f) **`not rated · beyond the rating's item limit` is kept, and now means what it says.** The issue asked for it to be retired from every surface. It cannot be while the pass bound exists: four passes over the owner's 63 cards ask about 30 + 10 + 10 + 10 = 60 of them, so three can still be left. The phrase is now reached only by an item NO pass was ever shown, which is what the last pass's `excluded` list is, and is empty in any run that finishes inside the bound.

**2026-09-15 (C-12, WKE-577) - a question is asked when the question has an answer, not when the vault is full; and the run says which questions it left alone.** The 2026-09-08 premise behind `hasVaultGear` (C-6, §5) was that with nothing in the vault there is nothing to catalyse or upgrade that is not already yours. It is wrong about the week a player is actually in, and the owner's first refresh after claiming his vault reward is the proof (§9): he held the Venom-Cursed Lynx's Spaulders, a Catalyst candidate his morning's `thisWeek` document had put in his best set, and the Lightgrasp Worldroot he had just crested to 315, two steps short of 321. Those two moves were his week, and the companion asked about neither, because the vault was empty.
  (a) **Each what-if is gated on its own question.** `catalyzed` when QE Live made at least one Catalyst clone out of what the character holds; `maxed` when at least one held item came back above the level the `asOffered` import valued it at; `thisWeek` when either, because it is the two together. `asOffered` is ungated and always asked. A vault section with gear in it, and `--force`, still waive all three outright - the gate could only agree, and a pool read to hear it agree is a read for nothing.
  (b) **The fork decides, because only the fork knows.** Catalyst eligibility is a season of slot and source rules in his `Item.canBeCatalyzed`; an upgrade cap is his `CONSTANTS.itemLevelCaps`. Lootpath restates neither and never will. So the gate is settled INSIDE the run, after that pass's import, by reading the same Top Gear cards C-8 and C-10 already read and counting them - clones for one half, and "higher than the base pass said" for the other, keyed by item ID so that a bonus list his engine may rewrite cannot make an upgrade look like a different item. **What a skipped pass saves is its Top Gear documents, not its import**, and that is the honest price: the alternative was a table of season rules in this repo, which the healer-value rule and the evidence rule both forbid.
  (c) **It fails open.** A pool that could not be read, a base pass that was never read, or a gate kind this build does not know all ask the pass anyway and say so. The defect being fixed here is a question skipped for want of evidence; it must not come back in through the guard.
  (d) **A run that asked fewer questions says so once, in one sentence, written in one place.** `lib/config.js` `scenarioNote` composes it - the scenarios in a reader's words, the distinct reasons said once each - and the same string goes into the log, into `Data/CompanionStatus.lua`'s `message` (the strip's tooltip) and into `Data/QEVerdict.lua`'s `scenarioNote` (the Vault tab). The pass-by-pass lines in the log keep the four contract names, because that is the grep; the sentence does not, because it is read on the Vault tab by a person who never chose those words.
  (e) **The Vault tab's plan footnote says it when, and only when, the plan on screen is not the plan on the label.** `Roads.Plan` already fell back to the first stored scenario and already reported that it had (`fellBack`); the tab drew the fallback without a word, which is what the owner saw. The note becomes the footnote in exactly that case, after the charge footnote rather than instead of it. With the highlighted scenario's own document stored, the note is about questions that sentence never claimed to answer and is not said there.
  (f) **The capture was NOT extended, and the issue's parenthetical is refuted.** It asked whether `Modules/Inventory.lua` records `upgradeLevel`/`upgradeMax`, and to extend the capture if not. It does not - it carries `bonusIDs` and no upgrade fields - and it cannot: the only documented read of them is `C_ItemUpgrade.GetItemUpgradeItemInfo`, which answers only after `SetItemUpgradeFromLocation` in an OPEN upgrade vendor window (`.luals/.../ItemUpgradeDocumentation.lua`). That is a vendor-side capture the owner has to stand at a vendor to run, it is WKE-574's (M3-17) subject and its recorded exception, and building the gate on it would have left this defect unfixed until he next visited one. The fork's own numbers need no vendor and no capture.
- **2026-09-14 (M3-16, WKE-557) - the vault capture is the SECOND capture that is not purely a read, and it asks Blizzard's own question.** Decided by the owner 2026-09-14 evening, built the same night. **The measurement is in §9:** over sixteen of the owner's own vault snapshots, every one taken after the week's first progress says `HasAvailableRewards()` true and `CanClaimRewards()` false while `GetActivities()` lists this period's progress with `rewards = {}` on every activity - last week's unclaimed rewards are simply gone from the list, `rewardLinks` is empty, the companion writes "no generated Great Vault reward ... the profile has no vault section", and the Vault tab has nothing to rank. A full client restart restores them; a `/reload` does not. Five things were decided.
  (a) **`OnUIInteract` is allowed, and `CloseInteraction` is its other half.** `C_WeeklyRewards.OnUIInteract()` tells the server the player is interacting with the vault, and the server answers with the reward list; Blizzard's own `WeeklyRewardsMixin:OnShow` calls it on every open and `OnHide` calls `C_WeeklyRewards.CloseInteraction()` on every close (`Blizzard_WeeklyRewards.lua` lines 69-91 under `.luals/`, read 2026-09-14). It changes nothing about the character, its items or its money. This is the second recorded exception to CLAUDE.md's "captures only read", after the journal's view state (2026-09-06); `ClaimReward` and `SelectReward` are never called and are not in `VAULT_FUNCTION_NAMES`. `CloseInteraction` runs on **every** path out of the wait - the update, the timeout, and a client that errors on `OnUIInteract` itself - because an interaction the server is never told ended is the one thing this capture could leave behind. The alternative the owner turned down was human-required: a full client restart before every refresh that needs the vault. Opening the Great Vault window first, which is what the SimulationCraft addon tells its users to do, is not available to him at all (that window has not opened on this client since 2026-09-08, cause still unknown, §11).
  (b) **The interaction happens only in the one state that the measurement describes.** `HasAvailableRewards()` true and not one activity carrying a reward. With rewards already in the list, or with none waiting, the capture reads and stops, and the snapshot says which of the two it was in words (`interact.attempted = false` with a `reason`). So a capture run in any other week is exactly the read it was before.
  (c) **The snapshot keeps both reads and merges nothing.** `activities` / `rewardLinks` / `exampleLinks` at the top level stay what they have always been - the read taken with no interaction, the "before" - and `interact.after` holds the same three lists read again once `WEEKLY_REWARDS_UPDATE` fired. `interact` also records `updateFired`, `timedOut`, `waitedMs`, and the raw probes of `OnUIInteract` and `CloseInteraction`. Nothing is copied between the two, so a transcript still says exactly what the client said at each moment; **the readers choose**, in one function each: `VaultPanel.SnapshotRewardLinks` in the addon and `snapshotRewardLinks` in `tools/companion/lib/simc-profile.js`, both "the after list when there is one, the before list otherwise". That keeps every snapshot written before this issue - and every snapshot whose wait timed out - readable with no special case.
  (d) **The bound is five seconds and it is a record, not an assumption.** Nothing has measured how fast the server answers; `ns.VAULT_INTERACT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 5` is the owner's "a few seconds", and the snapshot writes down whether the update fired and how long it took so the first real refresh says what the number should be. A wait that times out is said out loud rather than looking like an empty vault.
  (e) **The refresh waits, and says so.** `capture vault` is asynchronous now, so `/lootpath refresh` (C-3) became a chain - captures, then the vault's wait, then `ReloadUI` - and never reloads mid-wait, because the reload would throw away the snapshot the refresh exists to take. `Companion.Refresh(onDone)` returns `{ ok = true, pending = true }` while it waits, the way `ns.RunCapture` does. While the wait lasts the status strip's companion clause reads `waiting for the vault` - it is the live answer to "what is happening", where what the companion did on its last run is a stale one - and the chat frame says the same thing in a sentence. It clears on the reload and on the timeout.
  **A premise of the issue was wrong and the right thing was built instead.** The issue asked `Modules/Vault.lua` `Options()` to "read the `after` list when present, `before` otherwise". `Options()` never reads a snapshot: it is the live `C_WeeklyRewards` adapter, and after the capture's interaction the live client carries the rewards, so it needed no change. The snapshot's two readers are `VaultPanel.NameFromCaptures` (the name of a reward the client has not loaded) and the companion's `vaultRows` (the vault section of the SimC profile) - the second being the one that writes the warning the issue was filed on - and both were changed. What the issue's item 3 asked the tab to say was built where the tab actually says it: an empty reward list with `HasAvailableRewards()` **true** printed "the vault has not generated this week's rewards yet", which is the one thing that is not true in that state, and now reads `The client says vault rewards are waiting but did not answer with them. Run /lootpath refresh: it asks for them the way the Great Vault window does.`

**2026-09-14 (night), the floor stays, and the addon says when it is behind (owner):** asked whether the finished product must have players refreshing and reloading, the answer given was that two reloads per refresh is the floor of this architecture (2026-09-07), that the only ways below it are an engine inside the addon or a screen-pixel export for the first half, and that the floor is tolerable if the plan is fresh at login, the stale state is honest from the bags alone (576), and the refresh is rare. The owner kept the floor and asked that a player who opens the vault never has to know a reload is needed. WKE-578 (R-6): the nudge, the wait line, and fresh-at-login in the words.

- **2026-09-15 (R-3c, WKE-580) - a pick moves SLOT as well as key, and the one road nothing rated is the one road `maxed` exists to rate.** The owner did the next thing the plan said - crested the Lightgrasp Worldroot the plan picked out of his bags, to 321, and put it on - and the tooltip over it read `Skip this one, the plan uses your Worldroot.` / `Upgrade · Lightgrasp Worldroot (321) · no rating · crest type and cost not read · you hold 399 Adventurer Mistcrest, ...`. The plan told him to skip the staff in favour of itself. Three things follow, and two of the issue's own rules were narrowed against the files.

  (a) **The placement is not part of the identity.** `ns.Roads.IsArrivedPick` no longer stops at equipped items and no longer knows only vault and Catalyst picks: a held item whose ITEM ID is the slot's plan pick of any kind, whose key is not one the pick's road already carries, and whose level is not below the level the plan picked it at, is that pick moved on - in the bags, in the bank, or on the character. The sentence says which step the player has taken: `You've put this on. Refresh to rate it at 298.`, `You've crested this and put it on. Refresh to rate it at 308.`, or, still in the bags, `This is the Preyhunter cloak the plan wanted, crested to 308. Put it on; refresh to rate it.` The level is always the held item's own, off the link being hovered. The pick's row says where the piece has got to in R-3b's badge slot: `now worn`, `now crested`, and `claimed · now worn` / `claimed · now crested` for a vault reward. The sentence is asked even when the hovered item HAS a road of its own, because every worn piece has an Upgrade road and that is exactly what the owner's block opened on; the one road that still speaks for itself is a set-group road carrying the item's own key, because then the document rates this very copy.

  **Narrowed, on the week's own files: a WORN copy of a vault reward or a Catalyst clone is still not the pick.** The issue asked for `You've put this on.` there too. Nothing the client says can support it: the vault offered that staff at **305** while the owner wore **308**, so the level cannot tell the copies apart, and cresting REPLACES the upgrade bonus ID rather than adding to it, so the bonus IDs cannot either. The claimed-but-uncrested reward needs no rule - it carries the pick's own key and the road already speaks for it. R-3b's refusal therefore stands exactly where the evidence stops and no further. Proven: removed, the older assertion that the worn 308 is not the vault pick goes red.

  **Narrowed, on the voice rule: "claimed" stays the vault's word.** The issue asked for `claimed · now worn` on the bag pick's road. A piece that came out of a dungeon was never claimed from anything, so a non-vault pick's badge is the state alone. This is the owner's to overrule; it is a one-string change.

  (b) **The Upgrade road reads the `maxed` document.** That scenario is QE Live's own run with every owned item raised to its cap (C-6), which is the question "is upgrading this worth it", and the road used to be `no rating` with `arrivesAt` set to the level the piece is ALREADY at. The join is `ns.QEImport.Coverage` by KEY, which is exact: the upgrade boxes move an item's `level` without touching a bonus ID, so the piece keeps its key under `maxed` and only the level moves. The road carries that document's own word - `in your best set`, or the best alternative's percent - at the level it projected, and it sits with the other whole-set verdicts; `Roads.IsForward` then gates its `do:` and its glow as it does for every other rated road. **No road at all when the projected level is the level already worn**: nothing to crest is not a road that leads where the reader is standing. When the run never asked, the road carries C-12's `scenarioNote` as its reason instead of a bare `no rating`. Nothing here projects a level, and the crest cost is still the vendor window's (WKE-574). It is the only row on the panel whose plan is not its group's, so it names its own (`rated under everything upgraded`) and the group header goes on naming the plan the screen follows.

  (c) **The crest counts leave the tooltip.** `you hold 399 Adventurer Mistcrest, ...` is the Upgrade Map row's business, beside the cost it would pay; on the tooltip it was the longest clause on the block and answered a question nobody asked there. It is marked the way the cost clause is marked (`step(..., cost)`), so `row.costText` keeps it and `row.tooltipFactsText` drops it - one flag, not a second list, so a clause cannot be in one and not the other. R-2a's rule, one clause later.

  (d) **Part 4 needed no work.** R-6's nudge did fire on that screen; the owner's word is in §9.

- **2026-09-14 (R-3b, WKE-576) - the ITEM ID decides "this is the pick", never the key; a road takes its name from whatever can name it; and a plan that is behind the bags says so.** The owner did exactly what the plan said - claimed the vault's Lightgrasp Worldroot and put crests into it - and the tooltip over the result read `Skip this one, the plan uses the vault weapon.` Three defects and one rule, all of them the same root: a document is about keys, and claiming an item changes its key.
  (a) **The identity rule.** A key is an item ID plus its sorted bonus IDs, which is precisely what claiming and cresting a vault reward changes; the item ID is what survives both. So `ns.Roads.IsArrivedPick(held, pick)` compares item IDs and nothing else - against the pick's own item and against what it BECOMES, because a Catalyst clone carries the tier item ID rather than the one that went in. It is deliberately narrow, and it refuses two things that would otherwise be false positives on the very screen that filed it: a piece you are WEARING (the owner's old 308 Worldroot has the same item ID, and the slot already has its own road for what is on the character - a guard that was proven red and took four older tests with it), and an item whose key the pick's road already carries (the vault reward itself, or the bag piece a Catalyst road converts, both of which the road already speaks for). What it produces is a sentence, not a rating: `This is the vault Worldroot the plan wanted. Refresh to rate it at 315.`, with the level off the item's own link, over an honesty phrase that still reads `not rated · new since the last refresh`. **Nothing is estimated** - the product rule holds, and the line says what the item is, not what it is worth.
  (b) **The same evidence moves the vault road.** The claim is in no capture until the next refresh, so the only thing that knows about it is the item in the bags. `Roads.MarkArrived` reads the bags and nothing else: the pick's badge becomes `claimed · in your bags` instead of `open now`, its verb becomes Refresh and its step becomes `do: refresh to rate it`, because `do: take it · Show in vault` on something already taken is the same wrong sentence in three more places. After the refresh the vault section is empty, the road is not built at all, and this says nothing because there is nothing to say.
  (c) **Why the names never arrived, eight hours and several reloads after R-2a asked for them.** A QE Live item entry carries an item ID, a key and a level and **no name field at all**, so every road built from a document is nameless; R-2a's `RequestNames` asked the client and rebuilt when the answer landed, and the rebuild re-read the same nameless source record, so the name reached the drawn row and never the road. Two fixes, neither of them a guess: `ns.LinkName` (moved beside the link parser, and `ns.Vault.LinkName` IS it now) gives a vault road its name off the reward's own hyperlink with no request at all, and `ns.RoadsCache.FillNames` is the look at what the client has already answered. It runs before `RequestNames` and, when it fills anything, the map is built once more over the now-named roads - because a sentence is written inside the build, and a name that lands after it reaches the row but not the sentence. That is why the owner read `the plan uses the vault weapon` and not `the plan uses the vault Worldroot`.
  (d) **A stale plan names its own remedy (the small rule).** `rated 8 hours ago` beside `not rated · new since the last refresh` is the addon knowing the document is behind the world. `Roads.StaleBags` is that fact per slot - a held piece no road of the slot rates whose tail is the one a refresh cures - and where it is true the tooltip's header and the Upgrade Map's slot header both end `/lootpath refresh`. On the Upgrade Map it is text in the header rather than a button, because a slot header's one other column is the drop count; the rows below carry the Refresh VERB, which is where principle 9's button belongs, and it is `ns.Companion.Refresh` and nothing else - the same captures, the same two reloads, the same combat refusal.

- **2026-09-15 (R-2b, WKE-575) - a mark is drawn at the size it will be seen at, and a widget that is never asked says so itself.** R-2a's diagnosis ran end to end on the owner's client (§9) and every line of it said yes: `adapter: baganator`, the widget in the `top_right` corner, `map: 221 keys, 98 of them marked`, the helmet `in the map` and `glow yes`, with Baganator's own call path read in the installed 823 and cleared. The slot on screen still had nothing in it. Two things follow, and each is a rule rather than a patch.
  (a) **The picture was borrowed from a surface that had room for it.** R-2 drew `evergreen-weeklyrewards-reward-selected` - the Great Vault's own selection glow, which is what the Vault tab puts on the picked cell (M5-4) - into a 15-point frame with `SetAllPoints`, so the bag and the Vault tab would share one picture. The owner measured that atlas on his client with `C_Texture.GetAtlasInfo`: **214 x 121**, a wide, soft-edged banner made to sit BEHIND a cell, squeezed to a seventh of its width and an eighth of its height. Two surfaces sharing a picture was R-2's wish, not a rule, and a picture that cannot be seen shares nothing. **No second atlas is guessed at in its place** - every candidate would be another name whose size, shape and alpha nobody here has read, and this one file has now cost the owner two nights of exactly that. The mark is drawn by the addon: Blizzard's flat `WHITE8X8`, the same 1-pixel texture the Vault tab's own fallback border tints, in two layers - a near-black square filling the frame, which is the one-point edge that keeps the mark's shape over pale icon art, and the accent square inset one point inside it, fully opaque. The accent is `ItemLine.TONE.better`'s `#FFDF14`, this addon's one definition of its gold and the tone the Vault tab edges its own pick with, so the two surfaces still mean the same thing in the same colour - at a size each of them can be seen at. The conversion from that hex to a tint became `ItemLine.RGB`, beside the tones, because three files were about to do it three times; `VaultPanel`'s `toneRGB` delegates to it and nothing it draws changed.
  (b) **A widget that is never called is a different fault from a widget that draws nothing, and only a count tells them apart.** `/lootpath glow` could say the widget was registered, which corner Baganator puts it in, and what the map answers for one link - and still not say whether Baganator ever CALLED it. That was the one number two nights could not produce, and no screenshot can. `Adapter.calls`, `Adapter.lastLink` and `Adapter.lastAnswer` are session-lived on the adapter, incremented inside `OnUpdate`, and the command reads `Baganator asked the widget N times this session, last answer: yes for <name>` - no figure of that is written here, because none has been measured yet; the owner's next paste is the first one. At zero it says, in those words, that if the bags have been opened then Baganator never called it and **the cause is upstream of the picture** - which sends the next session to the registration, not to another texture. The `glow` capture records the same three fields, so a transcript carries it too. The link kept for the sentence is `ns.Safe`'s copy, never Baganator's raw field, and the name in it is read out of the link's own `|h[...]|h` rather than from the client, because a diagnosis line that needed a load would be blank exactly when the reader most wants it.
  (c) **The answer is forced to a plain boolean.** `ItemButton.lua:140` branches on `show == nil` to QUEUE a slot rather than hide it, so anything but `true` or `false` out of the callback puts a slot in a queue it does not belong in. `ns.Glow.WantsLink` already answers a boolean; `== true` in the adapter is what keeps that true of the callback whatever it grows into, and a guard asserts both exact values - `true` for the owner's best-set Lynx Spaulders and `false` for the Hearthstone his own `glow` capture recorded at bag 0 slot 1.

- **2026-09-14 (R-2a, WKE-571) - the plan takes a position on everything you own; a tooltip carries no clause whose referent is on another screen; and a bag mark says why it is not there.** R-2's first real screens (§9) showed four things at once, and each is fixed at the rule rather than at the line.
  (a) **Every piece you HOLD gets a sentence, rated or not.** `Roads.ItemSentence` answered only for a road in the set group, so the Miststalker's Spaulders - in the bags, beyond the rating's item limit, no road of its own - opened on `not rated` and nothing else, which is exactly what principle 16 forbids. The plan HAS a position on everything you own: use it, catalyst it, or skip it. `Roads.ForItemIn` now sets `answer.held` off the same inventory records the honesty phrase is read off, and the sentence has two ways in: the set group, or your bags. The Miststalker's reads `Skip this one, the plan uses your Lynx shoulders.` and keeps the phrase as its road line underneath. What stays sentence-less is unchanged and is the right boundary: a road to something you do NOT have - a journal drop, a crafted row, a delve row - where "Skip this one" would read as "skip the dungeon". Held means the inventory scan's records, which is equipped, bags AND bank; a piece in the bank is a piece you own.
  (b) **A tooltip carries no clause whose referent is on another screen.** Four left it, and the Upgrade Map row keeps all four, because on the panel every referent is visible. The crest COST clause (`crest type and cost not readable`) is 568's vendor-window gate: on the row it answers "what will this cost me", beside the crest counts; on a tooltip nobody asked, nothing is beside it, and it can never become readable. The RIVAL clause (`the same charge as the vault Spaulders road`) is principle 6's own rule turned on itself - "a reference is never to a road that is not on the same screen", and a tooltip about one item is never that screen. A NAMELESS road said `item 244572 (331)`; an ID is not a name, so the tooltip says what kind of thing it is (`a crafted piece (331)`) and the cache now asks the client for the name once per item ID and rebuilds when it lands. And a NO-RATING road took one of three lines under a best-set pick: on the tooltip "other roads" are rated roads only, forward ones first. Principle 10's three is a cap, not a quota. **`crest type and cost not read` - the "not read YET" clause - was deliberately left on**: it can change, where "not readable" cannot, and the owner named only the permanent one.
  (c) **`Why this?` says where to go.** On the Upgrade Map row it opens the row; on a tooltip it cannot be clicked and principle 9's rule is that a verb only exists where it goes somewhere. Principles 10 and 11 both write the line as an always, so it keeps its place and names its destination: `Why this? · /lootpath map`, and `/lootpath map` opens the window on the Upgrade Map. It shows rather than toggles - a reader who typed it off a tooltip asked to see the tab, never to close a window.
  (d) **A missing mark explains itself, and the explanation is a command rather than a screenshot.** Nothing on the owner's screen told "the adapter never installed" from "the bag addon never draws the widget" from "the lookup answers false", and each is a different fix. `/lootpath glow` prints all three plus one item's whole lookup - the key the link makes, whether the map has it, what the mark answers, and whether the item's own road is one the plan points at - and takes a shift-clicked link or falls back to the first bag slot. `/lootpath capture glow` records the same four facts for every bag slot, with the map's own key list beside them, so a transcript settles a key mismatch instead of a hover doing it one item at a time.
  (e) **The corner was wrong, and it was read rather than reasoned.** Baganator's `Core/Config.lua:46` ships `icon_top_left_corner_array` as `{"junk", "item_level"}`, and `ItemViewCommon/ItemButton.lua:174-177` walks one corner's widgets in array order and **breaks on the first that returns true** - only one widget is ever shown per corner. `API/Main.lua:185-196` inserts a `defaultPosition` at the priority INDEX, so R-2's `{ corner = "top_left", priority = 1 }` would have put Lootpath first in top-left and taken the item level off every slot the plan marks. The same rule that kept Lootpath out of Baganator's upgrade-plugin slot applies to a corner: a mark that costs the reader something he already had is not the mark he asked for. `top_right` ships empty. **Two of the issue's three candidate causes are refuted by that source, not by a screen:** `AutoInsert` is gated per widget id on `icon_corners_auto_insert_applied`, so a widget new to a saved profile IS inserted into an existing corner array; and registering late is handled, because `RegisterCornerWidget` fires `PluginsUpdated`, which rebuilds the per-corner callbacks and asks for an item-widget refresh. **Which cause it actually was is still open and is the owner's next step** (§11): the fix here removes one, and the command names whichever remains.
  (f) **`hour(s)` was a format string on a screen.** `UI.AgeText` built `second(s)` / `minute(s)` / `hour(s)` / `day(s)`; the voice rule is that it sounds like a person. `UI.Plural` writes English's own plural once and every surface that prints an age reads it. None of the four bands can actually produce a 1 - the minute band runs to 90 minutes and each band rounds into the next at 1.5 - so the singular is asserted on `UI.Plural` itself rather than through a moment that cannot happen.

- **2026-09-15 (V-2, WKE-573) - the strip drops the content type, and what that buys is measured, not assumed.** Three things were settled while building it. **(a) The two words moved rather than went.** `UI.VerdictNoteText` now names the export as well as the content type - "Showing the Dungeon Top Gear export (...)" - and that sentence is the strip's first tooltip line, so hovering the strip still answers what the line used to say. A separate tooltip line was rejected: the content type was already in that sentence and a second one would have said it twice. The Import dialog shows the same sentence and gains the export's name with it, which is the same fact in the same words. **(b) The width is asserted in CHARACTERS, against the owner's own cut line, and the test says so.** The stub has no pixels (9) and this repo holds no screenshot a headless test can measure, so no font metric was added and none was invented. The budget is the 104 characters 11 recorded as visible on his 2026-09-14 screen at the window's default width. The font is proportional, so 104 characters is a proxy and a LOWER bound on the real width - which is what makes it safe to assert against rather than a pixel claim, and the test's comment states the whole of that. Measured on the owner's own state (Dungeon export, companion-written, five days old): **122 characters before, 103 after**; the `never seen` clause comes to 97. **(c) The FAILED clause still does not fit, and this is said out loud rather than left to be found.** `companion: FAILED at qe live (07:00) - see companion.log` brings the line to **132 characters**, and dropping one fact cannot close a 28-character gap. A test asserts that it is over the budget and that the companion's whole sentence is in the tooltip, which is where that reader is sent. Shortening the clause, or wrapping the strip, stays available and is not taken without the owner. **(d) The glow command samples gear.** `/lootpath glow` with no link took bag 0 slot 1 and reported on the Hearthstone (the owner's run, 2026-09-14 night). It now walks bags 0..4 for the first slot whose `ns.ItemData.Instant(link).slot` is non-nil - the same static-data slot every other surface uses - and names what it sampled. `C_Item.GetItemInventoryTypeByID`, which the issue named, was NOT added: `GetItemInfoInstant` is already in `ItemData`'s declared list and already answers the question, so no new client call enters the addon for a diagnostic. With no gear in the bags it says `item: no equippable gear in your bags to sample` and still prints the shift-click instruction. **(e) The plurals (the issue's part 3) were already done** by R-2a (PR #99, `34ea350`): `UI.Plural` exists and the strip, `Tooltip.HeaderText` and `Companion.StatusText` all read ages through `UI.AgeText`. Verified, not rebuilt. The `(s)` forms that remain in the addon are other nouns on other surfaces (items, drops, rewards, rows) and are outside this issue.
**2026-09-14 (evening), five decisions by the owner, each with the options as presented:** (1) **The 30-item rating limit** (558): (a) a patron tier, (b) live within 30, (c) a second pass over the leftovers. **Chosen (c)**, WKE-572: every pass is its own document, nothing merged, no number combined; the phrase `beyond the rating's item limit` retires once every held item is in some pass. (2) **The vault leaving the profile** (557): (a) the capture calls Blizzard's own `C_WeeklyRewards.OnUIInteract()` and waits for the update, (b) keep the fresh-login habit, (c) the strip tells the player to log in again. **Chosen (a)**: the second recorded exception to "captures only read" after the journal's view state (2026-09-06); it marks the vault as looked at and claims nothing. (3) **The status strip**: drop "Dungeon Top Gear" or wrap to two lines. **Chosen: drop it**, WKE-573; the content type lives in the tabs' own controls and the strip's tooltip. (4) **Crest costs** (568 q2): (a) a capture places an owned item in the open vendor window and reads `GetItemUpgradeItemInfo`, (b) drop cost clauses everywhere. **Chosen (a)**: the third exception, WKE-574; `UpgradeItem` is never called and a test asserts it; vault items and drops stay unreadable. (5) **The companion starting with Windows** (C-9): run `install-startup.ps1` once and reboot, or keep starting it by hand. **Chosen: run it.** Recommended and chosen were the same five; the reasoning offered for each is in the session of 2026-09-14 evening and, in one line each, above.

- **2026-09-14 (R-3a, WKE-570) - an imperative belongs to a road the plan is going forward on, and to no other.** The owner read `do: equip it` on a Head row rated 0.95% behind the helm he was wearing. Three things were decided in fixing it. **(a) One gate, asked twice.** Principle 12 (the glow: in your best set, or a positive percent, under the highlighted plan) and principle 16 (the imperative is bounded to the road's own next step, and a road rated behind what you wear has no next step) are the same test, so they are one function - `Roads.IsForward` - which R-2's glow will call rather than write again. **(b) The gate runs over the finished slot, not inside the builders.** The defect was not one bad string: `do: equip it`, `do: raid it`, `do: run the key`, `do: take one vault reward` and `do: get the spark` were each written where its road is built, and any one of them could have been fixed while the next escaped. `Roads.GateImperatives` walks all three groups after every builder has run and finds an imperative by its `do: ` prefix, so a phrasing added later is gated without being enumerated. **(c) What a gated row says instead is the plan's own words, and only where they are true.** `keep what you've got on` is the slot sentence's own clause, and it is right only when the slot's plan pick IS the piece worn. On the Shoulder slot, whose plan is to Catalyst a bag item, it would be a lie - so those rows simply end after their facts, and the badge's `referent` ("taking the vault weapon instead") is what says where the plan went. Where the phrase does apply it applies to EVERY rated-behind road of the slot, including a Delves row that never had an imperative, because two rows carrying the same verdict must not end differently. A road nothing rated gains no phrase either way: not knowing is not a verdict. The Keep row keeps `nothing to do`; it is not an imperative. **What this costs, named rather than hidden:** three rows on the owner's own week lose a `do:` that was not wrong in itself - the vault Spaulders' `do: take one vault reward` and two Mythic+ rows rated `not in your best set`, one of which is `+0.38%` once crested. The vault still hands over one reward and the plan sentence above still says to take it; a row whose own rating points behind is not the place to say so.

- **2026-09-14 (R-4, WKE-565) - Delves and Crafted are cards by run, roads by slot, and they name no key level.** The Upgrade Finder documents the companion writes carry 33 delve rows and 18 crafted ones each; the by-run view had nowhere to put them and the by-slot view already does, through R-3. Four things were decided while building it:
  (a) **The by-slot half is `ns.Roads`', and is not drawn twice.** R-1 gave the road model a craft and a delve road per slot and R-3 renders them under `Other rated sources`; this build pointed that walk at `ns.UFImport.SourceRows` (one place where these rows are found, ordered and deduplicated) and added the one thing only the export knew - the stats line the crafted rating assumed - rather than adding a second list of the same rows under the candidates. The first cut of this PR did add that second list; `main` moved under it and the owner returned it for reconciliation.
  (b) **No key level on these rows.** Measured over the committed run: the five dungeon documents (+2 through +10) carry all 51 of these rows with identical `upgradePercent`, and the Raid document differs on 33 of them - so what values a crafted item is the content type, never the Mythic+ key. A grey `(at +2)` would tell the reader a dependency his own files deny. The document is still carried on the row, and the note comes back the moment two stored documents really disagree.
  (c) **A card's denominator says "ranked items", never "drops".** A run's denominator is every drop the Adventure Guide lists for it; a Crafting or Delves card has no loot table behind it, only the rows he ranked, and `13 of 18 ranked items are upgrades` is the honest wording of that. For the same reason `counts.drops` and `counts.rated` stay the walk's own figures and the cards are counted separately.
  (d) **They carry no difficulty and are shown under every filter**, and the difficulty dropdown gains no entry for them: a difficulty is a property of a walked run, and inventing "Crafting (18)" as a filter row would invent one. Said once on screen (`Panel.EXPORT_NOTE`) rather than on every row.
  Also decided by what could not be read: a crafted row says `spark and materials not read` and, where the export's settings name them, `the rating assumes Crit / Haste`; a delve row says `key and Bountiful state not read` - the issue's suggested wording was `Bountiful delve - not read`, and "Bountiful" is not something any committed file or client call confirms about these rows, so the row names what is unread instead of asserting which delve it came from.

- **2026-09-13 (T-1, WKE-560) - the headless stub models one widget's mixin chain per widget, and its own helpers live off Blizzard's namespace.** The rule PR #72 stated for dropdowns is now the rule for every kind and every template the stub recognises, and the audit that applied it found the fix had been only the visible half:
  (a) **The leak was in `newRegion`, not in `attachTemplate`.** Every widget - `Frame`, `Button`, `EditBox`, `ScrollFrame`, `FontString`, `Texture` alike - was built from one constructor that handed out the union of `Region`, `FontString` and `Texture`, and `newFrame` added `ScrollFrame`'s five scroll setters on top. So a bare `Frame` answered `SetText`, `SetTextColor`, `SetJustifyH`, `SetTexture`, `SetAtlas`, `SetTexCoord` and `SetScrollChild`; a `FontString` answered `SetAtlas`; a `Texture` answered `SetText` and `SetWordWrap`. Nineteen names in all. The templates were the symptom; the constructor was the cause.
  (b) **Subtracting a superset is still a superset.** PR #72 gave `SetDefaultText` / `GetDefaultText` / `SetSelectionText` to the `DropdownButton` KIND and then set them to `nil` inside `attachTemplate` for anything that was not `WowStyle1DropdownTemplate`. `attachTemplate` returns immediately when the template is not a string, so `CreateFrame("DropdownButton", nil, parent)` never reached the subtraction and answered `SetDefaultText` headlessly - the intrinsic does not have it (`Core/Widget/Intrinsic/DropdownButton.lua:9` is `DropdownButton : DropdownButtonMixin, Button`; `SetDefaultText` is `MenuTemplates.lua:582`, `DropdownSelectionTextMixin`'s). **A template is now added to and never subtracted from.**
  (c) **A gap is safe; a superset is not.** The stub deliberately models less than the client where no panel needs more - `CheckButton:SetChecked`, `DropdownSelectionTextMixin:SetSelectionText` and `SetTooltip`, `DropdownButtonMixin:Pick`, `ScriptRegion:SetScript` on a `FontString`. A missing method turns a test red where the client would have worked, which costs a session; an extra one turns a test green where the client breaks, which cost the owner a crash on screen.
  (d) **Stub-only affordances moved to `widget.stub`.** `Button:Enter` / `:Leave`, `dropdown:Pick(index)` / `:SelectedIndex()` / `:SelectByText()`, `scrollBox:Acquire()` / `:ReleaseAll()` and `GameTooltip:Text()` were all names the client does not have, and `Pick` was worse than absent - `DropdownButtonMixin:Pick` is real (`DropdownButton.lua:341`) and takes a menu description and an input context, not an index, so the stub was occupying a real slot with a different contract. All eight are now `widget.stub:X()`.
  (e) **One gap the audit exposed was real and is now modelled.** `DropdownButton` inherits `Button`, and `UpgradeMapPanel` calls `SetText` on the difficulty control (real twice over: `Button.lua:199` and `DropdownTextMixin:SetText`, `MenuTemplates.lua:522`). Taking the union apart turned 31 tests red at `UpgradeMapPanel.lua:1876` in one run, which is exactly the signal the audit was for; the Button surface is now given to `DropdownButton` because a `DropdownButton` is one.
  (f) **A released scroll-box row stops answering for its old data.** The real view sets `frame.GetElementData` on acquire (`ScrollBoxListView.lua:90`) and clears it on release (`:114`); the stub set it and never cleared it.
  (g) **Two panel comments were corrected against what the annotations actually say.** `VaultPanel.lua` claimed "Blizzard's generated annotations do not record that inheritance" for `WowStyle1DropdownTemplate` -> `DropdownSelectionTextMixin`. They do: `Blizzard_Menu/Mainline/MenuTemplates.xml:3` declares `mixin="WowStyle1DropdownMixin"` and `MenuTemplates.lua:753` composes it. What the GENERATED API documentation omits is FrameXML's templates, which is a different claim. The `pcall` stays and now says which client it is for.
- **2026-09-13 (C-9, WKE-559) - the companion writes two more files, and starts with Windows.** Three things were settled building it.
  (a) **The addon-folder exception is now three files, and the rule that made it one still holds.** The 2026-09-07 decision let the companion write `Data/QEVerdict.lua` into the addon folder because an addon can read no file it did not ship with. `Data/companion.log` and `Data/CompanionStatus.lua` join it under exactly the same terms: written by the same program on the owner's own machine, into that same folder and nowhere else, data and never code, the status chunk through the verdict writer's own escaper, and the addon still loads nothing but its own Lua at load time. Nothing else was granted: no network, no second directory, no file the addon writes.
  (b) **"Nothing happened" and "nothing to do" are different states, and the status file is what tells them apart.** A failed run writes NO verdict - that is C-1's rule and it is right, because a half-answer is worse than yesterday's - so from inside the game a companion that crashed looked exactly like a companion with nothing to do: an export a few hours old, either way (§11, 2026-09-09). `skipped` is therefore its own state beside `idle`, `running` and `failed`, and the strip says `profile unchanged, no run (23:06)` rather than dressing it as a successful run. The chunk is written at every stage change, not at the end, so a run in progress says so instead of leaving the last run's words up for the minute QE Live takes.
  (c) **The login-time start is a Task Scheduler task for the current user, and "never two watchers" stops being a rule people remember.** `install-startup.ps1` registers an at-logon task running `start-companion.ps1` (the fork's dev server if nothing answers its port, then `node companion.js --watch`); interactive, no stored password, idempotent, and it never stops a watcher the owner started himself. Because nobody can now know by hand whether one is already running, the watcher takes `<stateDir>/watch.lock` and a second one exits 7 naming the pid that holds it - and a lock whose process is gone is taken over rather than honoured, so a machine that lost power mid-run never needs a file deleted by hand. **The reboot itself is the owner's step and nothing here claims it was taken.**
- **2026-09-12 (R-3, WKE-564) - a slot's rows ARE its roads, the plan leads both surfaces, and Explain is counted over what is drawn.** Four things were settled while building surface 2, each of which the next surface would otherwise re-decide.
  (a) **Roads REPLACE the candidate list, and only when a plan is stored.** `Panel.Model` builds `section.roadGroups` when the caller hands over `opts.scenarios` and not otherwise, because the `set` group is the highlighted plan's own answer for the slot and there is no such group without one. `Panel.Gather` always hands them over, so in the client the roads are always there; a headless caller that asks nothing about plans gets exactly the list M5-3 shipped. **What this costs is named rather than hidden:** M3-3 printed a Top Gear coverage sentence on a journal drop whose exact key the top set carried, and a road for an item you own is in `set` while a drop is in `item` or `none`, so that sentence is no longer on a drop row. On the owner's own files the join covers no drop at all (`UpgradeMapPanel.lua`'s own header, measured 2026-09-06: the walk's keyed rows carry one bonus ID where his items carry two to seven), and `counts.covered` still carries the figure.
  (b) **One place writes the words, and the drawn row and the printed line are the same row.** `Panel.RoadRow` is the row; `Panel.RoadLineText` is that row as one line; `Panel.Lines` prints it. A badge, a fact or a "do:" that appeared on screen and not in `/lootpath status` would be two answers to one question, which is the drift M5-3 (§7, 2026-09-09) already refused for the candidate rows.
  (c) **A verb is dropped when it has no destination, in the ROW rather than in the model.** `ns.Roads` gives every journal drop `Show run` and every vault road `Show in vault` because those are the roads' next steps; whether the by-run view has that run and whether the reward has a key is a fact about this surface, so `Panel.RoadRow` clears the verb when the destination resolves to nothing. Principle 9's rule is about what the reader can press, and a dead button is worse than none.
  (d) **"First visible use" is counted over the element list, not over the model.** The Explain sentence for a word appears under the first element that USES it in the expanded slot, which on the shoulder slot is the section's own plan sentence ("Catalyst your Lynx shoulders...") rather than the Catalyst row. A collapsed slot explains nothing, and the match is whole-word, so "no crests here" does not spend the crest sentence before the row that says what is not readable earns it. The one figure a sentence may carry is the client's own charge count.

- **2026-09-12 (R-1, WKE-562) - one road model, three groups, and five things settled while building it.** A road is one way to get an item into a slot, and `Lootpath/Modules/Roads.lua` is the model all three Roads surfaces will render. Five decisions, each of which would otherwise be re-made three times:
  (a) **Three groups, and they are never merged into one ordering.** `set` is what the highlighted plan's own Top Gear document says about the slot, `item` is the Upgrade Finder's percents against what you wear, `none` is everything nothing rated. "In your best set" is a verdict on a whole set and "+3.07%" is a verdict on one item against the worn one; sorting them together would be an ordering of Lootpath's own, which is the same rule as "never computes a healer value" (docs/ROADS-UX.md principle 2). Inside `set` the order is the document's - the pick first, then its alternatives in his order; inside `item` it is the percent he gave, best first; the three groups are a fixed order and are not a ranking.
  (b) **A road reports the BEST set he ranked that carries its item, not the set that changes its slot alone.** This is `ns.QEImport.Coverage`'s own rule ("an item that appears in several alternatives is reported through the best of them") applied to a road, and it is what makes the shoulder board read the way the canvas does. It also corrects the issue's own figure: see §9.
  (c) **A row's figures all come from ONE run.** The arrival percent, the "upgraded 321 at" figure and the "at its cap 334" figure are read from the single document the arrival rating came out of, and the words "upgraded" and "at its cap" are his own `dropType` (`bonus` and `max`) rather than Lootpath's reading of two numbers. A level another key level's document happens to carry is not on the row at all.
  (d) **The plan sentence never names a crest track, and never chooses between two conversions.** Which crest an upgrade takes is not readable from the client (§7 2026-09-11, the engineering pass), so the sentence says "and crest it" and the canvas's "put your Hero crests into it" is not built. When his best set spends more charges than the client says are held, the sentence names the conversions in HIS OWN top-set order up to the count held and the footnote says the rest - "The plan would catalyst the Hide chest too, but you've only got one charge." - without the canvas's "so the shoulders are the one", which would be Lootpath choosing, and without its percentage, which principle 16 keeps out of the sentence.
  (e) **The module reads nothing.** No client call, no database, no `ns.VaultPanel`: which plan is highlighted is resolved by the caller and handed in as `highlightedScenario`, and the difficulty words a drop row needs are handed in as `difficultyLabels`, because the journal walk records only the difficulty ID and asking `GetDifficultyInfo` here would make the model a client reader. That is what lets the whole model be tested over committed files.

- **2026-09-09 (M5-4, WKE-553) - the Vault tab is drawn as Blizzard draws the vault, and every word of it still comes from somewhere.** Four things were decided while building it and are worth not reopening. **(a) The grid is a second VIEW of `Panel.Lines`, never a second answer.** The cell's verdict line, its hover, its level wording and its pick are the strings and the objects the text half already produced - `reward.verdictLines`, `reward.levelText`, `model.best` - so the two cannot disagree about what QE Live said, and `Panel.Lines` did not change by a byte. The drawn rows print `Panel.NoteLines` and `Panel.Lines` begins with the same function, which is what keeps that true as the notes grow. **(b) Blizzard's words are asked for at runtime and never written down.** The row order and the threshold patterns are read off Blizzard's shipped source, but the STRINGS - `RAIDS`, `DUNGEONS`, `WORLD`, `WEEKLY_REWARDS_THRESHOLD_*` - are globals whose values nothing in this repo has a transcript of, so the panel asks `_G` for each and falls back to the labels measured off the owner's own vault screen. The stub defines none of them on purpose: the fallback is the path a test can exercise honestly, and one test defines sentinel values to prove the global wins. The same rule covers the selected atlas, which is validated with `C_Texture.GetAtlasInfo` and has a gold border behind it. **(c) A pick that is not a pick does not glow.** The headline has said `none - nothing in the vault beats your set` since M3-9; a glowing cell under that sentence would be the screen contradicting itself, so the test the headline makes is computed once, carried as `model.headline.closest`, and the closest option gets a grey label instead. **(d) A currency icon is a fact the client handed over, so it is kept.** `Currencies.record` was dropping `iconFileID` while M3-11's call was fetching it; the field is now kept and the whole Catalyst record handed on. Nothing is derived from an icon, and the counts beside the scenario lines are unchanged - the strip names the currencies, it does not add a number.
- **2026-09-09 (M5-3, WKE-552) - the two lists are one data provider, the collapse state is per character, and the walk records the art it was already being told.** Four things were settled while drawing the Upgrade Map. (1) **`Panel.Lines` and `Panel.RunLines` do not become the drawing.** They stay the pure text `/lootpath status` and every render test read, and `Panel.Elements` / `Panel.RunElements` are a SECOND pure function beside them describing what is drawn. Two functions over one model is more code than one; it is also the only arrangement in which a redraw cannot change what the addon says, and every values-free guard written since M3-3 keeps its teeth without being rewritten. (2) **One frame TYPE for four element kinds.** The scroll box's factory takes a frame type or an XML template and pools by it, and this addon ships no XML, so the four kinds cannot have four pools; each element frame therefore builds the widgets of whichever kinds it has been asked to be and hides the rest. The alternative - shipping an XML file for the first time to get four templates - buys nothing a reader can see and costs a build step. (3) **A slot section is open by default and a run card is shut by default.** The slot view answers "what could fill this", and its answer is the rows; the run view answers "where should I go", and its answer is on the card, so 48 runs of a dozen drops each would bury it. Both are remembered in `db.char.upgradeMap`, per character rather than per account, because which slot the owner cares about is a fact about the character. (4) **The walk records `buttonImage1`, `buttonImage2` and `bgImage`, and a card with no art draws no art.** `EJ_GetInstanceInfo` is already in `JournalAdapter.FUNCTION_NAMES` and only reads; keeping more of what it returns is not a new client action (CLAUDE.md's capture rule). The positions were read from Blizzard's own shipped Encounter Journal under `.luals/` rather than from the wiki - `name, _, _, icon = EJ_GetInstanceInfo(id)` at line 2413 fixes the fourth return as the button art, and line 1211's `instanceName, description, bgImage, _, loreImage, buttonImage, dungeonAreaMapID` fixes the three around it. All six committed transcripts predate the recording, so every one of them answers nil and the cards ship without art until the owner runs `capture journal` again (M5-5, human-required). A plain dark strip is what a card with no art gets: a stand-in picture of some other instance would be the panel saying something the client did not.

- **2026-09-09 (M5-1, WKE-550) - there is ONE item widget and ONE item loader, and the badge on either is text.** Three tabs were about to grow three ways of drawing an item and two ways of waiting for one. Decided: `UI/ItemLine.lua` is the only place an item becomes pixels and `Modules/ItemData.lua` is the only place the client is asked to load one - M3-12's request-and-re-read lifted out of `Modules/Vault.lua` unchanged in behaviour, with `Vault` keeping every rule about what is pending and `C_Item.RequestLoadItemDataByID` leaving `Vault.FUNCTION_NAMES` with the call. Three consequences worth writing down. **(a) The badge is text, never a bar.** A bar's length is a number divided by a maximum, and both would be ours; QE Live's export carries a formatted string and the widget colours it and stops. **(b) QE Live's colours are read, not remembered.** `upgradeColor` in his `ItemUpgradeCard.js` (line 134) returns `#FFDF14` above zero and `#C16719` otherwise and that file's downgrade card is `opacity: 0.72`; his tag words are `getItemTags` in `MiniItemCard.tsx` (line 85) and are CSS colour NAMES - aqua, plum, yellow, orange, lightblue, lime - whose hex was resolved through the `color-name` table in the same checkout rather than from memory. No code or asset of his is copied; a colour is a fact about a look. **(c) A row that cannot be named is still a row.** The icon is `GetItemInfoInstant`'s fifth return, which is static data and answers before a load, so a pending row keeps its own icon and says `RETRIEVING_ITEM_INFO` - Blizzard's own string, in Blizzard's own situation - and a load that never arrives leaves it exactly there. The corollary is the cancel: a line handed another item drops its request, or a late answer would fill in a row that has moved on. The stub now records `SetTexture` / `SetAtlas` / `SetVertexColor` / `SetAlpha` / `SetTextColor`, because which icon and which tint a row got are decisions, not pixels; how they LOOK is still the owner's eye test.

- **2026-09-09 (M5-2, WKE-551) - the chrome is Blizzard's portrait frame, the paste box is a dialog, and the launcher costs no library.** Five things were settled while building it. (1) **The portrait is filled by `UI.ApplyPortrait` rather than by `PortraitFrameMixin:SetPortraitToSpecIcon`.** Read under `.luals/` (`Blizzard_SharedXML/PortraitFrame.lua:78`), the mixin IS those two steps - the spec's icon, the class icon when there is no spec - so calling it would change nothing about the result and would put the only path to a portrait behind a FrameXML mixin the headless harness does not model. Doing it here makes the spec read, the class fallback and the "names neither" case one guarded piece of code a test drives. (2) **`C_SpecializationInfo` first, the globals second.** Blizzard's own annotations mark `GetSpecialization` and `GetSpecializationInfo` deprecated in favour of the namespaced pair (`Blizzard_Deprecated/Deprecated_Specialization_Standard.lua`); the issue named the globals, so both are asked, in that order, and neither is required. (3) **The strip returns a model, not a string.** The amber age is a colour code, and a test that asserted on `|cffffd43b` inside a sentence would be a test of the sentence; `{ text, stale, tooltip }` lets the staleness be asserted as a fact and the colour as a consequence. **A client that will not say when the weekly reset is gets `nil`, never `true`**: "we cannot tell how old this is" must not read as "this is stale". (4) **The paste box moved whole, and the window's four keys became aliases onto the dialog's widgets.** `UI.Import` writes to `UI.frame.status` and has since M2-2; making the dialog's status font string be that key means the import path, its routing and every one of its refusals are byte-for-byte what they were, which is what makes "the dialog changed nothing about importing" a claim rather than a hope. (5) **The launcher is native.** `## AddonCompartmentFunc` names a global function and costs nothing; `LibDBIcon`/`LibDataBroker` would need vendoring and a licence line in `Libs/LICENSES.md` (§8) for one user's minimap button. `LootpathToggle` is therefore the ONLY global the addon defines besides the slash handler. **Not decided here, because M5-0 (WKE-549) has not answered:** the window size stays 620 x 640 (the mockups are 760) and no ElvUI skin hook was built.
- **2026-09-09 (M3-12, WKE-547) - a vault reward the client has not loaded yet is `pending`, is asked for with `C_Item.RequestLoadItemDataByID`, is read again on the client's load event, and is never printed as `[]`.** Measured on the 08:59 transcript (§9): right after a client restart `GetItemHyperlink` answers at once with `|h[]|h`, `GetItemInfo` / `GetDetailedItemLevelInfo` answer nothing, and the key is intact - the journal's 2026-09-06 second stage, on the vault. **Decided, and why each way:** (1) the exported function and the two documented events (`ITEM_DATA_LOAD_RESULT`, `GET_ITEM_INFO_RECEIVED`, both `itemID, success` in Blizzard's Event.lua) rather than `Item:CreateFromItemID(id):ContinueOnItemLoad(fn)`. Read under `.luals/`, the mixin IS that call: `ItemEventListener:AddCallback` runs `C_Item.RequestLoadItemDataByID(id)` once per id and fires callbacks on `ITEM_DATA_LOAD_RESULT` when `success` is true (`Blizzard_ObjectAPI/AsyncCallbackSystem.lua`, `Item.lua`). Calling it directly keeps every client call a literal name in `Vault.FUNCTION_NAMES`, keeps the bound and the combat check in the module where a test drives them, and takes no dependency on a FrameXML global (`Item`) the headless harness does not model. The 2026-09-06 journal decision NOT to call `RequestLoadItemDataByID` stands for the journal: there, Blizzard's own loot path never calls it and 351 events fired unprompted; here, nothing fires unprompted - the vault's links sat nameless for the whole 08:59 capture - and the mixin Blizzard ships for exactly this case makes the same call. (2) The bound is the journal's (8 x 0.25 s) and a record that never resolves stays `pending` in words and is asked for again on the next look; it is never filled from anywhere else. (3) The panel may show the name from the newest stored vault snapshot that names the same `itemDBID`, labelled `(from the last capture)` - a fact the client stated in an earlier session about the same reward - and never the level from it, because a level on that row reads as the client's current answer. (4) The issue's "slot unknown" was not what the transcript says: `GetItemInfoInstant` (static data) answered the equip location on every pending reward, so the slot is kept and a pending reward is counted, valued and eligible for the pick like any other; a pending reward with no static data at all stays with the gear rather than becoming an extra, because "not known yet" is not "not gear". (5) Captures do not request: `capture vault` records the nameless link as it stands, and the module asks at panel time, out of combat.
- **2026-09-08 (M3-10, WKE-545) - the client says at what item level a drop drops, QE Live says what that item is worth at each level he modelled, and Lootpath joins on both.** C-7 filed a document per key level and the panel picked ONE of them, the one the walk's preview level named. Measured on real documents that ranked no dungeon row at all: his +10 dungeon rows come back at 311 and the client's keystone-10 preview lists the same drops at 305 (§9). **Decided:** the join searches every stored document for the content type and keeps the exact `itemID@itemLevel` match wherever it is found - which is his **+6** document - and the row says which document answered. Three things this deliberately is NOT: it does not adjust either side's item level, it does not take a neighbouring level or interpolate between two documents, and it does not infer a key level from an item level (that would be Lootpath restating his `MPLUS_KEY_REWARDS` table in Lua, which C-7 already refused). A drop no document carries at the client's level stays unranked and is counted, exactly as before. **The tie, when several documents carry one drop at one level** (his +6 and +7 buttons both end at 305; every dungeon document carries the same raid rows): QE Live's own `dropType` first - the document that calls that level a `drop` rather than an `Upgraded` or `Bonus Roll` listing - then the lowest key level. Both are his facts or the list's order, neither is a judgement about his numbers. **Which side of the six-level disagreement is stale is still not Lootpath's to decide** and goes to WKE-528.

- **2026-09-08 (C-7, WKE-543) - the key level axis belongs to QE Live's engine, and the addon files what it is told rather than deriving anything.** "Which dungeon at the lowest possible key level gives me an upgrade" cannot be read out of one Upgrade Finder export: his engine values every dungeon drop at the single key `ufSettings.dungeon` names. Decided: the companion asks him once per level (`upgradeFinderKeyLevels`, default `[2, 4, 6, 8, 10]`), every Upgrade Finder document records the level it was run at, and `ns.UFImport` files one verdict per `(contentType, keyLevel)`. Four details were settled while building it.
  (a) **A key level is not `ufSettings.dungeon`.** That field is an INDEX into `MPLUS_KEY_REWARDS` (`src/Databases/MPlusKeyRewards.ts`): `M0, +2/3, +4, +5, +6, +7, +8/9, +10` are indices 0..7, and index 7 gives 311 / 321 / 334. The issue read it as a level and proposed `[2, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12]`; **there is no +12 in his table**, so the default is `[2, 4, 6, 8, 10]` and a level his page does not offer is a named failure rather than a nearest match.
  (b) **The mapping is read off his page, never restated.** `lib/fork.js` reads the toggle LABELS in the "Mythic+ Key Level" Paper, parses each into the levels a player would say (`+8/9` -> 8 and 9), and clicks the one covering the level asked for. Position in that row is his `key.index`, and that is not assumed either: every run compares the export's own `settings.dungeon` to the position it clicked and **fails the whole run (exit 4)** if they disagree, because a document filed under the wrong key level is a wrong answer that looks right.
  (c) **The addon does not derive a level from an export.** WKE-543 asked for a document without `keyLevel` to be filed under the level its `settings.dungeon` names; that is the index -> level table again, in Lua, where it would rot the day he adds a key. Instead such a document - a paste, or a file written before C-7 - is filed on an `"unknown"` shelf and the panel says the export does not say which key it was run at. **Rejected: guessing, and rejected: refusing the export.**
  (d) **The Raid Upgrade Finder document records a key level too**, against the issue's "the Raid one none". Measured on the committed 2026-09-07 pair: the Raid export carries the same 201 dungeon-sourced rows as the Dungeon one, all stamped `dropDifficulty: 7` at 311 / 321 / 334. A document whose rows were valued at a key is filed under that key; it simply runs once, at the highest configured level, rather than once per level, because the raid rows do not move with the key (measured: 156 of 156 non-dungeon rows are identical between the +2 and +10 runs).
  Also decided: the level list is hashed with the profile and the import settings (C-4/C-5), so adding or dropping a level costs a run and reordering one does not; and `ns.Companion` asks the importer what a verdict replaces (`importer.Existing(verdict)`) rather than matching on content type, because five dungeon documents in one file would otherwise read as four repeats of the first and be thrown away - a failure that is quiet, total, and was proven red before it was fixed.

- **2026-09-08 (C-3, WKE-536) - `/lootpath refresh` takes the three snapshots itself, and the reload is what flushes them.** The companion's profile is built from `newestSnapshot(captures.env|inventory|vault)` (`tools/companion/lib/simc-profile.js`), and nothing else in the addon writes gear to SavedVariables, so a refresh that only reloaded shipped whatever the owner had last captured **by hand**: the first real run, 2026-09-07, produced `spec=guardian` from the 09-05 capture, two days and one spec stale, with `region`/`level`/`race` missing because that snapshot predates S-2's reads. `Companion.Refresh()` now runs `env`, `inventory` and `vault` through `ns.RunCapture` in that order and only then calls `ReloadUI`; any refusal is repeated verbatim and stops the reload, so the owner never reloads believing a capture happened. **Why here and nowhere else:** not on `PLAYER_LOGOUT` (the client gives no reliable time there, and a capture at every logout writes SavedVariables nobody asked for); not in the companion (it cannot make the client read anything, only read what was written); not by having `Inventory`/`Vault` write snapshots on every scan (captures are the raw record the tests replay - one writer, or a golden stops meaning what it says). `journal` is excluded on purpose: it is asynchronous, the profile reads none of it, and a reload mid-walk would abandon it. The bank half of the chat line is read back out of the snapshot just taken rather than asked of the client again, so the words on screen are a fact about the bytes the companion will get. The cost is accepted and named: two refreshes now write six snapshots instead of none, and `/lootpath capture wipe` is still the only pruner.

- **2026-09-07 (C-2, WKE-534) - the companion file's shape, and who owns the game's copy of it.** Three things settled while building the addon half.
  (a) **The contract is one assignment and nothing else.** `Lootpath/Data/QEVerdict.lua` assigns `ns.companionVerdict = { writtenAt, companionVersion, exports = { {schema, contentType, json} } }`, Lua strings/numbers/tables only. The addon calls nothing in it, `loadstring`es nothing, and passes every field through `ns.Safe` before checking its type: it is the owner's own machine writing it, and it is still treated as data. `json` is QE Live's export text VERBATIM and goes through `ns.QEImport.Parse`, so the companion cannot smuggle past a pin the paste box enforces. The entry's `contentType` is advisory; the content type inside the JSON is what a verdict is filed under, because that is the one QE Live answered.
  (b) **The later of the file and the store wins, and the file is never re-imported.** A paste made after the companion wrote its file is kept, and said so in chat; a companion file newer than the stored import replaces it. Because the chunk loads at every login, the same `writtenAt` seen again is a no-op, not a re-import and not a chat line - otherwise every `/reload` would re-parse two exports and repeat itself. Where a verdict came from is stored on it (`source`), so the window and `/lootpath status` can say "pasted" or "companion, written N minute(s) ago" after a reload.
  (c) **`tools\sync.ps1` no longer owns the game's `Data\`** (this is §11's open question, now closed). A push copies every top-level child except `Data`, and writes the placeholder only when the game has no `QEVerdict.lua` at all - it must have one, because the `.toc` names it. `-IncludeData` overwrites it deliberately, for when the companion's file is what is broken. Measured against a scratch client tree, not the real one: the companion's file survived two plain pushes and was replaced by `-IncludeData`.
