The same measurement found that `Copy-Item -Path <dir> -Destination <that same dir>` NESTS (`Libs\Libs`) on a second push where `-Destination <its parent>` merges, which is why the loop names the parent.
  Rejected: gitignoring the placeholder (the `.toc` names it, so a release without it would not load); having the companion re-run after every sync (a sync is a dev action and the companion is not always up); a second `.toc` entry the companion creates (the file must exist for a fresh install). Upgrade Finder documents are refused by schema name until M3-6 lands, rather than handed to the Top Gear parser, which would report the wrong reason.

- **2026-09-04 - Addon over coach-workbench.** Bigger audience (every healer gears), Raidbots-shaped willingness to pay, and the only gap nobody fills. Workbench had better Healper reuse and a smaller market. Reuse does not drive bets.
- **2026-09-04 - Name: Lootpath.** Zero GitHub repos, no CurseForge hits (2026-09-04). CurseForge/Wago slugs to be claimed the day a public release is decided.
- **2026-09-05 - Licence: MIT.** Open source from day one; every dependency licence-checked.
- **2026-09-05 - Option A for the Upgrade Map.** Values-free for MVP: every spec-appropriate drop per slot by item level, QE deltas only where the Top Gear export covers the exact item (vault, owned). In parallel, prepare an Upgrade Finder export as a PR on the owner's fork (QE-1); owner offers it (QE-2); userscript scraper (QE-3) only if he declines - owner's call, recommendation is to leave the map values-free rather than scrape. Rejected: computing values ourselves (violates the product rule).
- **2026-09-05 - No own `/simc` exporter for MVP.** The SimulationCraft addon (Unlicense) already exports gear, bags and vault and is what QE Live expects. README tells the user to use it.
- **2026-09-05 - Tooltips descoped.** Pawn, GearingWise and GearWish already annotate tooltips; a fourth is not a gap. The dashboard and the loot map are the product. **REVERSED 2026-09-11 (owner) and now built out, 2026-09-14 (R-2, WKE-563):** the tooltip is the surface Blizzard will never build and the one most players use most, and what Lootpath puts there is not a fourth annotation but the one thing the other three cannot say - the item's part of this week's plan. Pawn's block sits above ours and is not fought. This entry stands as history; the live decision is the 2026-09-11 one below.
- **2026-09-05 - Ace3: AceDB only.** UI is native frames + templates; options through the modern `Settings` API (`InterfaceOptions_AddCategory` removed in 10.0). No AceGUI.
- **2026-09-05 - rxi/json.lua, strict `version: 1` pin.** Any other version refused with a message naming both versions. No guessing at a moved schema.
- **2026-09-05 - Item identity = itemID + sorted bonus IDs.** Two copies of an item at different upgrade levels are different items to QE Live; an itemID-only match is never accepted silently.
- **2026-09-05 - Test strategy.** busted over pure modules with a hand-written WoW stub (`spec/stubs/wow.lua`) and **committed capture transcripts** from the owner's client (`/lootpath capture <name>` -> SavedVariables -> `sync.ps1 -Pull` -> `spec/fixtures/`). wowless (MIT, active) evaluated and rejected for now: no Encounter Journal stubs. Every guard proven red before it ships.
- **2026-09-05 - CI gates are real failures**: luacheck (LuaRocks, Lua 5.1, explicit WoW globals list), StyLua `--check`, LuaLS `--check` with Ketho's annotations (MIT), busted, packager zip on every PR. Branch protection requires all of them; agents merge their own PRs only when green; never bypass, never disable a test to get green.
- **2026-09-05 - Packager: BigWigsMods/packager@v2** (v2.5.1, 2025-12-02): zip artifact on PRs, GitHub release on `v*` tags. No CurseForge/Wago upload for the MVP.
- **2026-09-05 - Values-free note wording is pinned**: "Values shown are QE Live's, for items it has ranked. Other drops are listed by item level only."
- **2026-09-05 - Nothing runs in combat**; every client read passes `issecretvalue`/`issecrettable`; no network; no backend.
- **2026-09-05 - Captures `inventory` and `vault` ship in M0-1** (moved from M1-1 and M3-3) so M0-2's single game session yields three transcripts (env, inventory with bank open and closed, vault before and after opening the window). The journal capture stays in M3-1 because it needs the async walk. Captures only call functions named in `Captures.lua`; iterating a namespace and calling what it finds is forbidden (`C_Bank` moves items).
- **2026-09-05 - Local gates.** StyLua 2.5.2 and lua-language-server 3.18.2 via winget; luacheck and busted in Docker (`tools/docker/Dockerfile`, Alpine Lua 5.1 + LuaRocks) because Chocolatey needs an elevated shell the dev machine does not have. `tools/check.ps1` runs all four; CI is the authority.
- **2026-09-05 - Packager layout via `move-folders`.** `release.sh` requires `.git` in its topdir (measured in CI: `-t Lootpath` fails with "No Git, SVN, or Hg checkout found"), so it runs from the repo root with `package-as: Lootpath`, `move-folders: Lootpath/Lootpath: Lootpath`, externals at `Lootpath/Libs/...`, and `ignore` for `spec`, `tools`, `docs` and the root markdown. The zip holds `Lootpath/` with the toc, code, `Libs/` and the MIT `LICENSE` the packager copies in (verified locally with `release.sh -d -e -z`).
- **2026-09-06 - Inventory records speak QE Live's slot vocabulary** and carry only gear. WKE-516's example used `"HEAD"`; the export's `item.slot` values are the strings above, and Match (M2-2) groups by them, so one vocabulary is kept. Items with no gear slot (330 of 453 bag entries in the transcript were `INVTYPE_NON_EQUIP_IGNORE`) are skipped rather than carried as records with a nil slot.
- **2026-09-06 - `qe-live-upgradefinder` v1 schema (fork branch, not yet upstream).** `{ schema, version: 1, exportedAt, player {name, realm, region, spec, gameType}, contentType, reportId, settings (ufSettings), equipped[] (Top Gear item shape), items[] {id, level, slot, source {instanceId, encounterId}, dropLoc, dropType, dropDifficulty, upgradePercent, hpsGain, score} }`. **Positive `upgradePercent` / `hpsGain` means better.** Lootpath's future parser pins that sign in a test the way QEImport pins Top Gear's. Items carry `id` + `level`, not bonus IDs, because the site's stored report has none; the Upgrade Map joins them to the journal by itemID and item level.
- **2026-09-06 - Golden fixtures for normalisers.** The transcript is replayed through the stub (`spec/helpers/replay.lua`) and the module's output is committed under `spec/fixtures/expected/`, regenerated only by running busted with `LOOTPATH_WRITE_EXPECTED=1`. A golden guards regressions; the field-by-field tests against the raw returns are what prove correctness.
- **2026-09-06 - The sign convention has one definition in code**: `ns.QEImport.ALT_WORSE_SCORE_PERCENT_SIGN = 1`, `ALT_WORSE_HPS_DIFFERENCE_SIGN = -1`, and `ns.QEImport.AlternativeIsBetter(alt)`. M2-2 and M3-3 read the direction from that predicate and never from their own arithmetic, because getting the sign backwards is this module's whole failure mode. The spec pins the same two constants independently.
- **2026-09-06 - `version` must be the JSON number `1`.** A string `"1"` is refused rather than coerced: a changed type means the schema moved, and the pin exists to fail loudly rather than wrongly (extends the 2026-09-05 version-pin decision).
- **2026-09-06 - The verdict keeps `topSet.order` and a per-item `count`.** A map keyed by item key has no order and collapses a matched pair of identical rings into one entry; `order` (keys in the export's own order, duplicates repeated) and `count` keep both facts. QE Live's exporter has a commit named "handle duplicates", so duplicates are real.
- **2026-09-06 - Vault items are harvested from alternatives as well as the top set.** A vault option QE Live ranked *below* the top set appears only in a differential; excluding it would hide exactly the option the Vault panel must talk about.
- **2026-09-06 - `QEImport.Parse` carries no combat guard.** It is pure Lua over a pasted string; its one client read is `UnitName("player")` for the name-mismatch warning, and that passes `ns.Safe`. The combat rule binds the UI (M2-2) and the scanners, not a string parser.
- **2026-09-06 - Captures can be asynchronous.** `ns.RegisterCapture(name, help, run, { async = true })` hands `run` a `finish(data, failure)` callback and `RunCapture` answers `{ok=true, pending=true}`; the snapshot is stored when `finish` is called, and `finish` counts only once. The Encounter Journal loads loot only after `EJ_LOOT_DATA_RECIEVED`, so `capture journal` cannot be one synchronous call the way `env`, `inventory` and `vault` are. Two guards come with it: only one capture runs at a time, and one that never calls back is abandoned after `ns.CAPTURE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (180) so the command cannot wedge. `RunCapture` also passes the rest of the slash command through as `args`, which is how `/lootpath capture journal 14` picks the M+ preview level.
- **2026-09-06 - `capture journal` registers in `Modules/Journal.lua`, not `Captures.lua`.** The rule is that a capture calls only functions its own file names, and the journal's list belongs beside the adapter that owns it; `Captures.lua`'s header points at it. `ns.captureOrder` is therefore `env, inventory, vault, journal`.
- **2026-09-06 - The journal capture is the one capture that is not purely a read**, and CLAUDE.md's rule is narrowed rather than broken. `EJ_SelectTier`, `EJ_SelectInstance`, `EJ_SetDifficulty`, `EJ_SetLootFilter` and `C_EncounterJournal.SetPreviewMythicPlusLevel` change the Adventure Guide's **view state** - what the journal is looking at - and nothing about the character, its items or its money. The walk reads the tier, difficulty and loot filter first and puts all three back when it finishes, **including when combat aborts it**. There is no getter for the M+ preview level, so that one cannot be restored: the owner is left with a preview level set in their Adventure Guide until they change it.
- **2026-09-06 - Difficulty IDs come from the client's own `DifficultyUtil.ID`,** with the values read from Blizzard's shipped `DifficultyUtil.lua` as the fallback (`Adapter.DIFFICULTY`). The journal's Mythic+ difficulty is `DungeonChallenge` (8), which is in Blizzard's own `EJ_DIFFICULTIES` list - not a wiki claim.
- **2026-09-06 - Both map-to-journal-instance lookups are recorded side by side, and a map neither resolves is recorded rather than dropped.** `capture journal` runs `C_EncounterJournal.GetInstanceForGameMap(mapID)` and the global `EJ_GetInstanceForMap(mapID)` against the same `GetMapUIInfo().mapID` for every map in `C_ChallengeMode.GetMapTable()`, and `pool.maps[i].resolvedBy` names whichever answered. This is the step §11 calls the most likely to differ from any doc, so the transcript decides it instead of the code assuming it.
- **2026-09-06 - An adapter's list of client functions is names, not values.** A table entry whose value is `nil` is simply absent, so a table of values cannot tell "this client does not have that function" from "not listed" - which is exactly what the capture has to record. `Adapter.FUNCTION_NAMES` is a list of strings and `Adapter.Resolve` looks one up; every real call site still names its function literally, so nothing is ever called because a lookup found it. Found by the test that deletes a function and expects `ok = false`.
- **2026-09-06 - The journal walk reads every target twice, mirroring `EncounterJournal_LootUpdate`.** Blizzard's `EncounterJournal_OnEvent` splits `EJ_LOOT_DATA_RECIEVED` two ways: an event carrying an itemID while the list is current refreshes just that row (`EncounterJournal_LootCallback`), anything else re-reads the whole list (`EncounterJournal_LootUpdate`, an `EJ_GetNumLoot` loop over `GetLootInfoByIndex`). The walk mirrors the second, because it has no frame per row to refresh and cannot come back to a target once the next `EJ_SelectInstance` has moved the journal on. `C_Item.RequestLoadItemDataByID` was the alternative and was **not** taken: Blizzard's own journal never calls it in the loot path, 351 events fired unprompted in the 09-06 walk, and adding a client function nothing has been observed to need is a guess. Both reads are kept in the capture so Tuesday's `capture journal` shows what the second one filled.
- **2026-09-06 - A link-less loot row is `pending`, not "no item level".** 240 of the 376 itemIDs in the 09-06 walk were link-less on *every* row they appeared in; treating a row with no equipLoc as non-gear would silently lose 64% of the map. So a pending row is carried with a nil `itemLevel` and `slot` and `pending = true`, and a row that is known and known not to be gear (a pattern, a mount, a toy - 55 of 613) is dropped the way Inventory drops one. The consequence is deliberate: an item that is a mount in one target and link-less in another stays in the map through the link-less row, because a link-less row cannot be told from gear.
- **2026-09-06 - `Journal` and `Inventory` share one slot table.** The journal's own `EncounterJournalItemInfo.slot` and the row's `GetItemInfoInstant` equipLoc agree one-for-one across all 244 rows of the 09-06 walk that carried both (`One-Hand`/`INVTYPE_WEAPON`, `Held In Off-hand`/`INVTYPE_HOLDABLE`), but the first is the client's localised UI text and the second is not. `ns.Journal` therefore goes through `ns.Inventory.SlotForEquipLoc`, which is what makes M3-3's join between the map and the scanner a join on one vocabulary - QE Live's (extends the 2026-09-06 Inventory decision).
- **2026-09-06 - A boss name is an instance-wide fact.** `EJ_GetEncounterInfoByIndex` answered nothing on every `DungeonChallenge` target and on both Midnight targets in the 09-06 walk, leaving 95 of 613 rows with no boss name in their own target. The aggregator indexes names per instance across every difficulty, which names 73 of those 95; the walk now also calls `EJ_GetEncounterInfo(encounterID)` per row, exactly as Blizzard's loot button does, which the next capture proves for the remaining 22.
- **2026-09-06 - The journal cache is keyed on the build NUMBER**, `GetBuildInfo()`'s second return (69587), not the version string: a hotfix build can move item levels without moving "12.1.0". Every entry from another build is dropped whenever `Build` runs, so a stale map cannot outlive a patch.
- **2026-09-06 - QE Live's content types are `"Raid"` and `"Dungeon"`, and "Mythic+" is not one of them.** `src/globalTypes.d.ts` line 130 declares `contentTypes = "Raid" | "Dungeon"`, `Redux/Reducers/RootReducer.ts` defaults the store to `"Raid"`, `SetupAndMenus/Header/ContentToggle.js` offers exactly those two toggle values, and the exporter writes `result.contentType || ""`. `Dungeon` IS the Mythic+ side. `ns.DB_DEFAULTS.profile.settings.contentType` therefore moved from `"Mythic+"` to `"Dungeon"`: the old default could never have matched an export's `contentType`, so the M2-2 setting would have silently done nothing. The two strings live once, in `ns.QEImport.CONTENT_TYPES`, and are compared to `verdict.contentType` with no translation between.
- **2026-09-06 - one import is kept per content type, as well as the most recent one.** `db.char.qeImport` still holds the last paste (M2-1's contract, unchanged), and `QEImport.Store` also files the verdict under `db.char.qeImports[contentType]`. Without that, the setting "which content type's verdict to show if the user pasted both" has nothing to choose between - the second paste would have overwritten the first. An export with no `contentType` files under `"Unknown"` rather than being dropped or being made to pretend it is one of QE Live's two.
- **2026-09-06 - Match makes one row per item in `topSet.order`, not one per slot name.** The export lists Finger twice and Trinket twice; a row per slot name would collapse a matched pair into one row and lose half the answer. Rows sort by a canonical slot order (read off the real export's own item order) with the export's sequence as the tiebreak, so the panel reads like a character sheet.
- **2026-09-06 - `dstSlot` comes from the scan, never from a table of slot IDs.** The equipment slot Lootpath equips into is the `slotIndex` the Inventory scan actually found the replaced item in (`INVSLOT_FIRST_EQUIPPED..LAST`, measured 1..19 on this client), handed out per slot in slot order. Hardcoding `INVSLOT_FINGER1 = 11` would have been a remembered number; this is a measured one. When the slot is empty there is no number to use and `dstSlot` stays nil, which is the client's own "you choose" case.
- **2026-09-06 - an itemID-only match is refused, and the itemID + item-level fallback is loud.** Extends the 2026-09-05 identity decision with the mechanism: `matchedBy` on the row, an entry in `result.fallbacks`, and a line in the chat frame naming both sets of bonus IDs. Proven red by making the fallback silent and by widening it to itemID only.
- **2026-09-06 - the panel keeps the last scan on screen in combat rather than blanking.** `Inventory.Scan` refuses in combat, so a naive refresh at `PLAYER_REGEN_DISABLED` would empty the window exactly when the user has stopped being able to fix it. The last good match stays, marked `stale`, the summary says so, and every button is disabled - and `EquipPanel.Equip` re-checks `InCombatLockdown()` itself, so a lockdown arriving between the draw and the click still refuses.
- **2026-09-06 - the export's age is computed without assuming a timezone.** `exportedAt` is ISO 8601 in UTC (`2026-09-06T21:14:24.465Z`). `time(fields)` reads its fields as LOCAL time and `date("!*t", t)` writes UTC ones, so `now - time(date("!*t", now))` is this machine's offset from UTC and adding it back turns the UTC fields into an epoch. The spec installs fake clocks at UTC, -5h, +9.5h and +14h and demands the same answer from all four; with the offset forced to zero, that test is the one that goes red.
- **2026-09-06 - the WoW stub grew a widget model.** Frames, regions, buttons, an editbox, GameTooltip and the Settings API, modelling the API's CONTRACT and nothing about pixels: `SetMaxLetters(0)` means no limit and any other value truncates; a disabled button swallows `Click` but still fires `OnEnter`. That is what makes "the editbox handler calls Parse" and "a 200 KB paste is not truncated" testable at all. It proves nothing about how the window looks or behaves on the owner's screen, which stays an in-game step (M2-3).
- **2026-09-06 - The journal source entry carries `itemKey` and `name`, and M3-3's join is the exact key and nothing looser.** WKE-524 asks the Upgrade Map to show a QE delta "where a candidate appears in the QE verdict (by item key)", and the map as M3-1 shipped it had no key at all, so the key was added: `ns.ItemKey` over the loot row's own link, nil on a pending row for the same reason `itemLevel` is. **Measured over the 2026-09-06 walk: all 189 keyed entries carry exactly ONE bonus ID, and it is 3524 on every one of them**, while the 15 items of the committed Top Gear export carry two to seven. Exactly one itemID is in both (251153, Arctic Explorer's Legwraps, Feet, Sentinel of Winter in Den of Nalorakk, 276 Heroic / 292 Mythic / 305 at M+10) and even there the keys differ. **So the Upgrade Map is values-free in practice as well as in principle: 0 of 558 sources carry a number.** The looser joins were considered and rejected - matching on itemID would put a number about the copy you own (263-321 with its own bonus IDs) next to a preview of a different item level, which is the estimate this addon never makes. The rule stands as written and the panel simply shows nothing; when the fork's Upgrade Finder export lands (QE-1/QE-2) it joins by itemID + item level as the 2026-09-06 schema decision says, and that is a different, honest join.
- **2026-09-06 - A vault reward's `type` and `itemDBID` are Blizzard's documented shape, not a measured one.** All four committed `capture vault` snapshots show `rewards` empty on every activity except Concession, which held a currency (`{ id = 3513, type = 2, quantity = 1 }`, `Enum.CachedRewardType.Currency`) and no `itemDBID`. `Modules/Vault.lua` is therefore written to `WeeklyRewardActivityRewardInfo` (`{ type, id, quantity, itemDBID? }`) from Blizzard's exported docs under `.luals/`, its tests drive that shape through the stub and say so in the file, and WKE-523's after-reset capture is what turns it from documented into measured. The alternative - guessing from the wiki - is the thing this project does not do.
- **2026-09-06 - Guard the value, then coerce it.** `tonumber(secret)` is `nil`, so `guarded(counter, tonumber(x))` silently swallows a secret and leaves the count at zero. Every read in `Modules/Vault.lua` passes `ns.Safe` first and is coerced after. Found by the test that made an activity's `threshold` secret and expected `secretsSeen` to be 1.
- **2026-09-06 - `spec/helpers/replay.lua` caches one transcript table, so a test that mutates it poisons every test after it.** `spec/journal_spec.lua`'s "prefers the second read" spliced a one-row second read into target 1 of the shared snapshot; every `it` declared after it therefore saw 588 rows instead of 613, and **the committed golden `spec/fixtures/expected/journal-20260906-161213.lua` was generated from that corrupted state** (533 sources, 375 items reported as 357, `rereadTargets = 1`). It passed because the write and the compare were equally wrong. M3-3 made that test work on `Stub.deepcopy(snapshot)` and regenerated the golden, which now reads 613 rows -> 558 sources over 375 items, matching what §0 and §11 have said all along. A test that mutates a fixture copies it first.
- **2026-09-06 - `QEImport.Coverage(verdict, key)` is the one lookup both M3-3 panels use**, so "covered" means the same thing on the Upgrade Map and in the Vault. A top-set item comes back with **no numbers** - the export gives per-item deltas only on its differentials, and inventing one for a top-set item would be Lootpath computing a healer value - so the panels say "QE Live: in your best set" there. An item in several alternatives is reported through the best of them by `QEImport.AlternativeRank`, which is the pinned sign constant and not fresh arithmetic.
- **2026-09-06 - Staleness is measured against the client's own reset boundary, never against a calendar.** `VaultPanel.IsVerdictStale` computes `nextReset = now + C_DateAndTime.GetSecondsUntilWeeklyReset()` and calls a verdict stale when its `exportedAt` predates `nextReset - 7 days`. `EpochFromISO` reads QE Live's UTC stamp through the client's own clock offset (`time() - time(date("!*t"))`), and answers nil for a stamp it cannot read, so an unreadable stamp produces no claim rather than a wrong one.
- **2026-09-06 - The Upgrade Map reads the newest stored journal walk, and says so when there is none.** `capture journal` is the only thing that produces a walk today (M3-1), so `Panel.Gather` reads `db.global.captures.journal`, and an empty map renders "No loot map yet. Run /lootpath capture journal out of combat to walk the Adventure Guide." rather than an empty season. **Driving a walk from a button in the window is still not built**: the tab exists now, but the walk changes the Adventure Guide's view state and takes seconds, so putting it behind a button is its own decision, and M3-4 is what says whether it is needed.
- **2026-09-06 - The window has one tab per promise, and only the visible tab is drawn.** M3-3's panels hang off the frame M2-2 built rather than opening windows of their own: `UI.TABS` names the three, `UI.ShowTab` owns which is visible, and `UI.Refresh` redraws that one alone. Redrawing all three on every `PLAYER_EQUIPMENT_CHANGED` and `BAG_UPDATE_DELAYED` would rebuild the journal map - 613 rows - to update a panel nobody is looking at. Blizzard's `PanelTemplates_SetTab`/`SetNumTabs` are called only for the selected/deselected artwork, through a `type(...) == "function"` check, because the tab has to keep working on a client that does not have them; what the tabs LOOK like is M3-4, in game.
- **2026-09-06 - A panel reads `ns.UI.ActiveVerdict()`, never `QEImport.Current()`.** M2-2 files one import per content type and the setting chooses between them, saying out loud when it falls back. A panel that reached for the most recent paste instead would show a Raid answer under a Dungeon setting with nothing said - which is the failure `ActiveVerdict` was written to prevent. Both M3-3 panels go through it and fall back to `QEImport.Current()` only when there is no window around them (a panel built directly in a test). Proven red on both panels by a character carrying a Dungeon and a Raid import at once.
- **2026-09-07 - A difficulty label is unique across a map, and the whole map is what decides it.** `GetDifficultyInfo` is the client's own localised name and stays the first choice, but it answers the bare word for dungeon Heroic (2) and raid Heroic (15) alike and for dungeon Mythic (23) and raid Mythic (16) alike - measured in game 2026-09-06, where the filter row read "Heroic (67)", "Mythic Keystone 10 (54)", "Heroic (38)", "Mythic (39)" and a clipped fifth. `Panel.DifficultyLabels` therefore labels every difficulty in a map together: a name nothing else shares keeps the client's localised word, and every member of a colliding group falls back to `Panel.DIFFICULTY_NAME`'s already-qualified form ("Heroic dungeon", "Heroic raid"), with the ID appended if even that collides. The set is the whole map's difficulties, never the filtered subset, so clicking a filter cannot rename the buttons. The alternative - qualifying always - was rejected because it would replace a localised name with an English one on every client, in the common case where nothing is ambiguous.
- **2026-09-07 - The filter row wraps, and the wrap is a pure function.** Five buttons at a fixed 120 points ran off the 620-wide window in game (2026-09-06, the fifth clipped). `Panel.FilterLayout` packs labels greedily into rows of a given width and returns `{ rows, buttons = { { index, row, column, width } } }`, so the wrap is a headless assertion rather than something only the owner's eye can check; `Panel.Refresh` re-anchors on every draw (the labels, and so the widths, change with the map) and moves the scroll frame down by however many rows it took. Words are kept - shortening "Heroic dungeon (67)" to fit would trade a clipped button for an unreadable one. The width comes from the button's own font string in the client and from a per-character estimate headlessly, which is what makes the layout test deterministic.
- **2026-09-07 - The pinned note belongs to the panel header, not to the list.** Both M3-3 panels put `note = Panel.NOTE` in the model AND printed it as the list's first line, while `Panel.Create` had already drawn it above the list - so it appeared twice on the Upgrade Map and the Vault tab in game (2026-09-06). `Panel.Lines` no longer emits it on either panel; the model keeps `note` because that is what pins the wording headlessly, and the wording itself is unchanged (2026-09-05).
- **2026-09-07 - A drop the client reports at item level 1 is hidden, and the slot says how many.** `C_Item.GetDetailedItemLevelInfo` answers `{1, false, 1}` for the cosmetic and quest drops the Adventure Guide lists beside real loot; they sort to the bottom of every slot. The owner's decision is to hide them behind `Panel.LEVEL_ONE_NOTE` ("N drops at item level 1 hidden: cosmetic and quest items"). This is not Lootpath forming a view about an item: 1 is the CLIENT's own figure, so hiding on it is a filter on a fact, and the count line is what keeps the list honest. Exactly 1 and no other threshold - the 12 rows at level 44 in the same walk are real loot and are shown. A pending row is never hidden, because its level is unknown rather than 1 (the same distinction `PENDING_NOTE` exists for), and the aggregator still carries every row: `counts.hiddenLevelOne` is the panel's own bookkeeping, and `ns.Journal` is untouched.
- **2026-09-07 - An unsubstituted packager token is not a version.** The `.toc` ships `## Version: @project-version@` and the BigWigs packager substitutes it only when it builds a release, so a copy `tools\sync.ps1` puts in the client carries the token itself and the title bar read "Lootpath @project-version@" (measured in game 2026-09-06). `ns.VERSION` treats a metadata value starting with `@` as absent and falls back to the `"dev"` that was already on that line. Fixed in Lua rather than in `sync.ps1` or the `.toc`, which stay exactly what the packager reads.
- **2026-09-07 - "Concession" stays the label for `WeeklyRewardChestThresholdType` 5, and it was checked.** Blizzard's own `Blizzard_WeeklyRewards.lua` (under `.luals/`) titles each vault row through `SetUpActivity(frame, LABEL, atlas, type)` with the global strings `RAIDS`, `DUNGEONS`, `PVP` and `WORLD` - and Concession is not one of them: it is a separate `ConcessionFrame` on `WeeklyRewardsConcessionMixin`, with the threshold type as an XML KeyValue, no category title at all, and only `WEEKLY_REWARDS_CONCESSION_FORMAT` (an icon and a quantity) as text. There is no display string to borrow, so the enum's own name stands.
- **2026-09-05 - LuaLS gate decides on the JSON report**, not the exit code: `lua-language-server --check` exits 0 either way (measured 2026-09-05), so `tools/luals-check.sh` fails on any diagnostic at Warning or above in `check.json`. CI invokes it as `bash tools/luals-check.sh` and the file carries mode 100755 in git, because a Windows checkout dropped the executable bit and CI failed with "Permission denied" (measured in run 33982979467).

---

- **2026-09-08 - The shape of `Data/QEVerdict.lua` is the ADDON's, and the companion writes to it.** C-1 and C-2 were built in parallel and each proposed one; the consumer's wins, so C-1 dropped its own `ns.QEVerdictFile`/`documents`/`kind` for C-2's `ns.companionVerdict` with `exports` of `{ schema, contentType, json }` (`Lootpath/Modules/Companion.lua` dispatches on `schema`, treats `contentType` as advisory and ignores fields it does not know, which is where the companion's `bytes` and `profileCapturedAt` ride). `json` is QE Live's own export TEXT rather than a decoded table. Text, because that is what keeps the courier a courier: the addon runs it through the same `ns.QEImport.Parse` a paste goes through, so the schema pin, the `version: 1` pin, the Retail pin and every refusal apply to a companion import exactly as they do to a paste, and there is one parser rather than two. The chunk declares one local, guards `type(ns) == "table"` so a stray load does nothing, and contains no call, loop or function; the escaper that makes that true is tested against quotes, backslashes, `]]`, newlines, NUL and DEL, and the golden it writes is loaded by a real Lua interpreter (`spec/companionfile_spec.lua`; C-2 owns `spec/companion_spec.lua`). Rejected: writing decoded Lua tables (a second parser, and the pins would live in the companion where the addon could not enforce them); long-bracket strings (a `]]` in an export would end the literal); `\\ddd` for every byte over 0x7F (three times the size for nothing - the client reads addon files as bytes and QE Live's JSON is UTF-8).
- **2026-09-07 - The local companion, and the paste rule amended.** The owner asked for the fewest actions and no alt-tab. An addon has no network and reads no files, so QE Live's answer must be carried in; what can change is who carries it. Decided: a companion program on the owner's PC (Node, `tools/companion/`) reads Lootpath's SavedVariables, builds the SimC profile, runs QE Live's engine through the owner's local fork, and writes `Lootpath/Data/QEVerdict.lua`, which the addon lists in its `.toc` and imports at load exactly as a paste (§5). The rule "everything external arrives by paste" becomes "by paste, or by that one file"; the addon still reads nothing but its own Lua at load, computes no healer value, and keeps every schema and version pin. Bounds: (a) **for the owner's machine only until WKE-528** - the companion drives his engine from the fork clone and nothing of his is copied into this repo or shipped; (b) the file is data, never code - string literals, numbers and tables, written by a tested escaper; (c) two `/reload`s is the floor and is stated as such, not hidden. Rejected: porting his engine to Lua (his code, and Lootpath would then compute healer values); a clipboard helper (still a hop outside the game); driving the live site (it has no export). Spikes first: S-1 (WKE-531, headless fork - Playwright before any engine surgery) and S-2 (WKE-532, SimC profile from SavedVariables, diffed against a real `/simc` string), then C-1/C-2 (533/534) and M3-6 (535, the Upgrade Finder import the companion's output needs). The 2026-09-05 "no own `/simc` exporter for MVP" decision is revisited by S-2 in the companion, not in the addon: the SimulationCraft addon stays the exporter for the manual path.
- **2026-09-08 - The Upgrade Finder parser is its own file, not a second schema inside `QEImport`.** The two exports disagree about what a positive number means: Top Gear's `scorePercent` is positive when the alternative is WORSE, the Upgrade Finder's `upgradePercent` is positive when the drop is BETTER. Both constants are `+1`, so a reader who reached for the wrong one would get no type error, no failing arithmetic and a backwards recommendation - the one failure mode this pair of modules has. They also key differently (itemID + bonus IDs there, itemID + item level here), store under different keys, and refuse with different words. `spec/ufimport_spec.lua` pins the trap directly: the same `+2.5` is "worse" through `QEImport.AlternativeIsBetter` and "better" through `UFImport.IsUpgrade`. Rejected: one parser with a schema switch (shared helpers were the only gain, and they are twenty lines of string formatting).
- **2026-09-08 - The Upgrade Map's second number joins on itemID + item level, exact or nothing.** An Upgrade Finder entry carries no bonus IDs, because QE Live's stored report has none, so the exact-key join M3-3 uses cannot apply. The join is `UFImport.Key(itemID, level)` and there is no looser fallback: a value he computed for a 334 drop is not a value for the 305 the journal previews. A row whose itemID he ranked at another level shows nothing, carries `rankedAtAnotherLevel`, and is counted - `counts.rankedAtAnotherLevel` plus one deduplicated entry per drop and a line on screen. Counting a disagreement is not estimating around it.
- **2026-09-08 - `hpsGain` is carried and not shown, and that is now a choice rather than a consequence.** WKE-535 asked whether the Upgrade Finder's `hpsGain` is a raw score delta or a real HPS figure. It is HPS: `UpgradeFinderEngine.js:372` computes `rawDiff = round(((newScore - baseScore) / baseScore) * baseHPS * modelDiff)`, and `baseHPS` is `player.getHPS(contentType)`, the active cast model's own HPS (§9). So Lootpath COULD show it without inventing anything. It does not, for now: the row shows his percentage alone, `Panel.UpgradeText` never emits the word, and a test asserts no rendered line contains "HPS". Putting a healing-per-second number on screen is a product decision, and it is the owner's - the number is parsed, stored and one format string away.
- **2026-09-08 - One paste box, routed on the schema string, read without decoding.** `UI.DetectSchema` finds `"schema": "..."` with a pattern and `UI.ImportAny` hands the text to the parser that owns it. Decoding a 120 KB export twice - once to route, once to parse - was the alternative. Nothing is trusted to the pattern: the parser it picks re-checks schema, version and game type, so the worst a bad guess can do is produce the wrong refusal. Text naming a schema that is neither is refused by name, naming both the ones Lootpath reads, because "not a Top Gear export" became a half-truth the moment there were two kinds.

- **2026-09-08 - The 2026-09-05 "no own `/simc` exporter" decision stands for the addon and is answered for the companion (S-2, WKE-532).** The question was whether Lootpath's own captures can be turned into a profile QE Live reads identically to the SimulationCraft addon's output. **They can.** Everything QE Live's importer reads off an item line - `id`, `bonus_id`, `gem_id`, `enchant_id`, `crafted_stats`, `drop_level`, `redirected_base_stats` - is in the item link, which the `inventory` capture already stores verbatim, and `tools/companion-spike/simc-profile.js` encodes it through the SimulationCraft addon's own offsets (Unlicense: mirrored, never copied). Measured: 26 items appear in both the 09-05 capture and the owner's 09-07 `/simc` string with the same itemID and bonus IDs, and every field of all 26 is byte-identical, including the three QE Live ignores; `adler32` reproduces his client's own `# Checksum: 14cbd9e1`; QE Live imported the generated profile with no error and agreed with the client's `GetDetailedItemLevelInfo` on 15 of 15 equipped items (§9). **The addon keeps the SimulationCraft addon as the exporter for the manual path** - nothing here adds an exporter to Lootpath, and the paste flow is untouched. Two findings shape the companion: **talents are not needed** (`talents=` reaches no branch of QE Live's importer, so no `C_Traits` capture was written, which the issue had allowed for), and `region` was the only field QE Live consumes that Lootpath did not capture - `env` now reads `UnitLevel`, `UnitRace`, `GetCurrentRegionName` and `GetCurrentRegion`. The one importer-consumed item field the companion still cannot produce is `titan_disc_id`, a tooltip spell scan of four specific belts.
- **2026-09-08 - QE Live's importer reads by LINE INDEX, so the profile's header shape is load-bearing.** `checkSimCValid` scans `lines.slice(0, 8)` for a line whose text before `=` the class name contains, and refuses the import otherwise; `processAllLines` starts its loop at `i = 8`, so an item line inside those eight is dropped in silence. `buildProfile` throws on either violation - checked against the joined text, not against the array, because a realm or character name carrying a newline would move every later line without changing an index. A field the transcript does not carry is omitted rather than written empty: an empty `region=` sets QE Live's player region to the empty string and reads as a measurement nobody took.

- **2026-09-08 - The companion's documents route the same way a paste does, and the staleness check compares like with like.** C-2 shipped with `qe-live-upgradefinder` refused by name and M3-6 named in the refusal; the M3-6 rebase closed it. `Companion.ImporterFor(schema)` resolves `ns.QEImport` or `ns.UFImport` at call time, and `ImportAll` calls exactly four things of whichever it gets - `Parse`, `ContentTypeKey`, `ForContentType`, `Store` - reaching past none of them into a verdict's shape. The `ForContentType` in that list is the point: a Top Gear import and an Upgrade Finder import for one content type are two different answers, and neither is stale because of the other. A schema neither importer owns is still refused by name, naming both; this module never guesses at an importer.

- **2026-09-08 - Vault what-ifs are QE Live's scenarios, named, never Lootpath's model.** The owner's point: a vault option is what it can become - catalyzed into tier (keeping a set bonus at a higher level), upgraded along its track - not what it is offered as. QE Live already models both through its import dialog's three boxes: `autoCatalyze` adds a catalyzed tier clone of every active (equipped or vault) item that `Item.canBeCatalyzed()` accepts (this season's conversion, Head/Chest/Shoulder/Legs/Hands, not already tier; `SimCImportEngine.ts` ~253-265); `autoUpgradeAll` / `autoUpgradeVault` raise tracked items to `CONSTANTS.itemLevelCaps` (Adventurer 282, Veteran 295, Champion 308, Hero 321, Myth 334; crafted excluded). Decided: the companion runs Top Gear under three fixed, named scenarios - `asOffered` (all off), `catalyzed` (catalyze on), `maxed` (all three on) - only Top Gear, the what-if scenarios only when the profile carries vault gear; every document says which scenario it is; the addon files verdicts per (content type, scenario) and **Equip Now and the Upgrade Map keep reading `asOffered`**; the Vault tab shows each gear option's standing under each scenario in QE Live's words and numbers, one line each, and highlights by `asOffered` unless the owner's setting names another. Rejected: modelling the Catalyst or crests in Lua (the product rule); a single "best guess" scenario (it would be Lootpath choosing which question to ask and hiding the others); QE Live's site defaults (asymmetric, §11). Issues: 539 (explicit boxes, all three), 538 (the tab's real-shape fixes), 540 (the scenarios). **Built 2026-09-09 (C-6), and four details were settled while building it.** (a) **A scenario is an IMPORT, not a flag on a run**: `runSimC` is handed the checkbox state at Submit (`SimCraftDialog.js` `handleSubmit`), so the companion re-pastes the same profile once per scenario; `plannedPasses` is the shape that makes, and the Upgrade Finder rides in whichever pass already asks for the configured upgrade pair with catalyze off rather than getting a scenario name it never answered. (b) **The exact key can never find a catalyzed option, so the panel needs a second join** - `ns.QEImport.CatalyzedCoverage`. His `autoCatalyze` keeps the original and ADDS a clone with a different item ID; the exporter carries neither `catalyzedID` nor `isCatalystItem`; so the clone is recognised by the fields `convertToTier` copied - same slot, same non-empty bonus IDs, still `isVault`. Measured: without it the `catalyzed` line for the vault Spaulders reads "not ranked" while his own answer has them in the top set as tier 271526. **This is a join and not a model**: nothing in Lua knows which items can be catalyzed, and "his run made no tier version of this item" is read off the ABSENCE in his output next to the `autoCatalyze` box the file records - never restated from `canBeCatalyzed`. (c) **`asOffered` is not optional and is the only thing written to `qeImport`/`qeImports`**: Equip Now and the Upgrade Map read those, and a `catalyzed` answer landing there would tell the owner to equip an item the Catalyst has not been used on. The config refuses a scenario list without it. (d) **A document that names no scenario is `asOffered`; a document naming a scenario this build does not know gets a shelf of its own** - absence is evidence (every paste and every pre-C-6 file is the character as it stands), a name nobody here recognises is not. Other transformations the game offers - crafting and recrafting, embellishments, enchants and gems - QE Live reads from the SimC string as they are (`crafted_stats`, `enchant_id`, gems, bonus 8960) and the Upgrade Finder has its own crafted-level settings; none is modelled here either, and a gap found there becomes a QE Live question, not a Lootpath estimate.

- **2026-09-08 - "Where should I run" is answered by ranking QE Live's numbers and counting the client's facts, never by weighting them.** The owner wants the Upgrade Map to name the best run at a glance, and to choose between the single best upgrade and the run with the most drops that could improve the set. Decided: a by-run view (WKE-542) with two sort orders - best `upgradePercent` in the run, and the count of drops with `upgradePercent > 0` shown over the journal's drop count for that run - both facts side by side. **Rejected: an expected-value or "probability of an upgrade" figure.** The journal gives no drop rates; "one in N" would be Lootpath's assumption, and his percentage times that assumption is a number he never gave - exactly the estimate the product rule forbids. The reader sees `3 of 36 drops` and judges. Key level is an axis QE Live owns (`MPlusKeyRewards.ts`), so "the lowest key that still gives an upgrade" comes from running his Upgrade Finder at several levels (WKE-543), not from Lootpath restating his table. **Built 2026-09-08 (M3-8), and three details were settled while building it.** (a) **A second model, not a mode inside the first**: `Panel.RunModel` / `Panel.RunLines` sit beside `Panel.Model` / `Panel.Lines` over one gather, so the slot view renders byte for byte what it did and its tests are unchanged; the two share the row builder, the difficulty survey and the filter, which is where a drift between them would have started. (b) **The denominator is every drop the journal lists for the run**, including the item-level-1 cosmetics the slot view hides and the rows whose item data never arrived - so `3 of 36` is the walk's own count and not a number this panel curated. That is the reason the run view walks `sources` itself instead of regrouping the slot model's already-filtered rows. (c) **"Sorts last" is a consequence, not a clause**: only a drop QE Live's own `IsUpgrade` calls an upgrade is counted, so a rated run always has a positive best and a count above zero and loses to nothing under either order; a guard for it was written, found to be dead code no mutation could turn red, and removed. What is deliberately absent is still absent: `Panel.RUN_NOTE` states on screen that neither ranking is weighted by drop chance, and a test forbids "expected", "probability", "weighted", "chance", "odds" and "1 in " on every other line of the view.
- **2026-09-09 - There is a fourth question, it is the one the vault actually poses, and it is the Vault tab's default (M3-13, WKE-548).** After a full check of the Vault tab the owner asked "what is my best vault option?" and C-6's three scenarios did not answer it: `asOffered` assumes no charge and no upgrade, `catalyzed` assumes the charge and no upgrade, and `maxed` assumes every item the character owns is at its cap - which nobody reaches in a week. The question a player with one Catalyst charge and a pile of crests asks is **take one thing, upgrade that one thing, use the charge once**, and QE Live answers it as one import: `autoUpgradeVault` on, `autoUpgradeAll` off, `autoCatalyze` on. Decided: a fourth named scenario `thisWeek`, asked and gated exactly as the other two what-ifs are (vault gear in the profile, or `--force`), filed under `(content type, scenario)` like every other document, shown on the Vault tab as `this week (vault upgraded, Catalyst used): …`, and **made the DEFAULT highlight** - `asOffered` stays the first line of the list so "nothing in the vault beats your set" is never hidden, and `asOffered` stays the only document Equip Now and the Upgrade Map read. Rejected: computing it (the product rule); leaving the default at `asOffered` (on this tab it answers "what if I take this and change nothing", which is not what a vault poses); a shorter label like "this week" alone (it would not say what was assumed, on the line read before anything else). **Three things were settled while building it.** (a) **Half his answer is about items the vault is not offering.** Under `thisWeek` his top set takes the vault's weapon at 321 AND catalyzes the Venom-Cursed Lynx's Spaulders the owner already had in a bag - not the vault Spaulders, because `ItemSet.ts:205` allows one vault option per set and the weapon won it. A headline naming only the vault option would report half of what he said, so `ns.QEImport.CatalyzedOwned` is C-6's `CatalyzedCoverage` join pointed at the INVENTORY instead of the vault: a top-set item with a set ID whose slot and bonus IDs are an owned item's. It is gated on the run's own recorded `autoCatalyze`, passes over any item whose exact key is in the scan (a tier piece already worn), claims nothing at all when there is no scan, and when his clone matches nothing owned it says `catalyze a shoulder you own (QE Live did not say which)` rather than picking one. **The level is deliberately not part of the join**, because his upgrade boxes move `level` without touching a bonus ID; the level on screen is the client's, off the owned record. (b) **His `autoCatalyze` clones everything it can, so a set may spend more charges than a scenario's name implies** - this one spends two, the Spaulders and a chest. The tab says both, beside the client's own charge count, and computes nothing; the owner reads "you have 1 of 8" next to two catalyzes and draws his own conclusion. That is the honest report of what QE Live answered, not a defect in the question. (c) **`maxed` gained "needs a Catalyst charge" as a correction**: its boxes have had `autoCatalyze` on since C-6, and once its line could read "and catalyze your Venom-Cursed Lynx's Spaulders into the tier shoulder", saying only "needs crests" beside it contradicted the sentence next to it.

- **2026-09-08 - The companion asks QE Live for both upgrade settings explicitly, and both default OFF (C-5, WKE-539).** QE Live's import dialog opens with `autoUpgradeVault = true` and `autoUpgradeAll = false` (`SimCraftDialog.js` lines 36-37), which values a vault option at the top of its upgrade track and owned gear at the level the client reports. The S-1 driver filled `#simcentry` and clicked Submit without touching either box, so the companion inherited that asymmetry and told the owner to take a 305 copy of a weapon they wear at 308. Decided: the driver sets both boxes on every run, by their label text, reads them back, and fails the run (exit 4) if a click did not take; `config.json` carries `qeAutoUpgradeVault` and `qeAutoUpgradeAll`, both **false** by default; the verdict file records the pair in `qeSettings` next to `writtenAt`; and the fingerprint (C-4) hashes the pair with the profile, so flipping a box is a new question and costs a run. **Both off and both on are the two consistent choices** - "what is best out of what I have, where it stands" and "what is best if I upgraded everything" - and `asOffered` is the one Equip Now, the Upgrade Map and the Vault tab's default highlight are built on (the 2026-09-08 scenarios decision above). Rejected: leaving his defaults and explaining the 321 on screen (it makes the reader do the reconciling, every week); locating the boxes by JSX index as the spike did (the vault and catalyze boxes render only for `gameType === "Retail"`, so an index means a different box elsewhere); touching `autoCatalyze` (that is WKE-540's, and a box nobody asked about is left where he put it); and refusing the mixed pair in config (it is his default, the owner may want to reproduce the site, and the log names it for what it is). **Premise correction, reported in the PR:** the issue said the three labels come from `locale/en/translate.json`'s `SimCInput.*` keys; that block holds five keys, none of them a checkbox, and all three labels are plain JSX string literals - so they are matched as literals read from his source.

- **2026-09-14 (evening) - A capture may put an owned item in the open upgrade vendor's window to read its crest type and cost. This is the THIRD recorded exception to "captures only read"** (the journal's view state, 2026-09-06; the vault's `OnUIInteract`, 2026-09-14, above). The owner's answer to WKE-568 question 2, built as M3-17 (WKE-574). **Why there is no read-only way:** every crest road on every surface says `crest type and cost not readable` (`Roads.CREST_NOT_READABLE`) because `C_ItemUpgrade.GetItemUpgradeItemInfo()` answers about ONE item - whichever is in the open vendor window - and Blizzard exports no call that asks about an item without putting it there. Blizzard's own `ItemUpgradeSlotMixin` does exactly this on a click: `ItemUtil.IteratePlayerInventoryAndEquipment` filtered by `CanUpgradeItem`, then `SetItemUpgradeFromLocation` (`.luals/.../Blizzard_ItemUpgradeUI/Mainline/Blizzard_ItemUpgradeUI.lua.annotated.lua` lines 906-940, read 2026-09-14). **What the exception covers and what it does not:** the vendor window's own display changes, and nothing else - no item moves, nothing is bought, nothing is upgraded, no currency is spent. `UpgradeItem` is the call that would spend crests; it is never made, never named in the capture's list, and `spec/captures_spec.lua` reads `Lootpath/Captures.lua`'s source and asserts the call is not in it. `SetItemUpgradeFromCursorItem` is not called (it would depend on what the owner is holding) and neither is `CloseItemUpgrade` (closing the window the owner opened is not this capture's business). The window is left EMPTY rather than restored: putting back an item the owner did not choose in this moment is itself a change, so the transcript records what was there (`itemInWindowAtStart`) and he puts it back himself. **The owner runs it at a vendor; nothing reads the result until he has.** No road, tooltip or panel reads an `upgrade` snapshot yet - the transcript is the only evidence for what the client actually returns, and the surfaces that want the number (the Upgrade Map's crest rows, the plan sentence's "crest it") are a follow-up on top of it, not a guess ahead of it.
- **2026-09-08 - The Vault tab may read the player's Catalyst charges and crests from the client and show them; it may not do cost arithmetic.** Currency counts are facts (`C_CurrencyInfo`, a new read-only capture named in `Captures.lua`, WKE-544), and each scenario's assumption is a fixed phrase ("needs a Catalyst charge"). Upgrade costs are not readable outside the upgrade UI and are not estimated. Which currency IDs are the season's crests and the Catalyst charge is read from the owner's transcript by name, never guessed. **Built 2026-09-09 (M3-9), and the last sentence is the part that shaped the code:** `ns.Currencies.CREST_NAMES` and `CATALYST_NAMES` ship EMPTY and are filled from the transcript, so the addon cannot show a count it has not been told the name of; a shipped-empty test pins that, and a second test fills them and proves the counts reach the line. The capture keeps the list's headers because the client's grouping is the evidence for which entries are upgrade currencies. **Amended 2026-09-09 (M3-11, WKE-546): read the currencies by ID, not by name.** The shipped-empty rule was the right instinct aimed at the wrong key - `GetCurrencyListInfo` walks the currency TAB, the tab lists only the rows of EXPANDED headers, and a name that is not in it may still be a currency the player is holding. So the measured IDs are the primary key (`Currencies.KNOWN_IDS`), the names stay as the fallback, and the capture probes `GetCurrencyInfo(id)` for every known ID. The rule that survives unchanged is the one that matters: **every ID and name in the repo was read from a tool** - the transcript, or the owner's own tooltip - and none from memory or a wiki. `ExpandCurrencyList` would open the headers and change UI state, so it is not called; captures only read. `/lootpath refresh` takes `currencies` as a fourth snapshot - the companion never reads it, but refresh is the command the owner already runs weekly and the one the "unknown" line names.

- **2026-09-09 - The fifth question is answered from QE Live's own alternatives, not from a new run (M3-14, WKE-555).** 548 left a wrinkle on the screen: his `thisWeek` best set catalyzes TWO of the owner's items (a shoulder and a chest) while the owner holds ONE charge, and the tab said both names beside `you have 1 of 8` and computed nothing. The owner's remaining question - **with one charge, which single conversion does he rate best?** - had two possible answers. **Rejected: asking him again.** His Top Gear page can catalyze one chosen item (`MiniItemCard.tsx`, `setCustomItemOptions`), so a companion change plus one fork run per candidate would produce a document per conversion; that is a real capability and it is scoped, not built, because it costs a run per candidate for an answer he has already given. **Decided: read it off the `thisWeek` document.** A Top Gear export carries the top set plus up to twelve `differentials` - alternative sets HE built and HE scored, each with its own `scorePercent` - so a set with exactly one catalyzed owned item is a set he ranked, and showing the best-ranked of those is display, not a healer value. Lootpath does not choose the item, does not average or add his numbers, and does not compare two of his answers: `AlternativeRank` (the sign constant) does the ordering, exactly as it does everywhere else in the addon. Applying a differential is not a guess either - his own `buildDifferential` (`TopGearEngine.ts:499-511`) pushes both rings or both trinkets the first time either differs, so a slot he names, he names completely, and the replacement is slot-wide with nothing interleaved. **It answered on the real data** (§9): three of his thirteen sets qualify on each side, best 1.7292% behind on the Dungeon document and 1.6461% on the Raid one, both spending the charge on the Hide of Pestilence - so design B stayed unbuilt. If a future week's export contains no one-charge set, the line says `not in QE Live's export - no set he ranked spends the charge just once` and stops there; that is the answer, not a prompt to estimate one. The line is omitted entirely when the question does not arise at all - no `thisWeek` document, the Catalyst box off in the run that made one, or no inventory scan - because an absence sentence about a document nobody read would be a claim.
- **2026-09-09 (night) - A Catalyst conversion of a vault reward is a charge like any other (M3-15, WKE-556; corrects 555's premise).** 555's issue defined a one-charge set as one with exactly one catalyzed **owned** item and told the build to skip `isVault` items; the build did exactly that. The premise was wrong: the Catalyst spends one charge per item converted, and a Great Vault reward the owner takes and converts costs the same charge. On the committed `thisWeek` documents that changes the answer from a line to an absence (§9): every set he ranked converts two items. **Decided:** `OneChargeCandidates` counts every clone - owned or vault - as a charge, distinguishing a vault clone from a vault reward that is tier as offered by comparing the set item's ID with the reward the vault snapshot carries; with no vault snapshot a vault tier item counts as a charge, the conservative reading. The absence sentence stays the answer when no set qualifies. Nothing else in 555's design moves: his alternatives, his ordering, no new run. **Built the same night** exactly as decided, with one addition the issue asked for and this note did not: `CatalyzedVault` puts the vault half into the scenario sentence too, because a line that named only the owner's conversions would go on understating the charges beside the client's `you have 1 of 8`.
- **2026-09-09 (late night) - The launcher measures the minimap; it does not assume one.** 551 placed the button on a fixed 80-point circle, right for Blizzard's 140-point round minimap and wrong for any addon that resizes or squares it: on the owner's ElvUI the button sat inside the map. **Decided:** `UI.MinimapButtonOffset(angle, width, height, shape)` is pure over the minimap's real size and its published shape; `UI.MinimapGeometry()` reads `Minimap:GetWidth()/GetHeight()` and `GetMinimapShape()` (the global square-minimap addons publish and every minimap-button library reads; only "SQUARE" changes anything, everything else is round) at every placement; the button re-places itself on the minimap's `OnSizeChanged`, because ElvUI sizes the map after the addon has drawn. On the square the button rides the edge, clamped, with the corner just inside it, LibDBIcon's convention. Still no library vendored. **Not measured:** that ElvUI's shape global says "SQUARE" on the owner's client - it is the convention LibDBIcon has read for years, and the owner's screenshot after the sync is the check.
- **2026-09-10 - A dropdown that must say what is chosen is the selection template; the stub models each template's mixin, not a union of them.** 552's issue asked for `WowStyle1FilterDropdownTemplate` and the build used it, then called `SetDefaultText` and `SetText` on it to show the chosen difficulty. Blizzard's filter template (`WowStyle1FilterDropdownMixin`: ButtonStateBehavior + DropdownText + WowFilterButton) has a fixed FILTER caption and no `SetDefaultText`; the selection template (`WowStyle1DropdownMixin`: ButtonStateBehavior + DropdownSelectionText) has both. The client said so at 1865. **Decided:** the Upgrade Map's difficulty control is `WowStyle1DropdownTemplate`, the Vault's choice (`Panel.DROPDOWN_TEMPLATE` in both files); and the stub hands a DropdownButton `SetDefaultText` / `GetDefaultText` / `SetSelectionText` only under the selection template, so a panel calling them on a filter dropdown fails headlessly the way it fails in the client. The general rule: when the stub models a template, it models THAT template's mixin chain as read under `.luals/`, never a convenient superset - a superset is a test that cannot go red.
- **2026-09-11 - The Roads lane: three surfaces over one road model, and two owner decisions on voice (`docs/ROADS-UX.md`, draft 8).** A road is one way to get an item: its source, the item as it would arrive, its rating, and the thing to do next. Three surfaces render it: **In place** (at most three lines on Blizzard's item tooltip and a bag glow that fires only for "in your best set" or a positive percent), **Roads** (the Upgrade Map slot's expanded row, in three groups that are never one ranking: your best set, other rated sources, no rating), **This Week** (only when a night is named in Options; one chip per night). Rules settled by the review: no step counts, rings or combined orderings; every rating carries its age; every comparison names its referent and its level; a drop is rated at the level the client previews (the 545 join); the vault is "open now · reset in" and never a deadline; a weekly resource names its rival road on both sides; a verb only where it goes somewhere. **Owner decision 1: the 2026-09-05 "tooltips descoped" entry is reopened** - the tooltip is the surface Blizzard will never build and the one players use most; Pawn's block sits above ours. **Owner decision 2: no source is named anywhere in the addon's UI** - not "QE Live", not "his", not an address; the screen shows the rating and says what to do ("do: take it"), which retires the old "never you should" rule. The imperative is bounded to the road's own next step, never a choice between roads. The product rule is unchanged: the addon computes nothing and never claims it did; "rated" is true whichever engine writes the verdict file, and the trace from a rating to its source lives in this document and the companion, not on screen. The research lesson that a mark should say who rated it is set aside by that decision, knowingly. Concepts rejected and why: the flow chart, the four-column panel, the metro map and the orbit all carried counts or rings, which are a score by another name. **Owner decision 3 (the same evening): the plan comes first.** The owner read the finished mockups and could not tell whether to take the weapon or catalyze the shoulders; every surface with more than one road now opens with the whole plan in one plain sentence - what to take, what each weekly resource goes on, what to leave - built from the best set's own facts, with the roads below explaining it. Principle 16 in the brief. **Owner decision 4: the sentence sounds like a guildmate in chat**, not a manual - "Grab the staff from the vault and put your Hero crests into it. Catalyst the Lynx shoulders in your bag. Skip the vault shoulders." No labels, percentages or item levels in it; hand-written phrasings per verb, never assembled from field names.
  - **Note 2026-09-14 (V-1, WKE-569): the three tabs that predate the decision now follow it.** The decision was written for the Roads lane; `Core.lua`, the status strip, the Import dialog, Options, Equip Now, the Upgrade Map, the Vault tab, both importers' refusals and `Match`'s reasons were written before it and said "QE Live" in 73 string literals. All 73 are swept. **Ratings speak for themselves:** "QE Live's pick" is "the pick", "QE Live valued it at 321" is "rated at 321", "QE Live: better by 3.08%" is "better by 3.08%", the Equip Now count "without a verdict" is "no rating", and an uncovered slot carries the phrase table's `no rating` rather than "QE Live's set does not name this slot". **The status strip lost its first fact**: it led with the engine's name and now opens on the spec, so it is five facts, not six. **The paste path is the one place a file format is named**, because the player is holding that file: "paste the Top Gear JSON export (its Download JSON button) here", "this is not a Top Gear export: its schema is %s". The site is not named, and the PR asks the owner whether he wants it named in the Import dialog alone; the default is not. **Internal names stay** - `no_verdict`, `thisWeek`, the `qe-live-droptimizer` schema constants, module names, this document - and one player-facing string keeps a word from the list by design: the companion's refusals name `Data\QEVerdict.lua`, because a malformed file is no use to a player who is not told which file it is. **Two strings were looked at and deliberately left.** `ns.VaultPanel.SCENARIO_LABEL.thisWeek` stays "this week (vault upgraded, Catalyst used)": it names no source, and `ns.Roads.PLAN_LABEL`'s own comment already records why this label says more than the plain plan name - its line is read on its own, and the parenthetical is the only place the tab says what that scenario assumed. `ns.UpgradeMapPanel.ValueBadge`'s `prefix` argument survives for the same reason: the Vault tab passes a scenario name through it, and that is not a source. The guard is `spec/voice_spec.lua`, described in 6.
- **2026-09-09 (late night) - A `nil,` in a serialised list is a hole, not an item, and every walk steps over it.** Measured in the owner's live SavedVariables at 21:06: Blizzard writes a sparse array as positional entries with `nil,` in the gaps - a freed backpack slot between two filled ones - and the companion's parser keeps it as a null entry so the slot numbers after it stay right (`luaArray` returns `[a, null, c]`). The bag walk read `.link` on it and the run died with exit code 3 on every refresh, leaving the previous verdict file in place (C-4's own rule, working as designed). **Decided:** the parser keeps the null; the three walks (equipped, bags, vault rewards) skip a null entry. Nothing is inferred about the hole.
- **2026-09-10 - Which thirty items QE Live is asked about is the companion's decision, and the ones it leaves out are said on screen (C-8, WKE-558).** His Top Gear takes `patronCaps[patronStatus] || 30` items (`TopGear.tsx:177`) and allows a card to activate only below that cap (`:739`); the owner has 45+ candidates. **Decided:** the driver reads every card first, never deselects anything that arrived active, and spends the room in one order - vault items, then Catalyst clones, then bag items taken a slot at a time. **Nothing here is a healer value:** it never scores an item and never orders two by what they are worth. It decides which items QE Live is ASKED about, which is a question his engine does not answer and cannot. The round over the slots is a queue that cannot starve a slot, not a ranking - page order is his slot list, so twelve rings ahead of one weapon meant the weapon was never asked about. **The issue's own remedy was refused on the evidence:** it asked for active bag items to be deselected, and there are none - `SimCImportEngine.ts:712` and `Item.ts:174` make equipped items, vault options and their clones the whole of what is active at import, so a deselect would drop the character's own gear or a vault option out of the pool. **And the omission is published:** `excluded` rides in `Data/QEVerdict.lua` beside `qeSettings` and on every Top Gear document, and the Equip Now and Vault tabs print `QE Live did not consider N of your items: ...` in one shared wording, because a verdict that omits items must say so on screen. **The cap itself is untouched.** `patronStatus` is Voulk's rule and the fork is read and driven, never changed in what it decides (2026-09-07); whether the owner becomes a patron, lives within 30, or runs a second pass over the leftovers is his decision and is stated in the PR rather than made in it.

- **2026-09-14 - R-2 (WKE-563), what surface 1 settled. (a) The cache is built from the Upgrade Map tab's own MODEL, not from the road inputs.** `Panel.Model` has already called `ns.Roads.ForSlot` for every slot it draws, and a second call would be a second answer to one question; the map takes `section.roads` by reference and builds only the slots the model does not reach (a vault option in a slot with nothing worn and no drop walked). No difficulty filter is passed: the tab's dropdown narrows what the READER asked to see, and a bag hover is not that question. **(b) The plan sentence is written only for a road the plan is ABOUT**, which is the set group - a piece you hold, a Catalyst clone, a vault option, or what you wear. A journal drop, a crafted row and a delve row are in the `item` group and the plan takes no position on them, so there is no sentence; "Skip this one" on a dungeon drop would read as "skip the dungeon", which no document said. The issue asked for the item's part of the plan on every hover and this is the honest half of that. **(c) The block appears only for an item the map has an entry for** - every piece in your bags, every vault reward, every drop the walk found, every row the export carries. A chat link, a merchant's stock and an auction row get nothing, because "not rated" there would be a sentence about a question nobody asked. The phrase belongs on an item you HOLD, and the cache puts it there. **(d) One glow interface, one adapter per bag addon** (the owner's question, 2026-09-14): the core exposes `ns.Glow.Wants(key)`; adapters live in `UI/Bags/` and the `.toc` order is the preference order. Baganator goes in as a corner widget, never as an upgrade plugin, so Pawn's arrow keeps its slot. ElvUI, Bagnon and AdiBags are future adapters, never core changes. **(e) The glow is the ITEM's, not one road's**: an item is marked when ANY road into it is wanted, because taking any of them is what the mark is about. **(f) Two of the issue's own instructions were refuted against the committed files and the truth was built instead** - the Hide of Pestilence glows (it is the tier chest in every set of this week, the M3-15 correction of 2026-09-09, so its Catalyst road reads "in your best set", which is exactly what principle 12 marks; the bag piece the rating really leaves behind is the Miststalker's Spaulders), and `ns.Roads.TOOLTIP_ROADS` is counted by the tooltip itself rather than trusted from the model, because with exactly three groups the model can never hand over a fourth road and a cap that cannot be exceeded is a cap no test can prove.

- **2026-09-14 (night) - The two-reload floor stays, and the addon says when it is behind (R-6, WKE-578).** The owner asked whether the two reloads could go. They cannot: SavedVariables reach disk only on a reload or a logout and the client reads an addon's files only at load (2026-09-07, above), and nothing since has moved either. **What was decided instead is that the player is never asked to know that.** The rules the build follows: (a) **one string builder**, `ns.Drift.Model`, so the strip row, the launcher tooltip and anything later cannot disagree; (b) **the click runs the command's own function**, never a copy of it; (c) **`ReloadUI` is reached only from an OnClick**, which is the hardware event the client requires, and never from a timer; (d) **the nudge is drift in what you can EQUIP** - equipped and bags, never the bank, because opening a bank frame is a window opening and not gear moving, and never a non-gear item, which cannot produce an `ns.Inventory` record at all; (e) **an item the plan already knew is not drift however it moves**, because the key is the same; (f) **the wait line quotes only a run that actually rated something**, rounded to 15 s, and says `usually about a minute` when nothing has been measured; (g) **C-9's FAILED beats the wait**, because "it died" is a truer sentence than "it is coming"; (h) **the wait gives up after ten minutes** rather than standing all session over a watcher that was never restarted (§11, 2026-09-09) - that bound is a judgement, not a measurement, and is labelled as one in the code.
  - **Premise refuted 2026-09-14 (night): the issue's "fresh at login is the default story" does not hold on its own.** WKE-578 asked for the README and CHANGELOG to say "log out, and your plan is current next time you log in". They did not, because it was not true: **the addon took its snapshots only when `/lootpath refresh` was run**, so a logout flushed whatever was last captured, the companion fingerprinted an unchanged profile and skipped, and the next login loaded the same plan. The docs said the true version - refresh once before you log out - and the companion's log named which case each run was. **A capture at `PLAYER_LOGOUT` would make the issue's version true and is the obvious follow-up; it was not built here, because the issue said "add nothing to the client for this" and widening is not this lane's call.** **Closed 2026-09-15 by R-7 (WKE-579), below: the capture at logout is built, the sentence is true, and the docs say the plain version.**
  - **And the log line cannot say "run after logout" outright.** A logout and a plain `/reload` produce the same write and the SavedVariables record no difference between them. What the file does record, since R-6, is how each snapshot was TAKEN; the companion compares the newest `env` snapshot's stamp with the one the last written run read (`envCapturedAt` in the fingerprint state) and says `run after a logout or a plain reload - nothing new was captured`, which is what it can prove. **Superseded in the normal case by R-7:** the flush now carries snapshots LABELLED for it, so the line is read off the addon's own label and not inferred. **R-6 was right and R-7 forgot it:** R-7 labelled them `logout` and the log duly said `run after logout` for plain reloads, which is the very claim this bullet said could not be made. R-7a restored the discipline - the label is `flush` and the line is `run after a logout or reload (gear captured at the flush)`. The inferred line survives for what it can still be - an addon from before R-7, and a forced logout or reload in combat, where every capture refuses and the last snapshot flushes again.

- **2026-09-15 - The capture at logout, so "log out and your plan is current next login" is a plain sentence (R-7, WKE-579).** `PLAYER_LOGOUT` runs `ns.Companion.CaptureAtLogout`, the same four captures `/lootpath refresh` takes - `env`, `inventory`, `vault`, `currencies` - and nothing else. The rules it is built to, each of which is a decision and not a detail: (a) **no reload**, because the client is already leaving, and the sequence does not depend on `ReloadUI` existing at all - which is what makes it not a second copy of the refresh; (b) **nothing asynchronous**, because `PLAYER_LOGOUT` is Blizzard's own synchronous event (Ketho's `SystemDocumentation.lua`: `LiteralName = "PLAYER_LOGOUT", SynchronousEvent = true`) and the client stops running Lua after it, so a timer or a listener registered there is a promise nothing can keep - `ns.RunCapture` now registers its abandonment timer only when the capture has NOT already answered, which changes nothing for a capture that is genuinely still running and leaves the logout path timer-free; (c) **M3-16's vault interaction never happens at logout** - the capture is handed `{ skipInteract = "logout" }`, `OnUIInteract` is not called, `CloseInteraction` is not called, and the snapshot records `interact.skipped = "logout"` so the plain read says outright that it is the plain one rather than looking like a refresh that found nothing. This does not widen the second recorded exception to "captures only read"; it narrows it, by naming a place it does not apply; (d) **nothing can fail loudly** - each capture goes through `ns.RunCapture` (which pcalls the capture body) and a `pcall` of its own, and the event handler pcalls the whole sequence, so a capture that throws leaves the ones after it to run and a logout is the last thing the player sees rather than an error; (e) **the `env` snapshot is stamped `capturedOn = "logout"` with the sequence's own `logoutMs`**, written after the sequence because its cost is not known until it is over; nothing in the addon reads either, they are the evidence.

- **2026-09-15 (M3-16a, WKE-581) - `ReloadUI` belongs to the player, so the vault question moves to login and a reload that cannot be immediate becomes a click.** **The evidence is the owner's own screen** (§9): on the first `/lootpath refresh` after M3-16 merged, with the reward unclaimed, all three refresh lines printed - the wait line, `captured gear, bags, bank (closed), vault, currencies - reloading so the companion can read them`, and the second-reload line - and then `Interface action failed because of an AddOn`. Nothing reloaded. The captures sat in memory until the owner typed `/reload` himself. **Cause:** before M3-16 the chain was synchronous, so the `ReloadUI()` at its end was still inside the slash command's execution and the client attributed it to the player's hardware event. M3-16 made the vault capture wait for `WEEKLY_REWARDS_UPDATE`, and `step()` then called `ReloadUI()` from that event's continuation, which the client refuses - the same rule that makes R-6's wait line a click and forbids a timer. The chain's own comment (`the chain is captures, then the vault's wait, then ReloadUI`) described the defect. **So, four decisions:**
  - **(a) The question is asked at login, with no capture and no reload.** The first `PLAYER_ENTERING_WORLD` of a session, when `HasAvailableRewards()` is true and no activity carries a reward, runs M3-16's interaction and simply lets the client fill its own data. It is the same exception the owner allowed on 2026-09-14, one call earlier, and it does not widen it: nothing is captured, nothing is reloaded, `CloseInteraction` still runs on every path out including the timeout, and `ClaimReward`/`SelectReward` are still never named. By the time the player types the refresh the rewards are in `GetActivities()`, the vault capture has nothing to ask for, and the chain is synchronous again. **Never in combat**; a login that lands in a fight asks at `PLAYER_REGEN_ENABLED`. **Once per session** - `PLAYER_ENTERING_WORLD` fires after every loading screen, and a vault interaction per zone change is not what was allowed.
  - **(b) One copy of the interaction, not two.** `ns.VaultInteract` in `Captures.lua` is the whole exception in one function, called by the `vault` capture and by `ns.Companion.AskVaultAtLogin`. A second hand-written copy is exactly the thing that drifts out of step with the rule it is allowed under.
  - **(c) A refresh that still had to wait ASKS for its reload.** It never calls `ReloadUI` from the continuation. `StaticPopupDialogs["LOOTPATH_RELOAD_AFTER_REFRESH"]` shows `Gear captured. Reload to send it?` with Reload and Not now, and `OnAccept` reloads - a dialog button is a hardware event, and it is Blizzard's own pattern for exactly this (`GameDialogDefs.lua` lines 482-489 `TOO_MANY_LUA_ERRORS` and 568-575 `ADDON_ACTION_FORBIDDEN`, read 2026-09-15). No timeout on the box, because one that vanished on its own would leave four captures in memory with nothing on screen saying so. A client with no `StaticPopup_Show` is told the same thing in chat.
