  - **(d) R-6's stamp is written on both paths.** `Drift.RefreshStarting()` runs before the reload AND before the popup, so the strip's wait line stands with its own `ReloadUI` click behind it and a player who chose Not now still has somewhere to click. The line's wording (`rating your gear`) is R-6's and was not changed here; it is slightly ahead of itself on that path, and whether it should say something else on a refresh that has not been sent yet is a voice question for the owner (§11).
  - **Premise corrected: there is no `env` record saying combat refused.** The issue said a forced logout in combat "means the inventory scan refuses, as it already does; the `env` record says so". It does not: `ns.RunCapture` refuses EVERY capture in combat, before the capture body runs, so a logout in combat stores nothing at all - not even an `env` record - and the last snapshot flushes as it did before R-7. That is the client rule ("nothing runs in combat") and the sequence does not work around it; it returns `{ ok = false, reason = "combat" }` and touches nothing. The companion's inferred line is what covers that case, and the README names it.
  - **`trigger = "flush"` is the fourth value R-6's label can take** (it was `"logout"` until R-7a below), and the companion's fourth log line - `run after a logout or reload (gear captured at the flush)` - is read off it rather than inferred.

- **2026-09-15 (evening) - `PLAYER_LOGOUT` is an unload, not a logout, and the label says only what it can prove; and every capture list is bounded (R-7a, WKE-582).** Two faults in R-7, both read off the owner's 13:58 pull (`spec/fixtures/captures/Lootpath-20260915-135808.lua`, §9), neither a fault in what R-7 set out to do.
  - **(a) The reload is not a logout, so nothing claims it is.** `PLAYER_LOGOUT` fires when the interface is unloaded, which a `/reload` does as surely as a logout. R-7's sequence therefore ran on every reload and stamped each snapshot `logout`: the pull carried **five `env` snapshots with `capturedOn = "logout"` for at most one logout**, and the companion printed `run after logout (gear captured at logout)` for a plain reload - the one thing R-7's own documents said it could not claim. **What could tell them apart is the next load**, not the event: a `/reload` is followed by `PLAYER_ENTERING_WORLD` with `isInitialLogin = false, isReloadingUi = true` (Ketho's `SystemDocumentation.lua`), and a real logout by nothing in this session. By then the snapshot is written, so the answer is not to wait for it - it is to stop claiming. Everything the sequence stores says `flush`, which is true of both: `trigger`, `capturedOn`, the vault's `interact.skipped`, `logoutMs` renamed `flushMs`, `Companion.LOGOUT_TRIGGER`/`LOGOUT_VAULT_ARGS` renamed `FLUSH_*`, and `Companion.CaptureAtLogout` renamed `CaptureAtFlush`. The companion's line becomes `run after a logout or reload (gear captured at the flush)` and `run after logout` is retired. A snapshot still labelled `logout` came from an addon at exactly R-7 - the same event, the same four captures - and reads as the same line rather than as an unlabelled write.
  - **(b) It keeps running on reloads, deliberately.** That is not a cost to be tolerated: a reload's snapshot is the reason the SECOND reload of the refresh loop carries the gear the player is in. What the reloads cost is the retention, which (c) fixes.
  - **(c) Every capture keeps the newest four and nothing older.** `storeSnapshot` appended and nothing anywhere trimmed, so the file grew **6.1 MB (09-14 11:30) -> 7.1 MB (09-14 17:13) -> 11.0 MB (09-15 13:58)** - 35 `inventory` snapshots worth 5.85 MB of it, 37 `vault`, 35 `env`, 28 `currencies`, 3 `journal` - and the client reads and writes the whole file at every login and every reload. `ns.CAPTURE_HISTORY = 4` is the refresh loop's two flushes plus two to compare against, `table.remove(list, 1)` on the list AceDB already holds. **Why four and not one:** one snapshot cannot be compared with anything, and comparing a pull against what came before it is how three of this project's findings were made. **Why not "keep the journal walk":** the cold walk is already committed under `spec/fixtures/captures/`, which is where evidence lives; the live file is not an archive. A `/lootpath capture <name>` typed by hand is stored and bounded like any other, and `sync.ps1 -Pull` still copies whatever is there.
  - **The companion reads only the newest, and that is checked, not assumed.** `newestSnapshot` (`tools/companion/lib/simc-profile.js`) is the one reader, called three times for `env`, `inventory` and `vault`; `profile.js` reads the `env` one's `trigger` and `capturedAt`. Nothing walks an older snapshot. The `--snapshot` / `--env-snapshot` / `--vault-snapshot` indexes are debugging flags over a transcript file and are untouched. **The vault's "sixteen snapshots" reading of 2026-09-09 was a scratch script, not the companion.**
  - **Measured, for the first time on a real client:** the sequence's own `logoutMs` in the pull, five runs - **33.13, 22.39, 19.51, 32.02, 32.55 ms** - against the 250 ms the issue named as a budget. R-7's headless figure of 0.67-2.82 ms was the stub's world; this is the owner's bags. Nothing in §11 needs to say the cost is unknown any more.
  - **What this still cannot say:** whether the file stops growing. The bound is proven in the stub only, and the `captures` table was 9.0 MB of the 11.0 MB file, so 2.0 MB of it is something else the bound does not touch. The owner's next two reloads on the merged build are the measurement (§0).

- **2026-09-15 (night) - on reset day the Great Vault WINDOW generates the rewards and the addon's interaction does not; the flush stops reading the vault; the wait says itself where it can be seen (M3-16b, WKE-583).** Read from the owner's own SavedVariables of 2026-09-15 14:27 local, the vault half of which is committed as `spec/fixtures/captures/Lootpath-20260915-142722-vault.lua` (§9).
  - **(a) The finding, and the thing that is NOT built because of it.** M3-16 and M3-16a both assumed that `OnUIInteract` plus one `WEEKLY_REWARDS_UPDATE` fetches whatever the client is holding back. On reset day it fetches nothing: four refreshes asked, the client answered every time (66.7 / 77.1 / 113.2 / 130.9 ms, `timedOut` false), and the read after the answer was the read before it - 10 activities, every `rewards = {}`, 0 reward links, `HasGeneratedRewards()` false. The owner then OPENED the Great Vault window (it opened; on 2026-09-08/09 it would not) and captured with it shown: **11 activities, 5 carrying rewards, `HasGeneratedRewards()` and `CanClaimRewards()` true, 9 reward links.** Six seconds after he shut it, the flush read the same 11 and 9 - the data stays in the client once the window has generated it. **So "held back until something interacts" was the right reading of last week's mid-week state and is not the reading of reset day**, and the addon does not get a second exception to chase it: the owner's decision of 2026-09-14 allowed `OnUIInteract`/`CloseInteraction`, and nothing here widens it. What is built instead is a sentence: `VaultPanel.OPEN_VAULT_NOTE`, shown when `HasAvailableRewards()` is true and `HasGeneratedRewards()` is false, says **"This week's vault rewards have not been generated yet. Open the Great Vault, then refresh: opening it is what makes the client fetch them."** The mid-week `WITHHELD_REWARDS_NOTE` keeps naming the refresh, because there the ask really is the remedy.
  - **(b) The capture reads every update, not the first.** Blizzard's `WeeklyRewardsMixin:OnEvent` re-reads on every `WEEKLY_REWARDS_UPDATE` while the frame is shown; the capture settled on the first and closed, so one update carrying nothing ended the whole ask. It now reads on each update inside `ns.VAULT_INTERACT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` and settles when a read CARRIES rewards or the bound ends, recording `interact.updates = { { ms, activities, links }, ... }` so the next transcript can say whether a second or third update ever brings what the first did not. `timedOut` now means "the bound ended" rather than "nothing fired", and `updateFired` says whether the client answered at all. `CloseInteraction` runs once, at the end, on every path out - unchanged.
  - **(c) The flush never captures the vault.** R-7 read it plainly there and labelled the read; the cost was measured on reset day. The refresh at 19:09:29Z asked and stored its answer, the flush at the refresh's own reload stored the same empty read at 19:09:31Z, and being the newest that is what the companion built the profile from: `0 vault`, `warning: no generated Great Vault reward`, and a reset-day plan with no vault in it. A read that cannot ask can only shadow one that did, so `Companion.FLUSH_CAPTURES` is `env`, `inventory`, `currencies`, and `skipInteract` / `ns.VAULT_SKIPPED_REASON` are gone with the case they existed for. The vault snapshot is the refresh's and the login ask's: the reads that ask.
  - **(d) The companion chooses its vault snapshot.** `chooseVaultSnapshot` (`tools/companion/lib/simc-profile.js`): of the snapshots whose `secondsUntilWeeklyReset` puts them in the same reward period as the newest (60 s of slack for the two clocks), the newest that carries reward links wins, and among those the newest whose `interact.attempted` is true. Links are the point of the snapshot - a read with none of them cannot be the better answer - and the period bound is what stops last week's rewards being carried into this week. The reason travels onto the built profile as `vaultChoice` and the companion logs `vault read used: ...`, because `0 vault` was a choice between snapshots and nothing said so. **This is broader than the issue asked for** ("prefer a read that asked"), and it has to be: the read that carries the owner's nine links is the hand capture he took with the window open, and it did not ask - its gate said `the activities already carry rewards`.
  - **(e) The wait indicator, in the owner's words.** He said it on 2026-09-15: "after I do a refresh and the screen loads and I'm back in game, I'm assuming the refresh is done. If that is not the case we should have an indicator letting the player know the refresh is still happening." Three surfaces, one `ns.Drift.Model`: a chat line at the first load after a refresh; the minimap badge, which R-6 kept off during the wait and which is now on for it (with the window shut the launcher is the only surface there is, and the badge goes out the moment the wait does); and the strip, where **the wait is now the FIRST clause and the only one**. His screen read `...on Druid - companion, written 4 hours ago - vault pick: everything upgraded - companion: run st...`, cut at the window's width, with R-6's wait line on the row below and past the cut. The four facts it displaces move into the strip's own tooltip, the way V-2 moved the content type; the strip's row answers the click while it carries the wait, so `click to load it` is true of the row it is written on; and the nudge keeps the second row, where a player who has not acted yet reads it.

- **2026-09-15 (night) - `ns.EpochFromISO` pairs its two `mktime` calls at `isdst = false`, and the stub grows a modelled clock (V-4, WKE-589).**
  - **The defect.** `time(t)` is C's `mktime` and reads `t.isdst`: absent means "decide for yourself", `false` means "these fields are standard time", `true` means daylight time. `EpochFromISO` built its offset from `date("!*t", now)`, which writes `isdst = false`, and read the stamp from a table that said nothing. The two interpretations were different, so while daylight time was in effect the offset came out an hour too large and every stamp was parsed an hour early. The comment said "nothing here assumes a timezone"; it assumed standard time. **Every age and every wall clock the addon prints goes through this function**, and the owner read the result on 2026-09-15 at 17:22 CDT: `companion, written 62 minutes ago`, and `(16:22)` for a 17:20 event (§9).
  - **The fix, and why this one.** Both tables now say `isdst = false`, so the standard-time interpretation is applied twice and cancels exactly. Three candidates were measured against musl's own `mktime` and `localtime` under `TZ=America/Chicago` (`docker run -e TZ=America/Chicago lootpath-lua`, tzdata installed), on a two-minute-old stamp in summer, the same in winter, a stamp across the November fall-back, and a stamp half a year old: **both-`false` was exact in all four; letting `mktime` decide on both sides, and matching the offset table to the local clock's own `isdst`, were both exact in the first three and an hour out on the fourth.** `Lootpath/UI/VaultPanel.lua`'s `utcOffsetSeconds`/`Panel.EpochFromISO` was read and has ALWAYS paired them this way, so the Vault tab's `reset in` was never affected and nothing there changed; the one function now matches the other.
  - **The stub grew a clock, because it had to.** CI's container is UTC and Alpine carries no timezone database at all, so a fault that only appears during daylight time cannot be made red against the real `os.date` and `os.time` on any machine that runs this suite. `world.setClock(zone)` (`spec/stubs/wow.lua`) replaces both globals with a pair modelling one zone with one daylight rule: `date("!*t")` answers UTC fields with `isdst = false`, `date("*t")` answers local fields with the instant's own `isdst`, and `time(t)` reads `t.isdst` the way `mktime` reads `tm_isdst`. Every one of those three behaviours is asserted in `spec/stubs_spec.lua` against numbers first read from musl, so the model is checked against a real clock rather than against itself. `spec/helpers/addon.lua` carries the owner's own zone (`H.CHICAGO`, `H.chicagoClock`). Nothing else about a timezone is modelled and nothing else should be: one zone with one rule is the whole harness this class of fault needs.
  - **What it was silently breaking, beyond the clocks.** R-6's wait compared `refreshStartedAt` - written correctly in UTC by `date("!...")` - against a parse running an hour late, so the elapsed time was 3600 s too large, `Drift.WAIT_GIVE_UP_SECONDS` (600) was cleared on the FIRST check, and `Drift.Waiting` deleted the stamp before any surface could draw it. **The wait could not have appeared on the owner's client at any point since daylight time began.** Proven red: with the fix removed, the thirty-second wait guard answers `behind` instead of `wait`. §11's open question about R-6's wait is closed by this, not by an eye test.


- **2026-09-15 (night) - a vault reward's phrase is decided by the document's record of its POOL, never by the document's existence (R-3d, WKE-584; C-13, the profile's vault count).**
  - **The premise that was too wide.** C-8 (WKE-558) read in QE Live's own importer that every vault option is active from the moment it is imported (`SimCImportEngine.ts:712`). The vault road took that to mean a reward the plan's document never mentions was in the run and was passed over, so `road.phrase = entry and PHRASE_NOT_IN_BEST_SET or PHRASE_NO_RATING`. **The premise is about a reward the run IMPORTED, and the run imports nothing the profile did not carry.** On reset day before the player opens the Great Vault - and any week the client withholds the links and no login restores them - the companion builds a profile with no vault section, QE Live is shown no vault card, and the document's silence about the reward means it never existed to the rating. The owner read the consequence on 2026-09-15 (section 9): `not in your best set` and `Skip this one, the plan uses your Miststalker legs`, over a 315 vault reward rated 80 minutes before the vault existed. Principle 3 forbids exactly that.
  - **C-13: the count travels.** The companion already knew - `profile.counts.vault`, and it logs `no generated Great Vault reward ... so the profile has no vault section` - but only the log knew. The verdict file now carries `profileVaultCount`, file-level like `scenarioNote` and for the same reason: the profile is the RUN's and not any one document's. A **number, not a flag**, because zero is the whole case and a field written only when it is interesting cannot say zero; absent means the run did not say, which is every file before C-13 and every paste. `ns.Companion` carries it onto each Top Gear verdict, the way it carries the note.
  - **The rule, in one function.** `ns.Roads.VaultConsidered(entry, inputs, reward)` answers **true** (some pass's `considered` holds the reward's key, by C-10's identity join through `ns.Companion.IsConsidered`), **false** (`profileVaultCount == 0`, or a pool was recorded and holds it nowhere), or **nil** (no pool and no count on record - every pre-C-11 file and every paste, where C-8's premise still stands unchanged). The count outranks the pools, because a pool that held no vault card and a pool nobody recorded look identical from the road. A vault option is a baseline card, so it is in every pass's pool or in none of them; the plan's own document and its later passes are all asked, which cannot be wrong either way.
  - **What `false` changes.** The road's phrase becomes `not rated - new since the last refresh`, it joins the **no-rating group** rather than the set group, `Roads.IsForward` and `Roads.IsRated` are false so it does not glow and carries no imperative, and its sentence is `Roads.NOT_RATED_YET_SENTENCE`: **"The plan hasn't rated this yet. Refresh, then look again."** That sentence is asked for BEFORE the group tests in `Roads.ItemSentence`, because the reward is in the vault rather than in the bags and its road is no longer in the set group - the pair of facts that would otherwise leave an offered item sentence-less against principle 16. And where the profile carried no vault at all, `Roads.PlanSentence` replaces the whole Vault tab headline with **"The plan was rated before your vault was generated. Refresh."**: a plan chosen without this week's vault in front of it is not a decision about the vault, and reading a pick out of it on that tab says it is.
  - **Proven red, one at a time:** the count branch (the owner's three rewards go back to `not in your best set` in the set group), the plan sentence, the pool walk (three guards), the `ItemSentence` branch, and the `nil` bound - which, broken, also reddens `spec/roads_spec.lua:781` and `spec/tooltip_spec.lua:428`, the two guards that have protected C-8's reading since 558. On the companion side: the payload field, and the `!== undefined` test that makes a zero a zero (`if (profileVaultCount)` drops it, and the end-to-end fingerprint test goes red with it).

## 9. Verification ladder

**Seen 2026-09-15, 14:5x local (the owner's screenshot), reset day, the Great Vault window open and the reward unclaimed, `main` at `61b27fc`, over the verdict of 14:09 - 80 minutes old, built from a profile carrying `0 vault` (WKE-583).** Hovering the vault's Enigmatic Dreamwatcher's Leggings (271527, 315, the tier legs, a +22% upgrade by Pawn):

```
Lootpath - Legs - rated 80 minutes ago - /lootpath refresh
Skip this one, the plan uses your Miststalker legs.
Vault - open now - Enigmatic Dreamwatcher's Leggings (315) - not in your best set - reset in 6d 19h
Other roads for this slot
Raid - Coiled Hex Legguards (344) - The Coiled Altar, The Venomous Abyss, Mythic raid - +2.09%
Why this? - /lootpath map
```

Two of those lines are the addon's opinion about an item the rating never saw, which principle 3 forbids; the header's `/lootpath refresh` (R-3b) was the only honest word on the block. **After R-3d (WKE-584), over the same reward and the same 9-link vault - snapshot 12 of `Lootpath-20260915-142722-vault.lua` - with the run's `profileVaultCount = 0`, read off a test rather than a screen:**

```
Lootpath - Legs - rated 5 hours ago - /lootpath refresh
The plan hasn't rated this yet. Refresh, then look again.
Vault - open now - Enigmatic Dreamwatcher's Leggings (315) - not rated - new since the last refresh - reset in 6d 19h
Other roads for this slot
Keep (what you wear) - Enigmatic Dreamwatcher's Leggings (295) - what you wear now - in your best set
Why this? - /lootpath map
```

(The separators above are the middle dot on screen; they are written plain here.) The same file WITHOUT the count reads the old block word for word, which is what proves the one field decides it. 1190 busted tests, 227 companion tests, five gates green. **Nothing of R-3d has been on a real screen.**

**Measured 2026-09-15 (night), V-4 (WKE-589), against musl's own `mktime` and `localtime` under `TZ=America/Chicago` (Alpine plus tzdata, inside `lootpath-lua`).** On the UTC fields of an instant in September, `os.time` answered **auto 1789881631, `isdst=false` 1789885231, `isdst=true` 1789881631** - `false` an hour later than the other two. In January, **auto 1768537231, `false` 1768537231, `true` 1768533631** - the other way round. The addon's own buggy computation, run over the same pair, was **3600 s early in September and exact in January**, which is the owner's 17:22 screen reproduced in a shell. The three candidate fixes over four cases (a two-minute-old stamp in summer, the same in winter, one across the November fall-back, one half a year old): **both tables `isdst = false` was exact in all four; both absent, and matching the local clock's `isdst`, were exact in three and 3600 s out on the fourth.** Headlessly, on the stub's modelled Central clock, the fix removed: `companion, written 62 minutes ago` for a stamp 120 s old and `companion: profile unchanged, no run (16:20)` for a 17:20 event - the owner's two strings, off a test. With it: `2 minutes ago` and `(17:20)`. And R-6's wait, thirty seconds in, answered `behind` without the fix and `wait` with it. 1177 busted tests, 225 companion tests, five gates green.

**Seen 2026-09-15 17:22 local (the owner's screenshot), after a reload and a refresh on M3-16b:** no chat line; the strip `companion, written 62 minutes ago`, its tooltip `The companion's last run: profile unchanged since 2026-09-15T22:20:31Z; the verdict file is current (16:22)`. 22:20Z is 17:20 CDT, two minutes before the screen: **the addon's ages and clocks are one hour old** (WKE-589, the `isdst` offset in `ns.EpochFromISO`). The silence was C-4's skip: the profile was unchanged since the 17:20 run, the companion skipped in under a second, and M3-16b's chat line fires only while a wait is on (WKE-590). Equip Now: 15 already best, including the Maledict Vest, Lynx's Buckle, Circlet and Oculus the owner has since equipped from the `asOffered` swaps, and the leggings now at 321.

**Measured 2026-09-15 16:20 local, the owner's `upgrade` capture at a crest vendor (`spec/fixtures/captures/Lootpath-20260915-162015.lua`, read with the companion's parser):** `frameShown = true`, 128 candidates walked, `durationMs` 20,059, no secret, seven functions named and `UpgradeItem` not among them. `CanUpgradeItem` true for 20 items; for each, `GetItemUpgradeItemInfo()` returned `name`, `currUpgrade`/`maxUpgrade`, `customUpgradeString`, `maxItemLevel`, `minItemLevel`, `highWatermarkSlot`, `upgradeCostTypesForSeason`, `upgradeLevelInfos[]` with `upgradeLevel`, `itemLevelIncrement`, `moneyCost`, `itemCostsToUpgrade`, `levelStats[]`, `currencyCostsToUpgrade[]` (`currencyID`, `cost`, `discountInfo`). **Costs:** 20 of one currency a step (3444 on Champion, 3445 on Hero, 3442 on Adventurer) plus 300,000 / 400,000 / 100,000 copper; the current level's row is the watermark row at `cost 0, isDiscounted true`. Lunar Raiment Champion 4/6 at 302: two steps of 20 x 3444 to 308; Seed of Radiant Hope Hero 2/6 at 308: 20 x 3445 a step to 321. **The event fired for 10 of 20 and timed out at 2 s for 10, with the info complete either way** (`waitedMs` 12.7 / 1,001 / 1,995). **`CanUpgradeItem` answered false for the worn leggings at Hero 5/6**, so it is not the gate. WKE-588 (M3-17b).

**Measured 2026-09-15 16:08 local, R-7a's bound on the owner's client (two `/reload`s on `65b0b08`, then `sync.ps1 -Pull`; the pull read with the companion's parser):** the file went **11.00 MB (13:58) -> 11.93 MB (14:27) -> 4.07 MB (16:08)**. Snapshot counts: vault 4, env 4, currencies 4, inventory 4 (all `trigger = "flush"`, the newest at 16:08:07), journal 3, glow 1, spike 1. Of the 4.07 MB, 1.89 MB precedes the captures table (the addon's other saved data: the journal's sources and slots, the namespace walks) and stays as it was. The bound holds and the file is no longer growing with reloads. The three pulls of the day sit untracked in the main checkout, none committed (11, 12 and 4 MB).

**Seen 2026-09-15 ~16:00 local (the owner's screenshots), the 14:55 verdict on screen after the reload: R-3c's rule on a real screen.** The worn leggings at 318: `Keep this on.` / `Keep (what you wear), Enigmatic Dreamwatcher's Leggings (318), upgraded to 321, what you wear now, in your best set at 321` - the identity by item ID and the Upgrade road reading `maxed`, both right. Equip Now: 14 already best, 1 to swap (Chest: the worn 302 Lunar Raiment to the bagged 276 Maledict Vest, the `asOffered` document's own answer), Legs 318 `already equipped`; the header still `23 of your items weren't rated this time` (586). **The owner's own words on the wait:** "after I do a refresh and the screen loads and I'm back in game, I'm assuming the refresh is done. If that is not the case we should have an indicator letting the player know the refresh is still happening" - the wait line exists (R-6) and is past the strip's width; 583 part 5 now carries the three surfaces (a chat line at the first reload, the minimap badge, the strip's first clause).

**Measured 2026-09-15 14:31 local, reset day, the first plan rated WITH the week's vault (the refresh after the owner's Great Vault open; `Data/companion.log`, the verdict read with a scratch parser):** `profile: 15 equipped, 38 in bags, 0 in the bank, 3 vault`; 32 Top Gear documents, 1,593 KB. The highest set in the file, `thisWeek` Raid **6115.012**, holds the vault's Enigmatic Dreamwatcher's Leggings (271527) **crested to 321**, the Lynx Spaulders clone (271526 at 295) and the Hide of Pestilence clone (271531 at 308) with the worn staff at 321; Dungeon 5912.094 the same shape; `asOffered` Raid with the leggings at 315 is 5920.541. The owner's tooltip on the vault leggings, with the window open: `Grab this from the vault and crest it.` / `Vault, open now, Enigmatic Dreamwatcher's Leggings (315), upgraded to 321, in your best set at 321`. The first reset-day answer the addon has given before a claim; the owner asked whether to trust it and the file says the same thing the screen does.

**Measured 2026-09-15 14:09 local, reset day, the reward unclaimed, the owner's first refresh on M3-16a (the live SavedVariables read with `lua-savedvariables.js`; `Data/companion.log`):** the refresh's own vault capture (`trigger = "refresh"`): before the ask `hasAvailableRewards = true`, `canClaimRewards = false`, `areRewardsForCurrentRewardPeriod = true`, **`hasGeneratedRewards = false`**, ten activities (3 raid, 3 dungeon, 3 world, 1 concession; every gear row without rewards; an earlier read of this note said "two", a parse error), no links; the ask: `attempted`, **`updateFired = true` in 113 ms**, not timed out, and `after` identical - the same ten, no links. **Then, 14:27:16, `capture vault` with the Great Vault window open** (`frameShown = true`, `Blizzard_WeeklyRewards` loaded; the window opened, which it would not on 2026-09-08/09): **11 activities, 5 carrying rewards, `hasGeneratedRewards = true`, `canClaimRewards = true`, 9 reward links** (Preyhunter's Lantern, Enigmatic Dreamwatcher's Leggings, Kyrakka's Searing Embers, five Mythic Keystones, a Thalassian Token of Merit); the capture's gate said `the activities already carry rewards` and did not ask. The flush at 14:27:22, six seconds after the window closed: the same 11, 5, 9. **On reset day the real open generates the rewards; the interaction does not, and the data persists once generated.** The login ask the same morning: answered in 233 and 265 ms, same emptiness. Two seconds after the refresh's capture, R-7's flush at the refresh's reload stored a plain vault read (`skipInteract = "logout"`, `"flush"` since R-7a) that the companion used: `0 vault`, 30 documents in 181 s, no vault in the plan. On 2026-09-09 a fresh login had restored five links and nine activities. So the 2026-09-10 reading ("held back until something interacts") described a mid-week state; on reset day the client has not received the week's vault at all and one interaction plus the first update does not fetch it. WKE-583 (M3-16b); the owner's open-then-capture is the deciding transcript.

- **Six gates green** (luacheck, StyLua, LuaLS, busted **1064 successes / 0 failures**, companion **209 tests / 0 failures**). **Eleven guards proven red and restored:** letting `deselect` touch a baseline card (3 red), removing the grid-moved check (1), dropping the `done` accumulation so a card is asked twice (4), removing the no-progress break (1), letting an Upgrade Finder document claim pass 2 (1), removing the `topGearPasses` validation (1), leaving the bound out of the fingerprint (1), letting a later pass reach the plan shelf (6), making `Existing` ignore the pass (3), not clearing the old run's later passes on a new pass 1 (1), and blanking `Roads.LaterPass` (3) and the beats-worn sentence (1).

What a second pass costs in wall clock, how many passes the owner's real profile needs per scenario, and how large `Data/QEVerdict.lua` becomes when a Top Gear export is ~16-17 KB and there are up to four passes per content type per scenario are all owner-run figures. **Measured 2026-09-14 late night (M3-17, WKE-574), headlessly, in busted.** The `upgrade` capture has never met a vendor; every figure here is from the test harness and the exported docs, and the whole point of the capture is that the client's real answers are not known yet.

- **`ns.CopyRaw`'s depth guard truncates a crest cost's discount, and that is what shaped the record.** `MAX_COPY_DEPTH` is 10. The first build stored each item's four probes under a `read = { ... }` sublevel, which put `discountInfo` - whether this cost is discounted, and by how much - at depth 11: the first run of the new tests came back with the string `<max-depth>` in its place, and the assertion on `discountInfo.isDiscounted` failed. Nothing was hidden (the marker is right there in the transcript), but the discount is half of what a crest road would have to say, so the sublevel went away rather than the guard: the probes are written flat onto the item's record and `discountInfo` sits at exactly 10. A real return that nests deeper than the documented shape will still come back marked, not lost.
- **Five guards proven red and restored:** the frame-open refusal flipped to `if false` (1 red: the capture ran with the window shut); the `CanUpgradeItem` filter flipped to `if false` (1 red: an item the vendor refuses was put in the window anyway); `record.cleared` dropped (2 red: the clear count fell from 3 to 1 on both the answered and the timed-out path); `eventFired`/`timedOut` pinned to the happy answer (2 red: a client that never fires `ITEM_UPGRADE_MASTER_SET_ITEM` read as a fast one); and `local spend = C_ItemUpgrade.UpgradeItem` added to the file (1 red: the source assertion found it at byte 43881).
- **Five gates green** (luacheck, StyLua, LuaLS, busted **1062 successes / 0 failures**, companion **194 tests / 0 failures**).
- **What is NOT measured, and is the human-required step:** whether `SetItemUpgradeFromLocation` needs `ItemUpgradeFrame` open at all, whether it fires `ITEM_UPGRADE_MASTER_SET_ITEM` (Blizzard's own frame re-reads on that event, which is the only reason to think it does), how long the client takes to answer, whether the info comes back nil for an item the vendor will not take, and whether any of it taints. The capture records all of it per item (`eventFired`, `timedOut`, `waitedMs`, `hyperlink` beside the read so a stale answer is visible) and `ns.UPGRADE_SET_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (2) is a bound, not a figure. Nothing reads an `upgrade` snapshot until the owner's transcript is committed.

**Read off the owner's own screen, 2026-09-15 morning (M3-16a, WKE-581)**, on `main` at `f82d789`, the first `/lootpath refresh` after M3-16 merged, with the reward unclaimed:

```
Lootpath: waiting for the vault: the client is holding the rewards back, so Lootpath asked for them the way the Great Vault window does. The reload follows.
Lootpath: captured gear, bags, bank (closed), vault, currencies - reloading so the companion can read them.
Lootpath: reload again when it says done; that second /lootpath refresh is all that is left - the file it writes is imported as the game comes back.
Interface action failed because of an AddOn
```

**No reload happened**, and the captures sat in memory until the owner's own `/reload` flushed them. This is one measurement and it settles two things: the vault wait DOES happen on a withheld-rewards week (the first line printed), and a `ReloadUI` called from its continuation is refused. It does NOT say whether `WEEKLY_REWARDS_UPDATE` fired or how long the wait took - the snapshot that would have carried `interact.waitedMs` is in the SavedVariables the manual reload flushed, and no one has pulled it. **The human-required step, now answering both M3-16's open question and M3-16a's:** one plain login on a build carrying M3-16a, then `/lootpath capture env`, `/reload` and `tools\sync.ps1 -Pull`. The `env` snapshot's `vaultLoginAsk` block says whether the login ask ran (`askedAtLogin`), whether the client answered (`updateFired`), and how long it waited (`waitedMs`) - which is M3-16's five-second bound measured for the first time. A `/lootpath refresh` in the same session then says whether the chain stayed synchronous: an immediate reload and no box is the whole point of the issue; a box means the login ask did not get there first, and the transcript says why.

**Measured 2026-09-09/10 (M3-16, WKE-557), in the owner's live SavedVariables** - sixteen `vault` snapshots read with a scratch script over the companion's own parser, the reward unclaimed the whole time. This is the measurement the vault interaction is built on.

- **Up to 2026-09-09 13:59Z (a fresh client start):** `HasAvailableRewards()` true, `GetActivities()` carrying `rewards` on the earned activities, **five** `rewardLinks`.
- **From 2026-09-09 22:40Z on (after the owner played: World 2/2 at level 1, Dungeons 1/1 at level 8, Concession 2/3):** `HasAvailableRewards()` still **true** and `CanClaimRewards()` false, but `GetActivities()` lists **this** period's progress with `rewards = {}` on every activity, so `rewardLinks` is empty, the companion writes `warning: no generated Great Vault reward in the SavedVariables, so the profile has no vault section`, the Vault tab has nothing to rank and QE Live is never asked about the vault.
- **A `/reload` does not bring them back; a full client restart does.** 2026-09-09 22:48 local: the owner exited the game, logged back in and ran `/lootpath refresh`; that snapshot carries the same five rewards as Tuesday's, the same `itemDBID`s, and the companion built `4 vault` and ran QE Live.
- **Blizzard's own frame says why** (`Blizzard_WeeklyRewards.lua` under `.luals/`, read 2026-09-14): `WeeklyRewardsMixin:OnShow` calls `C_WeeklyRewards.OnUIInteract()` and re-reads `GetActivities()` on `WEEKLY_REWARDS_UPDATE`; `OnHide` calls `CloseInteraction()`; and `UpdatePreviousClaim` shows the "rewards from last week" notice exactly when `HasAvailableRewards()` and not `AreRewardsForCurrentRewardPeriod()`. The server pushes the reward list at login and on interaction, and once the client has the new period's progress the previous period's unclaimed rewards drop out until the UI interacts. The SimulationCraft addon's users hit the same thing, which is why it tells them to open the vault first - and **the owner cannot open the Great Vault window on this client at all** (reported 2026-09-08/09, cause unknown; `Blizzard_WeeklyRewards` shows as not loaded in every snapshot).
- **What is NOT measured yet, and is the human-required step:** whether `WEEKLY_REWARDS_UPDATE` fires after `OnUIInteract()` on a client outside the vault window, and how long it takes. The capture records both (`interact.updateFired`, `interact.waitedMs`, `interact.timedOut`) and the bound is `ns.VAULT_INTERACT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 5`; the owner's first `/lootpath refresh` on the synced build, with the reward unclaimed, is the transcript that answers it. No figure for it is written anywhere in the code or here.

Skip the vault shoulders.` with the footnote `The plan would catalyst the Hide chest too, but you've only got one charge.` The slot lines are `Catalyst your Lynx shoulders, skip the vault ones, no crests here.` and `Grab the Worldroot from the vault and crest it.`

**Three of the issue's own figures were checked against those files first, and one was wrong.**
- **"Keep at 2.74% behind" is not this plan's.** 2.741789% is real and it is alternative 12 of the `maxed` Dungeon document (`qqrqsbudcszh`), the only set in any committed file that changes the weapon slot and nothing else. Under `thisWeek` no differential does that; the best set carrying the worn weapon is differential 7 at 1.729%, and under `maxed` it is 1.717%. Both are asserted.
- **The canvas's "+3.08% / +4.71% / +2.18%" round up.** The export says 3.070, 4.704 and 2.171, so the rows read `+3.07%`, `+4.70%` and `+2.17%`. Every other canvas figure (0%, +0.38%, +3.63%, +0.32%, +2.62%, 1.73%) reproduces exactly.
- **The raid two-hander's difficulty is Mythic, not Heroic.** The committed walk previews 268205 at 324 on difficulty 16 and at 311 on difficulty 15, so the row's own difficulty label is "Mythic raid". The canvas's "Heroic" was not read from the walk.

**Still not readable, confirmed again here:** the crest cost for a vault reward or a drop (permanent), the crest type for an owned item ("not read"), the delve key and which delves are Bountiful, crafting materials, and an item's own type word ("the staff") - the last of which is why the plan sentence says "the Worldroot" and `ns.Roads.ShortName` takes a `subType` only when a caller has one. **There is still no committed transcript carrying the Catalyst charge by ID** (§11), so the 1 of 8 in the spec is driven through the stub the way `spec/currencies_spec.lua` drives it, off the owner's own 2026-09-08 tooltip; the crest counts on the same rows are the 23:04 transcript's own.

**Verified (source read or measured):** QE Live export schema and sign conventions; Upgrade Finder has no export; SimC addon exports the vault; secret values lift out of combat and SavedVariables flush on reload/logout (Healper spike, in-client); toolchain versions/licences above.

**Verified 2026-09-05 from public sources (WKE-514 session; each still gets re-measured by its capture, but none is a guess any more):**
- **Interface 120100, build 69587**: `GetBuildInfo()` in the Healper spike's SavedVariables (2026-09-01) and `.build.info`. See §2.
- **Where the game APIs live.** Of the six named APIs, five are in Blizzard's exported docs (`C_EncounterJournal.GetLootInfoByIndex(index, encounterIndex?)`, `C_EncounterJournal.SetPreviewMythicPlusLevel(level)`, `C_WeeklyRewards.GetActivities(type?)`, `C_Item.GetItemStats(itemLink)`, `C_Container.GetContainerItemInfo(containerIndex, slotIndex)`). **`EJ_SetLootFilter` and the other `EJ_*` globals are not in Blizzard's generated docs**; Ketho carries them in `Annotations/Core/Data/Wiki.lua`. They are real: Blizzard's own `Blizzard_EncounterJournal/Mainline/Blizzard_EncounterJournal.lua` (Gethe/wow-ui-source, `live`, 3731 lines) calls `EJ_SetLootFilter` (2 sites), `EJ_GetNumLoot` (2), `EJ_SelectInstance` (1), `EJ_SetDifficulty` (5), `EJ_SelectTier` (1), `EJ_GetNumTiers` (1), `EJ_GetInstanceByIndex` (8), `C_EncounterJournal.GetLootInfoByIndex` (4), registers `EJ_LOOT_DATA_RECIEVED` (that spelling; payload `itemID` per Ketho `Event.lua`) and gates its re-read on `EJ_IsLootListOutOfDate()`. **It never calls `SetPreviewMythicPlusLevel`** (0 sites), so that function's effect on loot is unobserved until M3-2.
- **`EJ_GetInstanceForGameMap` (named in WKE-522) does not exist** in Blizzard's docs, Ketho, or wowless. The real function is `C_EncounterJournal.GetInstanceForGameMap(mapID) -> journalInstanceID?`, whose doc comment says it takes a game mapID, "not a uiMapID". `C_ChallengeMode.GetMapUIInfo(mapChallengeModeID)` returns `name, id, timeLimit, texture, backgroundTexture, mapID`. Candidate path: `GetMapTable()` -> `GetMapUIInfo().mapID` -> `GetInstanceForGameMap`. Whether those two `mapID`s are the same kind is for the M3-2 capture.
- **`EncounterJournalItemInfo` fields**: itemID, encounterID?, name?, itemQuality?, filterType?, icon?, slot?, armorType?, link?, handError?, weaponTypeError?, displayAsPerPlayerLoot?, displayAsVeryRare?, displayAsExtremelyRare?, displaySeasonID?. **No item level.**
- **Equip function**: `C_Item.EquipItemByName(itemInfo, dstSlot?)` in Blizzard docs; global `EquipItemByName` is in Ketho's `Blizzard_Deprecated/Deprecated_ItemScript.lua`.
- **`ContainerItemInfo` fields** (Blizzard docs): iconFileID, stackCount, isLocked, quality?, isReadable, hasLoot, hyperlink, isFiltered, hasNoValue, itemID, isBound, itemName. `C_Item.GetDetailedItemLevelInfo(itemInfo) -> actualItemLevel, previewLevel, sparseItemLevel`.
- **Bonus-ID layout in `item:` strings** (simulationcraft/simc-addon `core.lua`, pushed 2026-08-21, the parser QE Live's input comes through): fields split on `:`; `OFFSET_ITEM_ID = 2`, `OFFSET_ENCHANT_ID = 3`, `OFFSET_CONTEXT = 12`, `OFFSET_BONUS_ID = 13` is the **count**, the IDs follow it, then a variable list of modifier type/value pairs, then gem bonuses. M1-1's parser is written against captured links and cross-checked with this.
- **Vault**: `WeeklyRewardActivityInfo { type, index, threshold, progress, id, activityTierID, level, claimID?, raidString?, rewards[] }`; `WeeklyRewardActivityRewardInfo { type (Enum.CachedRewardType), id, quantity, itemDBID? }` - **`itemDBID` is nil for currency rewards**, and the SimC source notes `GetItemHyperlink` "may return nothing even for a valid itemDBID". SimC's vault export is `core.lua` lines 1294-1305 exactly. `C_DateAndTime.GetSecondsUntilWeeklyReset()` exists for M3-3's stale-verdict note. `C_Secrets.HasSecretRestrictions()` exists and `capture env` records it.
- **QE Live re-read 2026-09-05**: schema `"qe-live-droptimizer"`, `version: 1`, gameType `"Classic"|"Retail"`; the sign comment verbatim: "scoreDifference: number (% - positive means alt is worse), rawDifference: number (HPS - negative means alt is worse)"; download filename `qe-droptimizer-{safeName}-{resultId}.json`; no clipboard code; the exporter file has exactly one commit on `dev` (2026-05-20 "handle duplicates"); GitHub code search finds 0 uses of the schema string outside his repo; `UpgradeFinderReport.js` has no export/copy/download, `UpgradeItemData.js` line 3 reads `// == Currently unimplemented. ==`, `UpgradeFinderResult` is `{ id, itemSet, differentials, itemsCompared, contentType }`. Repo pushed 2026-09-02, default branch `dev`, no licence.
- **Ace3 licence** clause quoted in `Lootpath/Libs/LICENSES.md`.

**Verified 2026-09-06 from QE Live's source, re-read for WKE-518 (branch `dev`; read, never copied):**
- `TopGearJSONExport.ts` is unchanged from the 2026-09-05 reading: 80 lines, `SCHEMA = "qe-live-droptimizer"`, `VERSION = 1`, and the payload exactly as §4 records it. `gameType` is derived, not stored: `player?.spec?.includes("Classic") ? "Classic" : "Retail"`.
- **Both sign conventions are now verified twice, and they agree.** The exporter states them at lines 40-43: "scoreDifference: number (% - positive means alt is worse), rawDifference: number (HPS - negative means alt is worse)". `TopGearEngineShared.js` lines 50-51 computes them inside `buildDifferential(itemSet, primeSet, ...)`, where `itemSet` is the alternative and `primeSet` the top set: `scoreDifference = (primeSet.hardScore - itemSet.hardScore) / primeSet.hardScore * 100` (positive when the alternative scores lower) and `rawDifference = itemSet.hardScore - primeSet.hardScore` (negative when the alternative scores lower). The arithmetic confirms the comment; nothing here rests on the comment alone.
- The exporter renames the two fields on the way out: `scoreDifference` -> `scorePercent`, `rawDifference` -> `hpsDifference`. ~~`rawDifference`'s conversion to real HPS is commented out in his source, so `hpsDifference` is a raw score delta, not an HPS figure~~ **Corrected 2026-09-08 (WKE-535): that reading was of the wrong file.** `TopGearEngineShared.js` does carry the commented-out `/* * player.getHPS(contentType) */`, but `git grep` shows its `buildDifferential` is imported by `TopGearEngineClassic.js` alone; the **Retail** path is `TopGearEngine.ts`, whose own `buildDifferential` (line 487) computes `rawDifference = Math.round(((itemSet.hardScore - primeSet.hardScore) / primeSet.hardScore) * player.getHPS(contentType) * modelDiff)`. The committed Dungeon export agrees with the arithmetic and not with the old claim: its top set scores 5460.909, so a raw hardScore delta for a 0.33% differential would be about -12, and the file says -1194 - which needs an HPS around 350,000, exactly what `getHPS` returns for a modern healer. `modelDiff` is `CONSTANTS.modelDiff = 1.5` whenever the cast model is "Default", and it multiplies BOTH fields. So `hpsDifference` IS an HPS figure on Retail, and `UpgradeMapPanel.ValueText`'s `(+1234.5 score)` understates it rather than lying. **Left as it is in M3-6 and filed**: relabelling the Top Gear line is not this issue's scope, and the wording is the owner's call (§11).
- **The Upgrade Finder join, measured 2026-09-08 (M3-6).** The 2026-09-07 Dungeon export (`dungeon: 7`, `raid: [3]`) lists 357 drops over **315 distinct itemID + item level pairs** - 35 of them listed by more than one drop type, which is the only reason the two counts differ. **228 are ranked above the current set, 87 at exactly zero, none below it**, and `hpsGain` agrees in sign with `upgradePercent` on all 315. Joined to the committed journal walks, itemID + item level, exact or nothing:

| Walk | Sources | Rows that gained a number | Rows ranked at ANOTHER level | Distinct drops behind those |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-06 16:12 (369 rows still pending) | 558 | **30** | 151 | 70 |
| 2026-09-06 20:09 cold cache (0 pending) | 478 | **30** | 218 | 97 |
| 2026-09-06 20:09 warm cache | 265 | **30** | 218 | 97 |

  The same 30 rows every time, and they are the RAID half: the export values raid drops at 318 / 324 / 344 for difficulty 3 and the walk previews exactly those levels, so they meet. The dungeon half does not - the export assumed key level 7 (311 / 321 / 334) and the walk previewed key level 10 (276 / 292 / 305) - which is the whole of the 218. So §11's worry was half right: the join is not empty today, and it stays half-empty until whoever runs the Upgrade Finder runs it at the walk's own preview level. Every row's number is QE Live's: e.g. 268205 (Venomancer's Winged Channeler) at 324 from Vashnik the Malignant reads `QE Live: better by 3.08%` from his `3.076`.

**Measured 2026-09-08 (WKE-542, M3-8), the same walk regrouped by run** - `ns.UpgradeMapPanel.RunModel` over the 20:09 cold walk, read with a script before any figure was written down. The walk is **48 runs over 478 drops**: 23 dungeon runs (8 dungeons, seven of them walked at Heroic, Mythic and Mythic Keystone and Kings' Rest at two of the three) and 25 raid boss runs (13 Heroic, 12 Mythic). **9 runs carry a rated drop under either export, every one of them a raid boss**, and all 23 dungeon runs read `no drop rated by QE Live yet` for the key-level reason above. Under the **Raid** export, 21 of the walk's 30 ranked rows are upgrades (the other 9 are his zero): by best upgrade the order starts Ula'tek +3.42% (Gaze of the Coiled Watcher, Head, 2 of 3 drops rated), Vashnik the Malignant +3.191 (3 of 3), Nymrissa Wavecaller +2.501 (3 of 4), The Coiled Altar +2.035 (4 of 6); by most upgrades The Coiled Altar leads on 4 of 6 with a best that is only the fourth largest, which is exactly the disagreement the two orders exist to show. Under the **Dungeon** export, 22 upgrades and Vashnik the Malignant first at +3.08%. The Mythic Keystone half of the map is 54 drops over 7 dungeons.

**Measured 2026-09-09 (M5-3, WKE-552), the redraw over the committed data.** The 2026-09-06 20:09 cold walk renders **478 candidate drops** in the slot view and **48 runs** in the run view, which is what the by-run measurement above counted, and the element list is longer than either (a section per slot and the notes on top). Through the scroll box the panel creates **fewer than 60 frames** for all of it and reuses every one of them on the next refresh - the assertion goes red if the list stops being virtualized. Of the 558 source entries the 2026-09-06 16:12 walk produces, **189 carry an icon** - exactly the keyed rows whose item data arrived, since the journal only hands an icon over with the rest of a loot row - and **none carries instance art**, because every committed walk predates the recording (the golden fixture's diff for this issue is 189 added `icon` lines and nothing else). Joined to the six committed Upgrade Finder documents, every badge on the map is one of QE Live's own sentences in one of two tones: **`better` and `none` both occur and `worse` occurs on none of them**, because no drop of those documents at the item levels this walk lists comes back a downgrade - the negative case is the hand-built sample's, as it was before.

**Measured in-client 2026-09-05 (WKE-515; transcript `spec/fixtures/captures/Lootpath-20260905-133449.lua`, five snapshots: env, inventory with the bank open then closed, vault before then after opening the window; `sawSecret = false` on all five; no `error` markers):**
- **Build**: `GetBuildInfo()` = `"12.1.0", "69587", "Aug 27 2026", 120100, "", " "` in every snapshot. The `.toc` is right.
- **Secrets**: `issecretvalue` and `issecrettable` are functions; **`C_Secrets.HasSecretRestrictions()` returns `true` out of combat**, so the guard is live on this build, not a formality. `InCombatLockdown()` false.
- **Equip functions**: both `EquipItemByName` (global) and `C_Item.EquipItemByName` exist as functions in 12.1.0. M2-2 uses the `C_Item` one (the global sits in Ketho's deprecated file).
- **EJ globals present (35)**: including `EJ_SetLootFilter`, `EJ_GetNumLoot`, `EJ_SelectInstance`, `EJ_SelectTier`, `EJ_SetDifficulty`, `EJ_IsLootListOutOfDate`, `EJ_GetInstanceByIndex`, and **`EJ_GetInstanceForMap`** (a global that Ketho and wowless do not list; candidate alternative to `C_EncounterJournal.GetInstanceForGameMap` for M3-1 to probe with the same mapID).
- **Bags**: `Enum.BagIndex` has 21 values, -3..16 (`Accountbanktab=-3`, `Characterbanktab=-2`, `Keyring=-1`, `Backpack=0`, `Bag_1..4=1..4`, `ReagentBag=5`, `CharacterBankTab_1..6=6..11`, `AccountBankTab_1..5=12..16`). `GetContainerNumSlots` with the **bank open**: 0=20, 1=34, 2=30, 3=34, 4=34, 5=36, **6..13 = 98 each** (six character tabs, two purchased account tabs), 14..16 = 0, -3/-2/-1 = 0. **Bank closed: 6..16 all return 0**; the owned bags are unchanged. So the bank is "every `BagIndex` >= 6 that answers > 0", and closed means silence, exactly the case Inventory must report as `bankAvailable = false`.
- **Bank predicate**: `C_Bank.CanViewBank(Enum.BankType.Character)` and `(Account)` are `true` with the bank open and `false` closed; `Guild` false both ways; `BankFrame:IsShown()` agrees. `C_AddOns.IsAddOnLoaded("Blizzard_BankUI")` is false even with the bank open, so that is not the addon's name; use `CanViewBank`. `C_Bank` carries 23 functions including `AutoDepositItemsIntoBank`, `DepositMoney`, `WithdrawMoney`, which is why captures never call what a namespace walk finds.
- **Item link layout** on real 12.1 links, e.g. `|cnIQ4:|Hitem:271528::::::::90:104::23:7:6652:13439:13696:12838:13692:13698:1561:1:64:251140:::::|h[...]|h|r`: the colour prefix is the named form `|cnIQ4:` (not `|cffa335ee`); after `item:` the fields are itemID(1), enchant(2), gems(3-6), suffix(7), uniqueID(8), linkLevel(9)=90, specID(10)=104, flags(11), context(12)=23, **numBonusIDs(13)=7, then the 7 bonus IDs, then numModifiers(1) and type:value pairs** - identical to the SimC addon's offsets. `ContainerItemInfo.hyperlink` carries the same link.
- **Item level**: on equipped items `C_Item.GetDetailedItemLevelInfo(link)` = `(308, false, 219)`, `C_Item.GetItemInfo(link)[4]` = 308, `C_Item.GetCurrentItemLevel(ItemLocation)` = 308: all three agree; the third `GetDetailedItemLevelInfo` return (219) is the sparse/base level, never the one to show. `GetItemInfo` returns **18** values in 12.1 (the 18th is `""`); `GetItemInfoInstant` returns 7.
- **`ContainerItemInfo` values** on a real bag item: `hyperlink`, `itemID`, `stackCount`, `quality` (4), `isBound`, `iconFileID`, `isLocked`, `hasLoot`, `isReadable`, `isFiltered`, `hasNoValue`, `itemName` present as documented.
- **Vault**: `GetActivities()` returns the full activity list **before the window is ever opened** (9 activities: `type` 6 World x3 ids 207-209, 3 Raid x3 ids 210-212, 1 Activities x3 ids 213-215, plus type 5 Concession and 2 RankedPvP entries), each with `raidString = "Defeat %d Midnight Season 2 |4Boss:Bosses"`, `progress = 0`, `rewards = {}`. `HasAvailableRewards()` and `CanClaimRewards()` false. `WeeklyRewardsFrame` is **nil until the window is opened** (`Blizzard_WeeklyRewards` loads then). `GetExampleRewardItemHyperlinks(id)` returned two empty strings per activity. `GetSecondsUntilWeeklyReset()` = 246378. The week had no progress, so **when `rewards[]` and `itemDBID` populate is still unmeasured**.
- **Capture cost**: inventory 27.3 ms with the bank open, 14.2 ms closed; env 19.6 ms; vault under 1 ms (`debugprofilestop`).
- **Link edge cases found while writing the parser (WKE-516, 2026-09-06)**: a crafted item's string carries the crafter GUID as a trailing field (`...:2377::::Player-69-0F82625A:`) and its name carries atlas markup (`|A:Professions-ChatIcon-Quality-Tier2:17:15::1|a`), so the item string is not all digits; the Hearthstone's bonus-count field is empty (`::86:::::::`), read as zero; a bag slot can hold a **`|Hkeystone:`** link, which is not an item and yields no record. The 2026-09-05 backpack held no gear at all; gear counts by location were equipped 15, bags 35, bank 30.
- **Constants**: `INVSLOT_FIRST_EQUIPPED=1`, `INVSLOT_LAST_EQUIPPED=19`, `NUM_BAG_SLOTS=4`, `NUM_TOTAL_EQUIPPED_BAG_SLOTS=5`. 15 equipped slots carried items.

**Verified 2026-09-06 night from Blizzard's shipped sources and QE Live's, for WKE-520 (read under `.luals/vscode-wow-api/Annotations` and in `c:\Code\qe-live-fork`; never the wiki):**
- **`C_Item.EquipItemByName(itemInfo, dstSlot?)`**, `itemInfo` being `number|string` (`Core/Type/BlizzardType.lua`: `---@alias ItemInfo number|string`) and `dstSlot` a nilable `luaIndex`. The generated docs mark it `SecretArguments = "AllowedWhenUntainted"` and no hardware-event requirement; Blizzard's own `/equip` and `/equipslot` slash commands call it with one and two arguments respectively (`Blizzard_ChatFrameBase/SlashCommands.lua`).
- **`BasicFrameTemplateWithInset`** (`Blizzard_UIPanelTemplates/Mainline/UIPanelTemplates.xml:638`) inherits `BasicFrameTemplate` -> `BaseBasicFrameTemplate`, which is where the `TitleText` FontString and the `CloseButton` parentKeys come from. Both are still guarded in code rather than assumed.
- **`InputScrollFrameTemplate`** (`Blizzard_SharedXML/SecureUIPanelTemplates.xml:106`): a `ScrollFrame` whose scroll child is an `EditBox` at parentKey `EditBox` with `multiLine="true"`, `countInvisibleLetters="true"`, `autoFocus="false"`, plus a `CharCount` FontString and a `maxLetters` KeyValue **defaulting to 0**. `InputScrollFrame_OnTextChanged` writes `GetMaxLetters() - GetNumLetters()` into `CharCount`, which is a large negative number at maxLetters 0 - hence `hideCharCount` and `CharCount:Hide()`.
- **The Settings API signatures on 12.1** (`Blizzard_Settings_Shared/Blizzard_Settings.lua`): `RegisterVerticalLayoutCategory(name)`, `RegisterProxySetting(categoryTbl, variable, variableType, name, defaultValue, getValue, setValue)`, `CreateControlTextContainer()` -> `container:Add(value, label, tooltip)` / `:GetData()`, `CreateDropdown(category, setting, options, tooltip)`, `RegisterAddOnCategory(category)`, `OpenToCategory(categoryID)`. `Settings.VarType = { Boolean = "boolean", String = "string", Number = "number" }`; `category:GetID()` is `SettingsCategoryMixin:GetID`. `RegisterProxySetting` is used rather than `RegisterAddOnSetting` because the value lives in AceDB, not in a plain global table.
- **`UISpecialFrames`** is a list of frame NAMES (`Blizzard_UIParentPanelManager` iterates it), which is why the main frame is created with one. `Frame:SetHyperlinksEnabled(enabled)` exists, so the item links in the rows can raise a tooltip.
- **QE Live's content types are `"Raid"` and `"Dungeon"`** - `src/globalTypes.d.ts:130`, the Redux default (`"Raid"`), and the content toggle's two values. No "Mythic+" string exists anywhere in his source. See §7.

- **The addon accepts its own courier.** Loading that exact file through `loadfile` and handing each `json` to `ns.QEImport.Parse` (the shipped module, over the busted stub) gives `ok = true` twice: "Restoration Druid / Dungeon / 15 items / 12 alternatives" and the same for Raid. Nothing about the paste path changed to make that work.
- **C-1's real output goes through C-2's reader, on C-2's branch.** After C-1 moved to C-2's contract, the file was regenerated (a second run, 16,867 ms for the four documents - the browser profile skipped the welcome dialog in 30 ms) and dropped into a throwaway worktree on `origin/lp-534-companion-import`: `ns.Companion.Validate` answers `ok = true` ("2026-09-08T00:35:29Z v0.1.0 4 exports"), entries 1 and 3 reach `ns.QEImport.Parse` as "Restoration Druid / Dungeon / 15 items" and "/ Raid / 15 items", and entries 2 and 4 are refused by name - "export 2 is a qe-live-upgradefinder document, and Lootpath cannot read those yet (M3-6)". The worktree was removed afterwards. **The two open PRs fit end to end.**
- **The spec warning fires on real data**, as it should: that run logged `QE Live valued "Restoration Druid" but the SavedVariables were captured in "Guardian"`.
- **C-1 built its own profile builder in parallel and then threw it away.** Before S-2 merged, C-1 wrote a SavedVariables reader, an item-link reader and a profile builder of its own against the same fixtures, and measured the same thing independently: **26 of its 50 item lines byte-identical** to the owner's real `/simc` string, and **modifier type 64 = `redirected_base_stats`, type 28 = `content_tuning`** (joined on itemID: head 271528 carries `{64, 251140}` and `redirected_base_stats=251140`; neck 272228 carries `{28, 6014}` and `content_tuning=6014`). Two independent readings agreeing on 26 is worth recording; keeping two builders was not, so **the merge folded S-2's into `tools/companion/lib/` and deleted C-1's three modules and their 29 tests**. What survived from C-1's side is the spec check and the `{ ok, ... }` wrapper.
- **What QE Live's SimC parser actually reads** (`src/General/Items/GearImport/SimCImportEngine.ts` on the fork, read 2026-09-07): `checkSimCValid` looks at lines 0..7 only and needs one line whose key is a case-insensitive substring of the selected class; its `level` check starts `true` and is never set `false`, so level is unchecked; over 1000 lines is refused. `lines[0]` must contain `#` and the character name is everything before its first `-`. `server=` and `region=` are read from any line. **`race=` is read into a local and the assignment to the player is commented out. `talents=` has no reader anywhere in the fork.** `processAllLines` starts at index 8, which matters because it treats any line containing `id=` as an item and `druid="Hotornot"` contains one. An item line's slot prefix is decorative: `protoItem.slot = getItemProp(protoItem.id, "slot")` comes from his own database. `itemEquipped` is `!line.includes("#")`, which is how the commented "### Gear from Bags" block reaches Top Gear as candidates. Items after `### Weekly Reward Choices` are typed "Vault".
- **QE Live values the spec selected in its own character panel, not the string's.** The profile above was written `spec=guardian` (the 09-05 capture was taken in Guardian) and came back as a "Restoration Druid" report, because that is what the browser profile held. The companion compares the two and warns with both names.
- **Re-measured after the rebase onto S-2 and C-2 (2026-09-08 01:06 UTC), through the folded builder:** the same four documents in **16,386 ms**, 305 KB written, and the real file run through **`ns.Companion` on `main`** - `Validate` `ok = true` ("2026-09-08T01:06:45Z v0.1.0 4 exports"), entries 1 and 3 through `ns.QEImport.Parse` as "Restoration Druid / Dungeon / 15 items" and "/ Raid / 15 items", entries 2 and 4 refused by name as `qe-live-upgradefinder` until M3-6. The profile is now S-2's 148 lines rather than C-1's 101 (it writes each item's name as a comment, as the SimC addon does) and omits `region`, `level` and `race` rather than writing them empty, because the committed transcript predates S-2's `env` capture.
- **Gates:** luacheck clean, StyLua clean, LuaLS clean, busted **351 successes / 0 failures / 0 pending** (345 on `main` before this PR, measured in the shared checkout), `node --test` **60 / 0** (S-2's 32 among them). **Eighteen C-1 guards broken one at a time and each watched fail**, then restored: the writer's backslash, quote and control-byte escapes, its empty-document refusal and the contract name it writes (2, 2, 2, 1, 2 tests); the profile wrapper's named refusal, its named missing fields, the identity the spec check needs, and the spec comparison itself (1, 1, 1, 1); the config's content-type check and its account masking (1, 1); the output's temp-then-rename and its addon-installed check (1, 1); the watcher's settle check and its one-run-at-a-time check (1, 1); and, on the Lua side, dropping one escaped byte from the golden, adding a line of code to it, and setting a second field on the namespace (one busted failure each). Ten more were proven red before the fold, on the three modules S-2's builder replaced.
**Measured 2026-09-08, S-2 (WKE-532), the SimC profile built from SavedVariables (`tools/companion-spike/simc-profile.js`; the full table is in that directory's README):**
- **QE Live's importer, read line by line** (`src/General/Items/GearImport/SimCImportEngine.ts` on the fork). It reads: the character name as `lines[0].split("-")[0]`; `server=` and `region=` anywhere; the class token somewhere in `lines.slice(0, 8)` (`checkSimCValid` refuses the import otherwise); and, from line index **8** onward, the item fields `id`, `bonus_id`, `gem_id`, `enchant_id`, `crafted_stats`, `drop_level`, `redirected_base_stats`, `titan_disc_id`, `ilevel`/`ilvl`, with `### Weekly Reward Choices` and `### Linked gear` as section boundaries and a `#` anywhere in a line meaning "not equipped". **It never reads** `talents=`, `level=`, `race=`, `zandalari_loa=`, `role=`, `professions=`, `spec=`, the `### Additional Character Info` block, the checksum, or - on an item line - `content_tuning=`, `crafting_quality=` or `gem_bonus_id=`. Its `bonus_roll_items` branch matches a whole line equal to `bonus_roll_items`, which the addon never writes, so it never fires. Its `checks.level` and `checks.version` are initialised true and cannot fail.
- **The encoder, against the owner's real `/simc` string of 2026-09-07 18:36.** 26 items appear in that string and in the 09-05 13:33:25 `inventory` capture with the same itemID and the same bonus IDs; on all 26 **every field matches byte for byte**, including `content_tuning`, which QE Live discards. 0 field mismatches, 0 differences in ignored fields. The remaining differences are the character: 3 slot tokens (ring and trinket positions swapped), 8 items that moved between equipped and bags, 24 items only the capture has and 17 only the string has, over two days in which the owner equipped five swaps and upgraded several pieces. **No committed inventory capture shares a session with a `/simc` string**, which is why the overlap is 26 and not 44; one line on Tuesday's run sheet fixes that (`/lootpath capture inventory`, bank open, immediately before `/simc`).
- **`adler32` reproduces the owner's own client checksum**, `# Checksum: 14cbd9e1`, over his real string - so the checksum implementation and the "everything above the checksum line" boundary are both measured, not assumed.
- **Through QE Live, the fork at `localhost:3000`.** `run-fork.js` imported the generated profile with **no import error** and produced all four documents in **18,807 ms**: `qe-live-droptimizer` v1 Dungeon and Raid (15 items, 12 differentials each) and `qe-live-upgradefinder` v1 Dungeon and Raid (357 items each). 50 item lines became 46 Top Gear cards (QE Live's own `level > 50` and armour-type filter dropped 4); the real string's 44 lines gave 44 cards. Comparing the Upgrade Finder `equipped` array with S-1's committed one from the real string: 15 items each; **6 items are the same item and agree on every field** - `slot`, `level`, `bonusIDs` (in the same order), `gems`, `enchant`, `tertiary`, `setId`, `isVault`, `isExclusive`; and **QE Live's computed item level matched the client's own `C_Item.GetDetailedItemLevelInfo` on 15 of 15**. The only field QE Live wanted and Lootpath did not have was `player.region` (`""` against `"US"`).
- **The slot map is 13/19 measured.** SimC's slot NUMBER against Blizzard's inventory slot id is confirmed for head, neck, shoulder, chest, waist, legs, feet, wrist, hands, finger, trinket, back and (through the bag table) cloak and 2H weapon, by items appearing at a known inventory slot in the capture and under a known SimC token in his string. The other six are Blizzard's documented `INVSLOT_*` constants.
- **`env` now reads four more facts** - `UnitLevel`, `UnitRace`, `GetCurrentRegionName`, `GetCurrentRegion` (all four in Blizzard's exported docs under `.luals/`, all reads with no side effect). Only `region` is consumed by QE Live. **Not yet captured in game**: the committed transcript predates them, so the builder reports all three of region/level/race as missing and omits their lines.

- **The 12.1.0 client's currency list is 18 entries: 10 headers** (Midnight, Crests, Delves, Features, Professions, Season 2, Zones, Dungeon and Raid, Miscellaneous, Legacy) **and 8 currencies**: Voidlight Marl 3316 (37506), Tidal Spark Dust 3509 (5), Nebulous Voidcore 3418 (0), and under "Crests" **the season's five Mistcrests - Adventurer 3442 (356), Veteran 3443 (0), Champion 3444 (2), Hero 3445 (21), Myth 3446 (20)**. `Currencies.CREST_NAMES` carries those five names in that order.
- ~~No entry is a Catalyst charge.~~ **Corrected the same night: the list had none, the client has one.** The owner's Catalyst tooltip reads "Charge the Catalyst with **Venomblight Manaflux** … Total Maximum: 1/8, CurrencyID **3465**" (also earned from Mythic Keystone dungeons, Season 2 raid bosses, Bountiful Delves, rated PvP; a transform costs 1). The capture missed it because `GetCurrencyListInfo` walks the currency **tab**, which lists only the rows of expanded headers, and the transcript records `isHeaderExpanded = false` on 8 of its 10 headers ("Season 2" among them); the 8 currencies it saw sit under the two expanded ones. `C_CurrencyInfo.GetCurrencyInfo(currencyID)` reads any currency by ID regardless (`CurrencyInfo` carries `quantity`, `maxQuantity`, `canEarnPerWeek`, `rechargingCycleDurationMS`, …). PR #48's `CATALYST_NOT_A_CURRENCY = true` is therefore wrong. **Built 2026-09-09 (M3-11, WKE-546):** the constant is deleted, `Currencies.KNOWN_IDS` carries 3442-3446 and 3465, `Read()` resolves by ID first, and the capture probes `GetCurrencyInfo` for every known ID into `data.byID`. **What is measured and what is not:** the 23:04 transcript predates the probe, so replayed it still yields the five crests (out of the list's info half - 356 / 0 / 2 / 21 / 20) and still no Catalyst answer, and `spec/currencies_spec.lua` keeps it as the "collapsed headers, no `byID`" case. The "1 of 8" line is proven over the stub against the tooltip's own numbers. **The client's own `byID` answer has not been captured yet** - one `/lootpath refresh`, `/reload`, `tools\sync.ps1 -Pull`, commit, and §9 gets the measurement.
- **The headline wording:** with the fair `asOffered` verdict the best he says about any option is "worse than your set", so the first line now reads "QE Live's pick this week (as offered): none - nothing in the vault beats your set; closest: Lightgrasp Worldroot (World 2)" instead of calling the closest alternative a pick on the same screen that says nothing beats the set (the owner's screenshot, 2026-09-08 22:5x).
- **The Great Vault window not opening in game (2026-09-08 evening)** was ruled out as Lootpath's by the owner's disable test (no change with the addon off, no Lua error with `scriptErrors` on); Lootpath registers no vault event and hooks nothing of Blizzard's. **Checked 2026-09-09 mid-morning, at the owner's request: the Vault tab against the companion's documents, the client and his engine (`spec/fixtures/captures/Lootpath-20260909-085940.lua`; the verdict file written 2026-09-09 02:55 UTC from the 12:45 captures - unchanged since because the profile has not changed, which C-4 logged on every one of the owner's ten reloads overnight):**

- **Why "everything upgraded" prefers the weapon and "catalyzed" prefers the shoulders is his rule, not ours:** `ItemSet.ts:205` rejects any set with `uniques["vault"] > 1` - one Great Vault option per set, which is the game's own one-reward rule - so under `maxed` he cannot take both, and the weapon on its Hero track (cap 321, bonus 12841) beats the shoulders; the owner's worn Worldroot is on the Champion track (bonus 12838, cap 308), which is why it stays at 308 when everything is upgraded. Under `catalyzed` (no upgrades) the 305 vault weapon loses to the worn 308 and the tier shoulders win the slot.

**Read 2026-09-14 night, R-2a (WKE-571), from Baganator's installed source rather than from a screen** (`C:\World of Warcraft\_retail_\Interface\AddOns\Baganator`, version 823; read, never copied). Three facts settle what a corner widget is and refute two of the issue's three candidate causes:

- `Core/Config.lua:46-49` - the corner defaults. `icon_top_left_corner_array` is `{"junk", "item_level"}`; `icon_top_right_corner_array` is `{}`; bottom-left `{"equipment_set"}`; bottom-right `{"quantity"}`.
- `ItemViewCommon/ItemButton.lua:134-179` - one `Callback` per corner walks that corner's widgets in array order and **`break`s on the first that returns true**. Only one widget is ever shown per corner, so sharing a corner is not sharing: it is displacing.
- `API/Main.lua:185-196, 228-240` - `RegisterCornerWidget` asserts the id is new, stores the plugin, and `AutoInsert`s the `defaultPosition` at the **priority index** (`table.insert(cornerArray, priority, id)` when `priority <= #cornerArray`). `AutoInsert` is gated per id on `icon_corners_auto_insert_applied`, so **a widget new to an already-saved profile IS inserted** - candidate cause 1 ("only offered in the settings") is refuted. `ReportPluginAdded` then fires `PluginsUpdated`, which rebuilds the per-corner callbacks and asks for an item-widget refresh, so **a late registration is handled** - candidate cause 3's "installed too early to matter" is refuted for registration. What a late registration does NOT get is `ApplyItemDetailSettings`, which creates the per-button widget frame and runs only on the `ItemTextures` reason; `Baganator.Constants.RefreshReason` exposes only `ItemWidgets` and `Searches` publicly, so no addon can ask for it. Lootpath registers from `ns.onReady`, which is its own `ADDON_LOADED` - before any Baganator button exists - so this gap cannot be the cause on a fresh login, and it is recorded rather than worked around.

**So R-2's `{ corner = "top_left", priority = 1 }` was a displacement, not a mark**: Lootpath would have been first in top-left and the item level would have gone from every slot the plan marks. The widget is in `top_right` now, which ships empty. **What the blank screen actually was is still unmeasured**, and §11 carries it: the fix removes one cause, `/lootpath glow` names whichever remains.

Also read, and it closes candidate cause 2 as unlikely rather than as proven: `Syndicator/Search/BaseInfo.lua:7` sets `info.itemLink = cacheData.itemLink` unchanged, and Syndicator's live bag cache fills that from `C_Container.GetContainerItemLink` - the same call `Modules/Inventory.lua` scans with. The headless guard the issue asked for exists either way: `spec/tooltip_spec.lua` answers `ns.Glow.WantsLink` off the inventory capture's own link and asserts the key matches the map's.

A test breaks red when the capture asks with the wrong one.
- **The tooltip hook's shape, read from Blizzard's shipped source** under `.luals/vscode-wow-api/Annotations/FrameXML/Annotations/AddOns/Blizzard_SharedXMLGame/Tooltip/`: `TooltipDataHandler.lua:199` declares `TooltipDataProcessor.AddTooltipPostCall(tooltipType, func)`; `:298` calls the registered function as `func(tooltip, tooltipData)`; and the file declares `AddTooltipPreCall`, `AddTooltipPostCall`, `AddLinePreCall`, `AddLinePostCall` and **no remover of any kind**, which is why the spike's `off` is a flag the handler reads first and not an unhook. `TooltipUtil.lua:9` `GetDisplayedItem(tooltip)` returns `name, hyperlink, tooltipData.id`, taking the hyperlink from `C_Item.GetItemLinkByGUID(tooltipData.guid)` when the data carries a GUID (`ItemDocumentation.lua:286`) and from `tooltipData.hyperlink` otherwise. **One correction to the brief:** it says the annotated `TooltipData` declares no id, and `Structure.lua` confirms that - but `GetDisplayedItem` returns `tooltipData.id` as a third value anyway, so "no id" is true of the class and not of the call. Keying off the hyperlink still stands; the reason is that the third value is undeclared, not that it is absent.
- **Which bag addons the owner has, from his own transcript** (`spec/fixtures/captures/Lootpath-20260909-085940.lua`, `env` snapshot 10, 74 addons): **Baganator, Syndicator, ElvUI and Pawn are all loaded**. | what | when (UTC) | trigger | asked | activities | with rewards | links | `hasGeneratedRewards` |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| the refresh's ask | 19:09:29 | `refresh` | yes, answered in **113.2 ms** | 10 | 1 (the Concession row) | **0** | false |
| the flush two seconds later | 19:09:31 | `flush` | no | 10 | 1 | 0 | false |
| the login ask (19:26 session) | 19:26:20 | `flush` env | yes, **no update ever fired** (`waitedMs` 556.7) | - | - | - | - |
| **with the Great Vault window OPEN** | **19:27:16** | `command` | no (`the activities already carry rewards`) | **11** | **5** | **9** | **true** |
| the flush six seconds after it shut | 19:27:22 | `flush` | no | 11 | 5 | 9 | true |

The four asked refreshes of that day were answered in **66.7, 77.1, 113.2 and 130.9 ms**, `timedOut` false on all four, and not one of them changed a single field: `interact.after` carried the same 10 activities and the same 0 links as the read before it. The login ask of the 18:58 and 19:09 sessions fired in **233.0 and 265.0 ms**; the 19:26 one recorded `updateFired = false, timedOut = true` - the client never answered it. **Its `waitedMs` of 556.7 does not fit the 5 s bound that ended it, and is not explained here:** every one of these figures is `debugprofilestop()` differenced across an event, and that clock is the client's profiler rather than a wall clock. The four sub-140 ms answers agree with each other and with the login ask's two, so nothing built on them is in doubt; the one that disagrees is written down rather than tidied away. The nine links the open produced are Preyhunter's Lantern (275547), Enigmatic Dreamwatcher's Leggings (271527), Kyrakka's Searing Embers (193748), five Mythic Keystones (180653) and a Thalassian Token of Merit (269862). **The "two activities" figure in the issue as first filed was a parse artefact** - `data.activities` is a `ns.Probe` pack, so a reader that takes its length rather than its first value counts the pack - and every read in fact carries the full list.

**What this does not say.** Whether a second or third `WEEKLY_REWARDS_UPDATE` ever brings data the first did not: no transcript has more than one update per ask, because until M3-16b the capture settled on the first and stopped listening. `interact.updates` is what will answer it, and the next reset day is when.

## 10. Issue map (WKE-514..529, Linear project Lootpath)

**M5 lane - the UI polish (filed 2026-09-09 evening; proposal `docs/UI-POLISH.md`, mockups https://claude.ai/code/artifact/d66abe32-511d-424c-8613-d9a0504257f9):** **549 M5-0** (you) the five answers and baseline screenshots - blocks nothing, read by 551 and 553; **550 M5-1** (ai) `UI/ItemLine.lua`, the shared item loader lifted out of 547's `Vault.lua`, `icon` on Inventory and Vault records, the stub recording `SetTexture`/`SetAtlas`/`SetVertexColor`, Equip Now redrawn; **551 M5-2** (ai) `PortraitFrameTemplate`, edge tabs, the status strip, the Import dialog, compartment entry and minimap button, scale and compact options, the window size from 549 - independent of 550 (different files), so the two run side by side; **552 M5-3** (ai, blocked by 550) `WowScrollBoxList` on the Upgrade Map, slot sections, run cards, the walk recording `buttonImage1`, the difficulty dropdown replacing `FilterLayout`; **553 M5-4** (ai, blocked by 550) the vault grid, the pick's glow, currency icons, scenarios in the tooltip, the scenario dropdown on the tab - runs beside 552; **built 2026-09-09 night, PR open**; **554 M5-5** (you, blocked by all four) sync, the journal capture that carries instance art, screenshots of each tab, findings filed as issues. **Order: 550 and 551 together, then 552 and 553 together, then 554.** 548 (M3-13) is independent and may land anywhere in the sequence; 553 absorbs its fourth scenario as one more dropdown entry.

**Addon lane (sequential; blockers set on the board):**
514 M0-1 skeleton + CI + CLAUDE.md + captures `env`, `inventory`, `vault` (ai; **PR #2, 2026-09-05**) -> **515 M0-2** branch protection, three captures in one session, SimC addon install (you; **done 2026-09-05, PR #3**) -> 516 M1-1 Inventory normaliser against the committed transcript (ai; **done 2026-09-06, PR #4**) -> **517 M1-2** not needed: the 2026-09-05 transcript had the bank open and closed and 516 needed no further capture (you; close it) -> 518 M2-1 QEImport (ai; **done 2026-09-06, PR #5**) + **519 M2-1b** real QE export fixture (you; the spec carries a `pending` waiting for it) -> 520 M2-2 Match + editbox + Equip Now (ai; **done 2026-09-06 night, PR #14**) -> **521 M2-3** verify in game (you) -> 522 M3-1 Journal + `capture journal` (ai; two PRs, **both done 2026-09-06: PR #7 the adapter and the async capture, PR #12 the aggregator and the walk's second read**) -> **523 M3-2** journal + vault captures (you) -> 524 M3-3 Upgrade Map + Vault panels (ai; **done 2026-09-06 night, PR #15**) -> **525 M3-4** verify + one week of real use (you) -> 526 M4-1 v0.1.0 README/changelog/tag (ai). **Side issue, filed 2026-09-07: 530 M3-5** UI polish from the first in-game run (ai; before 526, independent of 525).
