**519 and 523 complete 2026-09-08:** 519's second export came from the companion (vault option in the top set), 523's second visit from `/lootpath refresh` after the reset; both in PR #35. **519 changed by 527's finding:** the live site has no Download JSON control, so the owner produces the fixture from the fork running locally, in Restoration spec, on a week with vault progress (Tuesday 2026-09-08 or later).

**Dispatchable now (2026-09-06 night):** nothing for an agent - 520 and 524 are both merged, and what remains before 526 is in-game work only. **521 M2-3 and 525 M3-4 are the owner's next steps**, in the same session as Tuesday's captures; the client is already synced (§0). 519 and 523 each keep one Tuesday step (§0). **523 is two visits:** the first (journal + before-reset vault pair) is committed on `lp-523-captures` (PR #10); the second (after-reset vault pair) is Tuesday's.

## 11. Risks and open questions

- **R-3d has never been on a screen, and its join is C-10's and inherits C-10's bound (2026-09-15, WKE-584).** Two things. **(a)** `Roads.VaultConsidered` matches a vault reward against a pass's `considered` on the item ID plus the bonus IDs - the vault link's on one side, QE Live's card's on the other. Where the two ever disagree about a reward the run really did import, the road falls to `not rated - new since the last refresh`, which is the safe way to be wrong (it takes no position) but is still wrong. No committed file carries a run made over a profile WITH a vault section together with the vault snapshot it was built from, so the true-case guard is a hand-built pool and not a real pairing; the owner's next refresh with an unclaimed vault is the first real one. **(b)** The `nil` case keeps C-8's premise for every pre-C-11 file and every paste, so a pasted document over a reset-day vault still says `not in your best set` about a reward it never saw. That is deliberate - a paste records no pool and no count, so there is nothing to read - but it means the defect survives on the paste path until a document with a pool replaces it.

- **M3-16b has never been on a screen, and one part of it cannot be tested until next reset day (2026-09-15, WKE-583).** Three things. **(a)** The multi-update read is asserted through the stub only: no committed transcript carries more than one `WEEKLY_REWARDS_UPDATE` per ask, because until now the capture settled on the first and stopped listening. Whether a second or third update ever brings data the first did not is what `interact.updates` is recorded for, and only a reset-day transcript on this build answers it. **(b)** The wait indicator's three surfaces are headless: the chat line, the badge and the strip's first clause are asserted over the stub's widget model and against the owner's own 104-character cut, which is a proxy for the width and not a pixel claim. Whether he now sees it is the eye test, and it is the whole point of the change. **(c)** The reset-day sentence sends him to the Great Vault window, and the reason it is safe to say is that his window opened on 2026-09-15 - on 2026-09-08/09 it would not open at all, which is why M3-16 was written the way it was. If that recurs, the sentence names a remedy he does not have, and the honest answer then is a different sentence rather than a second interaction.
- **What the strip should say on a refresh that has been captured but not sent (2026-09-15, M3-16a, WKE-581; open, the owner's).** On the popup path R-6's wait line reads `rating your gear, started …` before anything has reached the companion, because M3-16a writes `refreshStartedAt` there so the line's click can be the reload (§7 (d)). The click is right; the wording is ahead of itself. Left as R-6 wrote it rather than invented here. Only reachable on a week the login ask failed or timed out.
- **`ReloadUI` is the player's event or nothing (2026-09-15, M3-16a, WKE-581).** The refresh reloaded from inside the vault capture's continuation on the first withheld-rewards week after M3-16 and the client refused it. Every reload the addon performs must be reachable from the slash command's own execution or from a click; a wait of any kind between the command and the reload turns the reload into a button. R-6's click and the logout sequence already obey this; M3-16's chain did not.

- **The counter warning after a content switch (2026-09-14, C-11's first run).** `warning: QE Live's counter says N selected, and 15 of the cards look active` appears once per content-type switch, before the next pass 1; every selection that follows is correct. Either the page's counter lags the card re-render by a tick or the deselect at the end of pass 3 is not reflected in the counter. Harmless on this run; read `fork.js`'s check and the fork's counter before deciding, and either wait for the re-render or drop the warning with a reason. A refresh now costs about 3 minutes 14 seconds; whether that is acceptable for a mid-session refresh is the owner's call once R-6's wait line has been seen against it.

- **R-3c has never been on a screen, and two of its rules are the owner's to overrule (2026-09-15, WKE-580).** The four sentences, the two badges and the rated Upgrade row are asserted headlessly over the committed week; nothing here has been hovered. Two deliberate departures from the issue are waiting on his word: a WORN copy of a vault or Catalyst pick is still not called the arrived pick (the evidence cannot support it - §7, R-3c (a)), and a non-vault pick's badge says `now worn` / `now crested` without the word `claimed`. Both are one-string changes if he wants the issue's version.

- **A freshly crested key is rated by nothing, and that is not a defect anything can fix here (2026-09-15, WKE-580).** The Upgrade road can only be as good as the document's knowledge of the key in the player's hands, and cresting makes a new key. So the road the owner is most likely to look at - the piece he just upgraded - carries `no rating` until the refresh after. The arrived-pick sentence is what speaks on that block instead. Whether `no rating` is the right phrase there, rather than `not rated · new since the last refresh`, is an open voice question: the key IS new, but the phrase would then be saying something about the upgrade question that the document never asked.

- **`crest type and cost not read` is still on the tooltip (2026-09-15, WKE-580).** R-3c took the crest COUNTS off it and left this clause, because the issue named only the counts. By R-2a's own rule it looks like the same case - it is principle 7's vendor-window gate, and on a tooltip nobody asked and it can never become readable there. One flag would move it; it is left for the owner to say, because WKE-574's transcript may turn it into a real cost clause first.
- **~~The capture at logout has never run on a real client~~ - it has, and it is cheap; what remains is narrower (2026-09-15, WKE-579, WKE-582).** R-7 could say only that the sequence costs about a millisecond in a fixture world. The owner's 13:58 pull carries **five real runs at 19.51-33.13 ms** (§9), so two of the three questions are answered: `PLAYER_LOGOUT` leaves room for it, and an inventory scan of the owner's real bags fits. **Still open:** whether the plain vault read at the flush loses anything the refresh's interaction would have found - which needs a week with a reward unclaimed, the same week M3-16a's own open question needs. **And what the same pull exposed, now fixed by R-7a:** the event fires on `/reload` too, so the sequence had been running and labelling itself `logout` on every reload; the label is `flush` and the lists are bounded at four. **Neither of R-7a's own halves has been on a real client:** whether SavedVariables stops growing is the owner's next two reloads, and 2.0 MB of the 11.0 MB file sits outside the `captures` table the bound touches.

- **R-6 has never been on a screen, and the wait COULD NOT have been (2026-09-14, WKE-578; answered 2026-09-15, WKE-589).** The nudge has been seen; the wait had not, and V-4 says why rather than leaving it to an eye test: `Drift.Waiting` compared a correct UTC stamp against a parse running an hour late, so the elapsed time cleared the ten-minute give-up bound on its first check and the wait deleted itself before anything could draw it. That is fixed and guarded (§7). What is still headless: the nudge row, the launcher badge and the wait line are asserted through the stub's widget model. Nothing here claims a pixel: whether the second row is noticed where it sits, whether the badge is visible on the owner's ElvUI minimap, and whether the wait line's counting reads as reassuring rather than as nagging are all his run.
- **`ITEM_UPGRADE_MASTER_UPDATE` is named but unwitnessed (2026-09-14, WKE-578).** It exists in Blizzard's exported documentation (`ItemUpgradeDocumentation.lua:210`) and is registered for that reason. Whether it actually fires when a piece is crested, and whether `BAG_UPDATE_DELAYED` would have caught it anyway, is one crest away from an answer and has not been taken.
- **The wait's ten-minute bound is a judgement, not a measurement (2026-09-14, WKE-578).** It exists so that a watcher the owner forgot to restart cannot leave `rating your gear` on the strip for a session; the entry above from 2026-09-09 is exactly that failure. A real run time will be measured the first time the companion answers a click, and the wait line quotes it from then on - but the bound itself is still a guess.
- **"Fresh at login" needs a refresh first, and the fix was deliberately not built (2026-09-14, WKE-578).** See §7. A logout flushes the last CAPTURE, not the current gear, so a logout with no refresh in it gives the companion nothing new and the next login loads the same plan. A capture at `PLAYER_LOGOUT` would close it; the issue forbade client work for this, so it is a follow-up and not a silent widening.

- **C-11 has never met the real fork, and its wall clock and file size are unknown (2026-09-15, WKE-572).** Deliverable 4 of the issue asks for the second pass's duration on the owner's profile, the number of passes per scenario, and the items each pass considered, read from `Data/companion.log` and the verdict. The owner runs the refresh; nobody else can. Three figures matter: (1) **wall clock** - the first loop was 14 documents in 51 s, and four passes per Top Gear document could take that to something in the minutes; (2) **passes per scenario** - 63 cards with a 20-card baseline is ten new cards a pass, so the owner's profile likely needs five and the bound of four will leave a tail, which is exactly when `beyond the rating's item limit` is still shown; (3) **file size** - a Top Gear export measured 16-17 KB in `spec/fixtures/qe/`, and eight Top Gear documents becoming up to thirty-two makes `Data/QEVerdict.lua` roughly half a megabyte, parsed at every load. If any of the three bites, the bound is the dial: `topGearPasses` is one config number and it is in the fingerprint.

- **The `upgrade` capture has never met a vendor (2026-09-14, WKE-574).** It is built, guarded and green over the stub, and every answer that matters is the client's: does `SetItemUpgradeFromLocation` need the frame open, does `ITEM_UPGRADE_MASTER_SET_ITEM` fire, how long does the client take, what does an item the vendor refuses return, and does any of it taint. Until the owner opens a crest vendor, runs `/lootpath capture upgrade`, `/reload`s and `tools\sync.ps1 -Pull`s, the transcript does not exist - and `Modules/Roads.lua` still says `crest type and cost not readable` on every crest road, by design. **The follow-up that reads the snapshot is deliberately not in WKE-574's PR:** which of `upgradeLevelInfos` a road should name, and whether the cost the vendor quotes matches the crest the road wants, are questions only the transcript answers. Building the read first would be a guess wearing a test.
- **C-12's gate has never met the real fork (2026-09-15, WKE-577).** Every figure above is headless. Three things only a real run answers: what one pool read costs (a `/topgear` visit plus the card read, once per gated pass - it may be cheap enough that the vault waiver is not worth keeping, or dear enough that it is), whether a `maxed` import really raises the level ON THE CARD for an item below its cap (the gate's whole upgrade half rests on it; if his cards show the pre-upgrade level the gate would read "nothing below its cap" on a character who has plenty, which the fail-open rule does NOT cover because the pool reads fine), and whether a `catalyzed` import on a character with nothing catalysable really produces zero clone cards rather than none of the cards being reachable. **Human-required, and it is the owner's next ordinary refresh:** merge, sync, `/lootpath refresh` twice, then paste the `QE Live scenarios:` line and the per-pass `asked`/`SKIPPED` lines out of `Data/companion.log`. If the `maxed` pass says `SKIPPED - nothing you hold is below its upgrade cap` while he is holding the 315 Worldroot, that is the second reading and the gate's upgrade half moves to a different signal.
- **The upgrade level of an owned item is not readable outside a vendor window, and C-12 did not change that (2026-09-15).** `Modules/Inventory.lua` records `bonusIDs` and no upgrade track; `C_ItemUpgrade.GetItemUpgradeItemInfo` (`currUpgrade`/`maxUpgrade`) answers only after `SetItemUpgradeFromLocation` with the upgrade UI open, which is WKE-574's (M3-17) recorded exception and the owner's to run. Anything that wants "how far is this from its cap" as a NUMBER, rather than as QE Live's yes-or-no, waits on 574.
- **A converted Catalyst clone whose bonus IDs are exactly the ones the document projected still reads `Catalyst this one.` (2026-09-14, WKE-576).** `IsArrivedPick` refuses a held item whose key the pick's road already carries, and the Catalyst road carries the clone key the export wrote (`271526:6652:12830:13662` for this week's shoulder). So a clone that comes out of the Catalyst at exactly that key is not "arrived" - it is the road's own item, and the road speaks for it, saying to convert something that has already been converted. The conservative refusal is deliberate: the alternative is claiming an item is not the road's when the document says it is. A clone crested after conversion has a key no document carries and does read `This is the tier shoulders the plan wanted.` Not seen in the client either way; the next refresh dissolves it.

- **The Upgrade Map's stale-slot remedy is text, not a button (2026-09-14, WKE-576).** The issue asked for it "in the row's own verb column". A section header has exactly one column beside its name and the drop count is in it, so the header ends `/lootpath refresh` in words and the Refresh VERB lives on the row below, where principle 9's button belongs. If the owner wants a button on the header, the header element needs a second control.

- **ANSWERED 2026-09-14 night: the mark is seen after R-2b, 304 widget calls measured (§9); the two entries below are kept as the record of how it was found.** Why the bag glow did not appear: one cause found and fixed, one question left, and both are now one line on screen (2026-09-14/15, WKE-571 then WKE-575). R-2a's diagnosis ran and cleared the whole answer path (§9): the adapter is `baganator`, the widget is in the `top_right` corner, the map holds the helmet, the mark answers `glow yes`, and Baganator's own call path shows nothing that can hide a widget that answered true. **R-2b found the picture** - the vault's `evergreen-weeklyrewards-reward-selected`, measured by the owner at 214 x 121, drawn into 15 points - and replaced it with a mark the addon draws itself (§7). **What is still open is whether that was the whole of it**, and the addon now answers it without a screenshot: if the mark is still absent, `/lootpath glow` says `Baganator asked the widget N times this session`, and `0` means Baganator never called the widget, so the cause is upstream of the picture and the next session goes to the registration rather than to another texture. **Human-required, and it is one line:** sync, `/reload`, open the bags on the best-set helmet, look at the top-right corner of the slot, and paste that line either way.

- **The superseded reading of that question, kept because its three outcomes are still how a blank mark is read (2026-09-14, WKE-571).** R-2a fixed one cause it could prove from Baganator's own source (§9: the top-left corner would have displaced the item level, and only one widget per corner is ever shown) and refuted two of the issue's three candidates from the same source, but none of that says what the owner's blank screen actually was. **Human-required, and it is now one line:** sync, `/reload`, open the bags, and run `/lootpath glow` - with a bag item shift-clicked into it if he likes. It prints the adapter and why, what Baganator answers about the widget's corner, the map's key and mark counts, and for that item the key, whether the map has it, what the mark answers and whether its own road is one the plan points at. If a key mismatch is suspected, `/lootpath capture glow` records the same four facts for every bag slot with the map's own key list beside them; `/reload`, `tools\sync.ps1 -Pull`, and the transcript settles it. **Three outcomes and what each means:** `adapter: blizzard` with Baganator loaded means the detector ran before Baganator's globals existed and the install moves to an `ADDON_LOADED` for Baganator; `Baganator has the widget but no corner is drawing it` means the corner array is the cause and the status strip now says so on its own; `item: NOT in the map` or `glow no` on a piece the tooltip calls best-set means the key shapes differ and the capture's two key lists say where.
- **`ApplyItemDetailSettings` is not reachable from Baganator's public refresh reasons (2026-09-14, WKE-571).** A corner widget registered AFTER a button already exists never gets its frame created, because the per-button `onInit` runs only on the `ItemTextures` reason and `Baganator.Constants.RefreshReason` exposes only `ItemWidgets` and `Searches`. Lootpath registers from its own `ADDON_LOADED`, before any Baganator button exists, so this cannot bite on a fresh login - but it would bite anything that registered later, and it is written down rather than worked around with a private constant.
- ~~**The status strip is cut at the window's width, and the owner has not said what to drop (2026-09-14, WKE-570).**~~ **Answered 2026-09-14 evening (drop it) and built 2026-09-15 by V-2 (WKE-573), with one half still open.** It was: After V-1 the strip's first fact reads `...on Druid - Dungeon Top Gear - companion, written 5 day(s) ago - vault pick: this week - compani...` and C-9's companion clause is the half lost. The cheapest cure is dropping `Dungeon Top Gear`, because the content type is already on the Vault tab's dropdown; the alternatives are wrapping the strip or shortening the companion clause. **Nothing was changed for this in R-3a** - which clause matters is the owner's call and the PR asks it. Now: the two words are off the line and in the strip's tooltip, and on the owner's own state the line went from 122 characters to 103 against the 104 his screen recorded - the companion clause fits whole. **Still open:** the FAILED clause brings the line to 132 characters and no single dropped fact closes that; a test asserts the overflow and that the companion's whole sentence is in the tooltip. Shortening that clause or wrapping the strip is the owner's call. **Still unmeasured:** every figure here is characters, not pixels - the stub has none and the budget is his recorded cut read as a lower bound. His first look at the shorter strip is what confirms it.
- **A `.toc` entry for a Data file the game lacks is a login error, and `sync.ps1` was the one that could leave it out (2026-09-14).** Fixed: missing Data placeholders are always written, existing ones always kept. The rule for the next Data file: add it to the `.toc`, the placeholder, and nothing in `sync.ps1`.
- **Roads lane, from the engineering pass (2026-09-11, `docs/ROADS-UX.md` Buildability), as R-0 left it (2026-09-12, §9).** **Answered:** the "behind X" referent is read from the alternative's own item list, not inferred; the keystone reads exist and the name is asked for with the challenge map ID; Baganator, Syndicator, ElvUI and Pawn are all loaded in the owner's client. **Still open:** the tooltip post-call fires for every item tooltip in the game and nobody has measured how often, or what one call costs, on his client - the counter is built and waits on his run; which bag FRAME is live (four bag-ish addons loaded says nothing about which one draws the buttons), which decides whether R-2's glow is Blizzard's `UpdateItems` hook, Baganator's plugin API - which displaces Pawn's arrow, one upgrade plugin at a time - or an ElvUI `UpdateSlot` hook. The crest cost for a vault item or a drop is not readable from the client at all (`C_ItemUpgrade` answers only for an owned item in the open vendor window), so "cost not readable" is permanent for those rows. Which delves are Bountiful is not exposed; crafting materials need the crafter's open window. The excluded list carries the item ID and bonus IDs since C-10 (567, merged 2026-09-12), so "beyond the item limit" is an `ns.ItemKey` comparison rather than a name match.
- **Not one pixel of R-3 has been seen (WKE-564, PR open).** Every row, every group header, every gold edge, every button and every Explain sentence is asserted headlessly over the stub and has never been on a real screen. Five things only the owner's client can answer: whether a road row is readable at the window's width (the facts line wraps and `Panel.NOTE_CHARS_PER_LINE` is an ESTIMATE of how far, because headless there is no font to measure - a slot with thirty "no rating" rows is the case to look at), whether `Show run` actually lands on the run's card rather than near it, whether the Vault cell's mark is visible for long enough at `POINT_SECONDS` = 4, whether the slot header's two lines crowd the worn item, and whether the sentences sound like a guildmate (568, the owner's). **The human-required step is on the issue:** sync, reload, expand the shoulder and the 2H Weapon slots, screenshot both and the Vault headline.
- **The `none` group is long and the owner has not judged it.** Over the committed week the shoulder slot's third group is 20 rows and the weapon's is 34, almost all of them journal drops from a difficulty nobody is running. R-3 draws them all, behind a section the reader opened on purpose; whether that is a list or a wall is a question for the week of use, and the answer may be a cap with a count, which the model already supports (`counts`).
- **The road model is built and nothing renders it (R-1, WKE-562, PR open).** `ns.Roads` answers for every slot and every item over the owner's committed week, and not one of its strings has ever been on screen. Three things it needs from the surfaces that come next, each of which it takes as an input rather than reading: which plan is highlighted (`highlightedScenario`, resolved by `ns.VaultPanel.HighlightScenario` so the tooltip and the Vault tab cannot disagree), the client's own difficulty words (`difficultyLabels`, because the walk records only the ID), and the item's type word (`subType`, the difference between "the Worldroot" and "the staff" in the plan sentence). **The `none` group is every unrated journal drop in the slot** - around thirty rows for a busy slot on the 478-drop walk - which is honest and is not something a tooltip or a row can print: R-2's cache and R-3's list decide what to show of it, and the model carries `counts` so a surface can say how many it left out.
- **The plan sentence has never been read by a player (R-1).** It is generated, asserted in full in `spec/roads_spec.lua` so the owner can read every one of them in the spec, and held to principle 16 by a test that refuses a percentage, a level, a colon, a label or a source in any of them. Whether it actually sounds like a guildmate is the owner's call, and the phrasings are a hand-written table precisely so that changing his mind costs one edit.
- **Roads lane, from the engineering pass (2026-09-11, `docs/ROADS-UX.md` Buildability):** the bag glow has three code paths (Blizzard bags, Baganator's plugin API, ElvUI's Pawn-only slot arrow) and Pawn contends on two; which bag the owner runs is unrecorded. The tooltip post-call fires for every item tooltip in the game and must be an O(1) cache lookup. The crest cost for a vault item or a drop is not readable from the client at all (`C_ItemUpgrade` answers only for an owned item in the open vendor window), so "cost not readable" is permanent for those rows. Which delves are Bountiful is not exposed; crafting materials need the crafter's open window. The excluded list from 558 carries no item ID. All of these are in the spike.
- **Roads lane, from the engineering pass (2026-09-11, `docs/ROADS-UX.md` Buildability):** the bag glow has three code paths (Blizzard bags, Baganator's plugin API, ElvUI's Pawn-only slot arrow) and Pawn contends on two; which bag the owner runs is unrecorded. The tooltip post-call fires for every item tooltip in the game and must be an O(1) cache lookup. The crest cost for a vault item or a drop is not readable from the client at all (`C_ItemUpgrade` answers only for an owned item in the open vendor window), so "cost not readable" is permanent for those rows. Which delves are Bountiful is not exposed; crafting materials need the crafter's open window. The excluded list from 558 carried no item ID; **C-10 (WKE-567, 2026-09-12) added the item ID, the sorted bonus IDs and a clone's `originalItem`**, so "beyond the rating's item limit" is an `ns.ItemKey` comparison (`ns.Companion.IsExcluded`) - with name+level still the only join a pre-C-10 verdict file allows. All of these are in the spike.
- **The source-free voice is now guarded, and the guard has a known edge (V-1, WKE-569, 2026-09-14).** `spec/voice_spec.lua` catches a forbidden word in any string a panel table holds or any surface the committed fixtures render. What it cannot catch is a sentence built at runtime out of pieces that are each innocent, in a branch no fixture reaches - the badge builders were exactly that until V-1 lifted their four sentences into constants, and the same shape could be written again. The cure when it happens is the same one: name the string on the panel, where the walk reads it. The guard also cannot see a word that is wrong without being on the list ("Voulk", an address, "the engine says"); the list is five entries and is in the spec's own header, to be extended when one is found.
- **Attribution set aside by decision (2026-09-11).** No source is named on screen. If the engine behind the verdict file is ever someone else's product in public, that decision is revisited with 528; until then it is the owner's call and recorded as such.
- **~~The stub can be more generous than the client~~ (2026-09-10, CLOSED 2026-09-13 by T-1, WKE-560).** The Upgrade Map crash was invisible to 761 green tests because the stub gave every DropdownButton the selection mixin's methods. The audit that followed found the leak was not in `attachTemplate` at all but in the one constructor every widget was built from, and that PR #72's own fix still leaked for a dropdown built with no template; nineteen method names left the widgets they do not belong on, eight stub-only helpers moved onto `widget.stub`, and `spec/stubs_spec.lua` (18 tests, every one proven red by a mutation) now fails if any of it comes back. **What is NOT closed by this:** the stub still models less than the client wherever no panel needs more, and it still proves nothing about pixels - what the owner sees at his UI scale is M5-5's eye test (WKE-554) and remains his. A template the addon starts using for the first time still has to be read under `.luals/` and modelled from its XML before it is trusted.
- **QE Live's Top Gear sees at most 30 items for a non-patron, and the companion fills the 30 in page order (WKE-558, decision owed).** Measured 2026-09-09 22:48 (§9): with 41 gear items in bags the vault Spaulders, the off-hand and every Catalyst clone were never in his pool, so `catalyzed` equalled `asOffered` and the Vault tab's answers were about two of four options. Nothing in the export says what was left out. 558 makes the selection prioritised (equipped, vault, clones, then bags) and names the leftovers on screen; whether the owner raises the cap as a patron, lives within 30, or runs twice is his. The cap is Voulk's rule and stays untouched in the fork.
- **QE Live's Top Gear sees at most 30 items for a non-patron; the cap decision is still the owner's (WKE-558, PR open).** C-8 changed WHICH thirty and made the omission visible - vault items, then Catalyst clones, then bag items a slot at a time, with the leftovers named on the Equip Now and Vault tabs - so the `catalyzed` and `thisWeek` scenarios finally get their clones. **What is still open:** whether the owner becomes a QE Live patron (which raises the cap for real), lives within 30 knowing bag items past it are invisible to Equip Now, or runs a second Top Gear pass per content type over the leftovers as its own document. **Also open:** C-8 has never been run against the fork - the numbers it changes are proven over the shape of the 22:48 log and not over a live page, so the first real run is the check (the owner's watcher owns that browser profile). Historically:  Measured 2026-09-09 22:48 (§9): with 41 gear items in bags the vault Spaulders, the off-hand and every Catalyst clone were never in his pool, so `catalyzed` equalled `asOffered` and the Vault tab's answers were about two of four options. Nothing in the export says what was left out. 558 makes the selection prioritised (equipped, vault, clones, then bags) and names the leftovers on screen; whether the owner raises the cap as a patron, lives within 30, or runs twice is his. The cap is Voulk's rule and stays untouched in the fork.
- **ANSWERED and BUILT 2026-09-14 (WKE-557), and one question is left inside it.** Last week's unclaimed vault rewards leave `GetActivities()` once this week has progress, until the UI interacts. Measured 2026-09-10 (§9); the owner decided the capture may call `C_WeeklyRewards.OnUIInteract()`, wait for `WEEKLY_REWARDS_UPDATE` and call `CloseInteraction()` (§7, the second recorded exception to "captures only read"), and M3-16 built it. **Still unmeasured, and the human-required step - now asked at LOGIN rather than at the refresh (M3-16a, WKE-581):** whether that update actually fires outside the Great Vault window, and how fast. The bound is still 5 s and the same three fields still record it, but since M3-16a they come back on the `env` snapshot's `vaultLoginAsk` block rather than on a `vault` capture, because the login ask captures nothing of its own. The owner's first login on a build carrying M3-16a, with the reward unclaimed, is the transcript that says. The 2026-09-15 refusal (§9) proved the wait happens; it did not measure it. If it never fires, the fallback is unchanged and already in the snapshot's words: the full client restart that carried the rewards on 2026-09-09 08:59. **Separately unexplained and untouched by this: the Great Vault window does not open on the owner's client at all** (2026-09-08/09; `Blizzard_WeeklyRewards` shows as not loaded in every snapshot), which is also why "open the vault first" - the SimulationCraft addon's own advice - is not available to him.
- ~~**The companion runs invisibly unless the owner runs it (2026-09-09).**~~ **Closed 2026-09-13 by C-9 (WKE-559).** It was: a watcher started from a session window printed into that window and nowhere else, with no log file and no in-game line, so a refresh whose run died looked exactly like a refresh with nothing to do. Now: `Data/companion.log` carries every line it prints (rotated at 200 KB, one `.1` kept), `Data/CompanionStatus.lua` carries the last run's `state` / `stage` / stamps / exit code and is rewritten at every stage change, and the window's status strip says one clause of it - `companion: wrote 3 minute(s) ago`, `run started 22:48`, `profile unchanged, no run (23:06)`, `FAILED at profile (21:06) - see companion.log`, or `never seen`. What is NOT closed by it: the strip reads the file the client loaded, so a status written while the game is up is read on the next `/reload` - which is the same constraint every addon file has.
- **The Upgrade Map's run cards have no art until a new journal walk (M5-3, WKE-552).** The walk records `buttonImage1` / `buttonImage2` / `bgImage` now, but all six committed transcripts were taken before it did, so every card draws its plain strip and the present case is proven only on a stub-shaped walk. The owner's next `/lootpath capture journal` on a synced build settles it, and is part of M5-5 (WKE-554). Until then, nothing on screen is wrong - there is simply no picture.

- **The chrome is built and nobody has looked at it (WKE-551, M5-2).** The portrait ring, the status strip, the tabs on the bottom edge, the import dialog and the minimap button are all proven over the stub's widget model - which template was used, which keys exist, what each thing was anchored to, what texture the ring was told to draw - and the stub has no pixels. **What is open, and is the owner's:** whether the strip's five facts fit on one line at his UI scale in a 620-wide window (they are on a non-wrapping font string, so what does not fit is cut off, not wrapped); whether the middot separator (`U+00B7`) renders in the game's font at all; where the minimap button lands at the default angle of 200 degrees against his other addons' buttons; and whether the tabs hanging below the frame collide with anything. All four are M5-5's (WKE-554) eye test. **Also unanswered: M5-0 (WKE-549)**, so the window is still 620 x 640 rather than the mockups' 760, and there is no ElvUI skin hook - if the answer is "yes, ElvUI", a hook would need somewhere to re-skin the portrait frame, the strip's two buttons and the tabs after the skin loads, which is a second issue and not a change to this code.
- **The vault grid is built and nobody has looked at it (WKE-553, M5-4).** Nine cells, a currency strip and a dropdown now share the same 620-wide window that held a column of font strings, and everything proven about them is what a widget was TOLD - which atlas the glow got, which file ID a chip's icon was set to, which strings a hover put on the tooltip - because the stub has no pixels. **What is open, and is the owner's (M5-5, WKE-554):** whether three cells fit across the body at his UI scale (each is about a third of the width less a 74-point row label, and the item name is a non-wrapping font string, so what does not fit is cut off rather than wrapped); whether `evergreen-weeklyrewards-reward-selected` looks right stretched over a cell that is not the size of Blizzard's own; whether the currency icons read at 14 points; and whether the `WowStyle1DropdownTemplate` on the header row collides with anything. The 760-wide window the mockups assume is still M5-0's (WKE-549) unanswered question.
- **`compactRows` is stored and read by nothing.** M5-2 registers the checkbox and keeps the value in `db.profile.settings`; the item line that is supposed to read it is M5-1's (WKE-550), which is being built in parallel. Until M5-1 merges, ticking the box changes the profile and redraws the tab identically. That is deliberate - the two issues touch different files on purpose - but a reader who ticks it before M5-1 lands will think it is broken.

- ~~**Vault rewards can arrive before their item data (WKE-547).**~~ **Built 2026-09-09 (M3-12), PR open; proven headlessly over the 08:59 transcript, not yet in client.** The tab now says `name pending (item 275547) - level pending` (or the last capture's name, labelled), asks the client for the item, and redraws once when the item loads. **What is open:** nobody has watched the 12.1.0 client answer `RequestLoadItemDataByID` for a vault link - which of the two events it fires, and how long after - so the first fresh login with the Vault tab open is the measurement; if the client fires neither, the bounded timer (8 x 0.25 s) is what fills the rows, and if the item never loads at all the row keeps saying pending and the next look asks again. `capture vault` still records the nameless link as it stands; a capture taken while rows are pending is the evidence, not a fault. Also found this morning: Docker Desktop, the fork's dev server and the companion watcher all stop at a reboot; the first must be started for `check.ps1`'s Docker gates, the other two for the loop.
- **The window is text where the whole popular set is icons (proposal 2026-09-09, `docs/UI-POLISH.md`).** **Half answered 2026-09-09 by M5-1 (WKE-550), PR open:** Equip Now draws item lines - icon, quality border, level in the corner, badge, tags - and the widget every other tab will use exists. The Upgrade Map (552) and the Vault (553) are still columns of font strings, and the chrome (551) is still `BasicFrameTemplateWithInset` with the paste box on every tab. **Nothing is proven at the owner's UI scale**: the stub asserts which icon and which tint a row got, never how any of it looks, and 554 M5-5 is where that is settled. Every popular addon that lists items draws an icon, a quality-tinted `WhiteIconFrame` border and corner badges, and Blizzard's own Adventure Guide row does the same; Lootpath draws colour-coded sentences in 14-point font strings, creates no texture, and gives the paste box the top third of every tab after the companion made pasting the exception. QE Live's own cards put the item level in the icon corner and say better in gold and worse in burnt orange, and healers name QE Live as their sim, so the proposal keeps his palette rather than Pawn's green/red. The three things an issue must not do are already decided elsewhere: no value of our own (a bar scaled to his numbers is arithmetic on them, so the badge is text), no hook into Blizzard's vault or journal frames (2026-09-05, tooltips descoped; annotating the vault in place is a later, separate ask), no new library. Facts checked for it: the journal aggregator drops the raw row's `icon` and the walk never records `EJ_GetInstanceInfo`'s `buttonImage1`, so run-card art needs a capture change and one owner `capture journal`; `ContinueOnItemLoad` can never fire for some items (the wiki's warning), so the loader keeps a cancel and a fallback. Open until the owner answers §7 of the proposal.
- **Neither the fork's dev server nor the companion watcher survives a reboot or logoff.** Found 2026-09-09 morning: both had been started from a Claude Code session's background tasks and were gone after the owner's client restart the night before; the companion's `startFork` covers the fork once the companion itself is running, but nothing started the companion, so the loop silently stopped working after a reboot and the only tell was an ageing "companion, written N hours ago". **Answered 2026-09-13 by C-9 (WKE-559):** `tools/companion/install-startup.ps1` registers an at-logon Task Scheduler task for the current user running `start-companion.ps1` - the fork's dev server when nothing answers its port, then `node companion.js --watch` - and `uninstall-startup.ps1` removes it; both idempotent, neither touching a watcher the owner started himself. **Open, and only the owner can close it: the task has never been run.** There is no Windows, no Task Scheduler and no logon in CI, so what is proven of those three scripts is that install and uninstall name the same task and that the task starts the script that starts the watcher. The owner registers it once and reboots; until then this risk is answered on paper.
- **The currency tab hides collapsed headers (WKE-546).** Anything read through `GetCurrencyListInfo` depends on which headers the owner has expanded; the 2026-09-08 capture saw 8 of the client's currencies for that reason and missed the Catalyst charge (Venomblight Manaflux 3465). Known currencies are read by ID from now on; the list stays as the way to discover names, never as the source of a count. **Built 2026-09-09 (M3-11):** `Currencies.KNOWN_IDS`, `Read()` by ID first, and the capture's `data.byID` probe. What is still owed is one transcript taken with that probe in it - see the entry below.
- **525's first feedback (2026-09-08, all three tabs)** was filed as 541, ~~542 (Upgrade Map by run)~~ **built 2026-09-08 (M3-8)**, 543 (Upgrade Finder at several key levels), 544 (Vault guidance). **What 542 does NOT answer, and the owner asked for it in the same breath: "which dungeon at the lowest possible key level".** Every dungeon run in the view says `no drop rated by QE Live yet` today, because both committed Upgrade Finder exports assumed key level 7 and the walk previewed 10; the view says which key level it is showing and does not invent item levels for any other. ~~That is 543's whole job, and the model is already keyed for it.~~ **Half closed 2026-09-08 (C-7, WKE-543):** QE Live is now asked at five key levels, each answer is filed under its level, and the Upgrade Map reads the one matching the walk's preview level and says which it got. **What is still open:** the WALK is still one key level (the journal's preview level, +10 today), so the by-run view still shows Mythic Keystone runs at that one level - C-7 deliberately did not touch the walk (the issue forbade it), and until a walk previews several levels the by-run view cannot rank +2 against +10 side by side even though the exports for both are now stored. **And the owner's actual question is answerable from the exports today** but is not yet on screen anywhere: at +2 QE Live rates 102 dungeon drops as upgrades and at +10 he rates 142 (§9), which is the comparison a "lowest key that still helps" line would make. ~~541 (Equip Now: a vault option in QE Live's best set is a pointer to the Vault tab, not a "not owned" swap; reasons were truncated)~~ **Built 2026-09-08 (M2-4).** Premise confirmed by replaying the join he saw before touching it - inventory snapshot 7 of the 2026-09-08 transcript against the companion's own export - which gave `14 already best, 0 to swap, 1 not owned, 0 without a verdict` and a red `2H Weapon` row reading `[Lightgrasp Worldroot] -> item 251935 (ilvl 321) (not found: it is a Great Vault option you have not taken yet)`. **What is still open there: nothing headless can say how the wrapped line LOOKS**, and nothing here proves the note fits on the owner's screen - the guards are that the reason is on a font string of its own, that it wraps, and that its width comes from the row's anchors rather than a number. The one deliverable deliberately not built is the scenario name in the status line, which the issue itself gates on 540. The Equip Now staff row the owner saw was the asymmetric default verdict (539); with 539 merged it appears only when a vault option wins as offered. Whether the Catalyst charge is a currency in the 12.1.0 client is unknown until `capture currencies` runs.
- **A five-level refresh stores five dungeon Upgrade Finder verdicts in SavedVariables, where it stored one - and since C-6 three - since M3-13 four - Top Gear verdicts per content type where it stored one.** The Top Gear side is much the smaller (16-17 KB of JSON each against ~120 KB), and `qeImports[contentType]` still duplicates the `asOffered` one, but the same unmeasured question applies and the same answer is available if it bites. The committed Dungeon export is ~120 KB of JSON and the parsed verdict is written out again by the client on every `/reload`; `db.char.ufImport` and `db.char.ufImports[contentType]` already duplicated the newest one before C-7, and `ufImportsByLevel` adds the rest. Nothing has measured the SavedVariables file after a five-level companion run on the owner's machine - that needs a `/reload` and is the owner's step. If it bites, the answer is to stop writing `ufImports[contentType]` and resolve it through the by-level shelf, which is one function and no new shape.
- **A best set may spend more Catalyst charges than the scenario's name implies, and Lootpath has no way to ask for fewer (M3-13, WKE-548).** QE Live's `autoCatalyze` adds a clone of everything his `canBeCatalyzed()` accepts and his set builder picks freely among them; measured on the owner's own profile, `thisWeek` - the "use the charge once" question - spends two, a shoulder and a chest. There is no per-item catalyze control in his import dialog to turn that into one, so the tab reports both catalyzes beside the client's own charge count (`you have 1 of 8`) and computes nothing. **Open:** whether the owner wants the line to say something stronger when the number of catalyzes exceeds the charges he holds - that would be one comparison of two facts he already has on screen rather than an estimate, so it is allowed, but nobody has asked for it. His first week with the tab decides. **Reopened and closed the same night (WKE-556, M3-15, built):** the fifth line counted only owned conversions, so on the real documents it presented a two-charge set as the one-charge answer. Every clone is counted now, the vault's included, and on today's documents the line is the absence sentence; the scenario lines name the vault half of the conversion as well, so the sentence beside `you have 1 of 8` no longer understates the charges. What that leaves open is only the original half below - whether the tab should say something stronger when the conversions outnumber the charges - which is still the owner's. **Half-closed 2026-09-09 night (M3-14, WKE-555):** the "which one, then?" half is answered without a new run and without Lootpath choosing - his own alternatives already contain one-charge sets he scored, and the tab now prints the best of them as a fifth headline line (§9 for the figures). What is still open is unchanged and is the owner's: whether the tab should say something stronger when the number of catalyzes exceeds the charges he holds. **Also still true:** if a week's export ever contains no one-charge set at all, the line says so and stops there - design B of 555 (driving `MiniItemCard`'s per-item catalyze options on his Top Gear page, a companion change and a run per candidate) was scoped and deliberately not built, because design A answered on the real documents.
- **No `thisWeek` document exists in the owner's client yet (M3-13).** The scenario is asked by the companion from this PR on, so the first one arrives with his next `/lootpath refresh` and companion run. Until then the Vault tab's default highlight falls back to `asOffered` and says so on its own line, which is exactly the fallback path 548's tests pin - but the in-client proof that the fourth line appears is still owed, and so is the owner's verdict on whether `thisWeek` is the right default.
- ~~**Gear transformations are QE Live's to model; the companion has to ask the right questions (WKE-540).**~~ **Built 2026-09-09 (C-6), extended to a fourth question the same day (M3-13), and measured.** The three scenarios are run, filed and shown; the Catalyst question is answered by his engine and joined through his own clone. **What is open after it:** the panel shows one line per scenario per option, so a week with four gear options and three scenarios draws up to twelve verdict lines - readable on the measured vault (six lines in all, because he ranked two options) and untested on a week where he ranks everything. **The `maxed` scenario's item levels are his assumption, not the vault's**, so `Panel.LevelText` reports the difference (`305; QE Live valued it at 321`) off the HIGHLIGHT scenario's verdict, which means pointing the highlight at `maxed` changes which level pair is shown - correct, and the sort of thing only real use will judge. **And nothing has yet run the full loop with scenarios in the game**: the measurement above wrote to a temp file, so the first proof that a three-scenario `Data/QEVerdict.lua` (112 KB here, and the real one carries six Upgrade Finder documents beside it) loads and imports on `/reload` is the owner's next refresh. Catalyst and upgrade tracks are covered by his three import boxes as named scenarios; crafted stats, embellishments, enchants and gems arrive in the SimC string. What is still unasked: a bag item is not "active" at import, so `autoCatalyze` never clones a bag piece - only equipped and vault gear; if the owner wants "what if I catalyzed the shoulders in my bag", that is a per-item option in Top Gear's UI the companion does not drive. Also the Upgrade Finder's `craftedLevel` / `craftedStats` settings are left at his defaults (`2`, `Crit / Haste`). Both recorded, neither built until real use asks for them. **One more, found while building C-6:** a bag item is not active at import, so no scenario ever tells the owner what a bag piece would be worth catalyzed.
- ~~**The second refresh starts a QE Live run of its own (WKE-537).**~~ **Closed 2026-09-08 (C-4, PR #32, merged):** the companion fingerprints the profile, remembers the fingerprint of the last verdict it wrote in `.state/last-profile.json`, and skips the run when the gear is unchanged and that file is still there. Measured on the 2026-09-07 log: the needless third run cost 17 735 ms; the skip path opens no browser at all. `--force` overrides.
- **The second refresh can still beat the companion's write, and C-4 did NOT fix that half.** The game loads whatever `Data/QEVerdict.lua` holds at reload time; if the owner reloads before the companion's "wrote" line, the window shows the previous verdict with an honest age. Nothing in the client can see the file the companion has not written yet. What C-4 changed is the price of recovering: the extra `/reload` that fixes it now costs a skipped run and one log line instead of another 17 s of QE Live. Still: **wait for the "wrote" line.** A refresh that ends in `profile unchanged since <stamp>` instead is the companion saying the file on disk is already the answer for this gear.
- ~~**Refresh does not capture (WKE-536).**~~ **Closed 2026-09-08 (C-3):** `/lootpath refresh` runs `env`, `inventory` and `vault` itself and only then reloads, so the companion's profile is this session's gear; a refusal stops the reload rather than letting the owner reload on a stale snapshot. Proven headlessly (order, the three stored snapshots, the combat refusal, a refusal from another running capture, the bank named open and closed from the snapshot's own `CanViewBank`), **not yet in game** - the first live proof is the owner's next `/lootpath refresh` after a sync, where the companion's profile header should read an `# Inventory captured` stamp seconds old and `spec=restoration`. What is still visible rather than fixed: the companion names the snapshot it read (`profileCapturedAt`), which is now a check rather than a warning. New and small: two refreshes write six snapshots where they used to write none, and `/lootpath capture wipe` is the only pruner (§7).
- ~~**Nobody has run `/lootpath capture currencies` yet (WKE-544).**~~ **Closed 2026-09-08 23:04**, and its conclusion half-corrected on 2026-09-09 (M3-11, WKE-546). The transcript gave the five Mistcrest names and IDs, which were right. It also appeared to say the Catalyst charge is not a currency, which was wrong: the list it walks is the currency TAB and 8 of that night's 10 headers were collapsed (§9). **What is still human-required, and is the only thing left on this feature: one `currencies` capture taken with the by-ID probe in it.** `/lootpath capture currencies` (or `/lootpath refresh`), `/reload`, `tools\sync.ps1 -Pull`, commit the transcript unedited. Until it lands, the addon reads the charge from the LIVE client correctly - that path is tested over the stub - but no committed fixture proves it against the owner's own client, and §9's "1 of 8" rests on his tooltip rather than on a transcript. The IDs to expect in `data.byID`: 3442-3446 and 3465.
- **The companion runs QE Live's engine from the owner's fork clone.** Fine for the owner's own machine; not distributable without Voulk's agreement (his repo has no licence, §4). WKE-528 is therefore the gate for anyone else, and the companion's README must say so. If he declines, the companion stays a personal tool and the manual paste path is what ships.
- ~~Whether his engine runs headless is unproven~~ **Proven 2026-09-07 (S-1): Playwright drives the local fork end to end in 27.7 s** with no engine surgery (§9), and 2026-09-08 (C-1) the companion did the whole round trip in 21.5 s with a persistent browser profile. Node-direct is not needed. The fork must be running for the companion to work at all; the companion starts it and says so. Two of his pages are fragile for this character (`/embellishments` crashes); the companion must not depend on any page but `/circlet`, `/topgear`, `/report`, `/upgradefinder`, `/upgradereport`, and must treat a CRA runtime-error overlay as a failure to report, not to click through.
- ~~**The dungeon join needs the cross-level lookup (WKE-545).**~~ **Closed 2026-09-08 (M3-10).** The reading was right: `dungeon: 7` is his +10 button, his +10 drops at 311 and the client's keystone-10 preview lists 305, so an exact join against the +10 document found no dungeon drop. The panel now searches **all** stored documents for the client's own level and finds the rows in his **+6** document (305) - 84 rows ranked where any one document ranks 30, and 16 rated runs where there were 9 (§9). **What is still open is the six-level disagreement itself**, which is a fact for WKE-528's conversation with Voulk and not Lootpath's to resolve: one of his `MPLUS_KEY_REWARDS` table and Blizzard's own preview is stale, and the addon adjusts neither. Two consequences a reader should know: a raid drop, which every dungeon document values identically, is labelled with the lowest document that calls its level a `drop` (`(at +2)` on a Mythic raid row) - true about where the number came from, and odd-looking until the header line is read; and the walk itself still previews ONE key level, so "the same dungeon at +2 and at +10" is still two runs only when a walk exists for each.
- **QE Live's `hpsDifference` and `hpsGain` are both real HPS on the Retail path, and neither reaches the screen.** §9's old claim that Top Gear's is a bare hardScore delta was a reading of the Classic file and is corrected there. Nothing is wrong in the code today - `(+1234.5 score)` understates a number rather than inventing one - but the Upgrade Map now has two value lines whose units are both his and neither named. Whether a healer sees "HPS" on either line is the owner's call; the numbers are parsed and stored either way.
- ~~**The profile built from SavedVariables may lack fields QE Live needs** (talents are not captured today)~~ **Closed 2026-09-08 (S-2, WKE-532).** It lacked one: `region`, now read by `env` along with `level` and `race`. **Talents were never needed** - QE Live's importer does not read `talents=` at all, so no `C_Traits` capture was written. What is left is narrow and named: **`titan_disc_id`** is the one importer-consumed item field the companion cannot build from a link (a tooltip spell scan of four belts, itemIDs 242664/245964/245965/245966, none of them the owner's); the **vault section is written but untested**, because no committed `vault` snapshot has ever carried a generated reward - Tuesday 2026-09-08's after-reset capture is its first real input, the same capture §9 already waits on; and QE Live's **30-item Top Gear cap** means a bank-open capture offering 81 items still gets 30 selected, which is C-1's problem to solve, not a defect here.
- **No committed capture shares a session with a `/simc` string, so the encoder's proof is 26 items wide, not 44.** The 2026-09-05 inventory and the 2026-09-07 `/simc` string overlap on 26 items and agree on every field of all of them, but the other differences cannot be told apart from drift without a same-session pair. **One line on Tuesday's run sheet closes it:** `/lootpath capture inventory` with the bank open, immediately before step (c)'s `/simc`. Until then, a defect that only shows on an item neither profile shares would not have been caught.
- ~~**`sync.ps1` and `Data/QEVerdict.lua`** - a repo sync must not clobber the companion's file with the placeholder~~ **Closed 2026-09-07 (C-2):** a push skips `Data\` unless the game has no `QEVerdict.lua`, and `-IncludeData` overwrites it on purpose; measured against a scratch client tree (§7).

- ~~**The profile built from SavedVariables may lack fields QE Live needs** (talents are not captured today)~~ **Mostly closed 2026-09-08 (C-1).** QE Live reads neither `talents=` nor `race=` - both were traced in his parser and neither has a reader (§9) - so no talents capture is needed. What the SavedVariables genuinely lack is **region, level and race**; the companion writes those lines empty and names each one in its log, and QE Live checks none of them (`checks.level` starts true and is never set false). S-2 is now the small `profile` capture that would carry them, plus a fresh Restoration-spec inventory capture: the only one committed is 2026-09-05 in Guardian, which is why every companion run today warns about the spec.
- **The spec QE Live values is the one selected in its browser, not the one in the profile.** Measured 2026-09-08: a `spec=guardian` profile produced a "Restoration Druid" report. The companion compares and warns, but nothing stops a wrong browser selection from producing a full set of confident numbers for the wrong spec. If the fork ever grows a way to set the spec from the string, take it; until then the warning is the guard.
- **`sync.ps1` and `Data/QEVerdict.lua`** - **measured 2026-09-08 (§9): the file survives a sync while the repo has no `Data\` folder, and is overwritten by the placeholder the moment C-2 ships one.** C-2 decides what to do about it (exclude `Data\` from `Push-Addon`, or accept that a sync costs one companion run) and records it in §7. Nothing else in the game folder is ever written by the companion, and it refuses rather than creating an AddOns tree that is not there.
- Ketho's generated annotations are one patch behind the client (12.0.5 vs 12.1.0). An API added or renamed in 12.1 fails the LuaLS gate as undefined; handle each case with a scoped `---@diagnostic` or `---@cast` plus a note here, never by loosening the gate. Blizzard's live docs on Gethe/wow-ui-source are the tie-breaker.
- ~~The M+ pool -> journal instance mapping is the step most likely to differ from any doc~~ **Closed 2026-09-06:** `C_EncounterJournal.GetInstanceForGameMap` resolved all 8 season maps; the global `EJ_GetInstanceForMap` answered 0 for each (§9). Blizzard's own journal never calls `SetPreviewMythicPlusLevel`, so its effect on loot rows is still unobserved beyond one read at level 10.
- ~~Loot rows are read before their item data is cached, and nothing handles it~~ **Handled 2026-09-06 (WKE-522 part 2), not yet proven in client.** 369 of 613 rows in that walk had no `link`, `slot` or `name` while `EJ_IsLootListOutOfDate` was false on every read, so that gate does not signal it. The walk now reads each target a second time after waiting for `EJ_LOOT_DATA_RECIEVED`, mirroring `EncounterJournal_LootUpdate` (§7), and the aggregator carries a link-less row as `pending` rather than as "no item level" (§6). **What is still open is whether the second read fills them on the live client**: the stub proves the mechanism and the committed transcript predates it (`summary.rereadTargets` is 0 there). Tuesday 2026-09-08's `capture journal` decides it, and the walk records `pendingRowsFirstRead`, `pendingRowsFinalRead` and `rowsFilledByReread` so the answer is a number, not an impression. If the events turn out to arrive too slowly for 8 x 0.25s, the next move is a longer `ITEM_DATA_MAX_ATTEMPTS` or `C_Item.RequestLoadItemDataByID`, not dropping the rows.
- **`EJ_GetEncounterInfoByIndex` answers nothing at some difficulties.** Every `DungeonChallenge` target and both Midnight targets in the 2026-09-06 walk listed zero encounters (§9). The aggregator's per-instance name index covers 73 of the 95 rows that left; the walk's new `EJ_GetEncounterInfo` call should cover the rest, and Tuesday's capture is what shows it. Why the index-based call goes empty is unexplained - a difficulty the journal has not finished switching to is the guess, and it is only a guess.
- The journal walk leaves the owner's Adventure Guide on whatever M+ preview level it last set, because the client offers no getter for it; tier, difficulty and loot filter are restored. If M3-2 shows that this matters in practice, the fix is to set the preview level last and to a sensible default, not to stop reading it.
- ~~Every value in `spec/journal_spec.lua` is a stub placeholder~~ **Half closed 2026-09-06.** The adapter's tests are still stub-shaped and prove only behaviour around the client (probe, mask, both waits, both bounds, abort, restore); the aggregator's tests replay the real 2026-09-06 transcript and every figure in them was read out of it. The one thing no headless test can reach is the walk itself running against a live journal.
- **The Upgrade Map has no values, and on today's data it never will.** Measured 2026-09-06 (M3-3): a journal preview link carries exactly one bonus ID, an owned item's link carries two to seven, so the exact-key join covers 0 of 558 sources. The panel is honest and useful without numbers - what drops, from whom, at what item level, and whether you already own one - but the second promise is only half kept until QE-1/QE-2 land the Upgrade Finder export. That makes Voulk's answer to WKE-528 the difference between a values-free map and a valued one, which is the same risk the paste path already carries.
- ~~**UI findings from the first in-game run (2026-09-06), filed as WKE-530 (M3-5)**~~ **Closed 2026-09-07 (PR #21): all five fixed, each guarded headlessly, four gates green.** (1) `Panel.DifficultyLabels` makes every difficulty in a map read differently; (2) `Panel.FilterLayout` wraps the filter row and the list moves down to clear it; (3) the pinned note left `Panel.Lines` on both panels, so the header draws it once; (4) rows the client reports at item level 1 are hidden with a per-slot count line, on exactly 1 and never on a pending row; (5) `ns.VERSION` falls back to "dev" for an unsubstituted `@` token. **The Concession label was checked and stands** - Blizzard's own weekly-rewards frame gives that threshold type no display string (§7). **What no headless test reached: how any of it LOOKS.** The wrap is proven as anchors and widths against the panel's own width, not as pixels; the owner sees all five at the next `/reload` after a sync, and 525's Tuesday pass is where they are confirmed on screen. One figure in the issue was wrong and the fix was not changed by it (§9). *Original text:* (1) the Upgrade Map's difficulty buttons read "Heroic" twice and "Mythic" twice because `GetDifficultyInfo` returns the bare localised name for dungeon and raid alike - qualify the live name the way `Panel.DIFFICULTY_NAME` already does; (2) the difficulty row overflows the frame width (five buttons, the fifth clipped) - wrap or shorten; (3) the pinned note is drawn twice on the Upgrade Map and Vault tabs, once by the tab header and once inside the list; (4) 86-108 journal rows sit at item level 1 (cosmetic and quest drops the Adventure Guide lists) at the bottom of every slot - owner's call whether to hide rows at level 1 explicitly (a fact from the client, not an estimate) or leave the list complete; (5) the title bar shows `@project-version@` on a synced dev copy - either `sync.ps1` substitutes it or the frame falls back to "dev". Also: the Vault tab labels activity type 5 "Concession 2", which reads oddly; check the enum name.
- ~~**QE Live's importer upgrades vault options by default and owned gear not at all (WKE-539).**~~ **Closed 2026-09-08 (C-5), and measured both ways.** The companion sets both boxes itself on every run - by their label, read back, exit 4 if a click did not take - and defaults to both OFF, so the two sides of a comparison are asked the same question; the pair rides in the verdict file's `qeSettings` and in C-4's fingerprint, so flipping one costs a run. Measured through the companion the same day: his defaults put the vault weapon in the top set at 321 (5647.977 / 5860.499), both off leave the worn 308 there and make the vault's 305 copy an alternative 0.5681% / -1983 HPS behind (5544.654 / 5757.608) - §9. What is NOT closed: `autoCatalyze` is still left where his dialog puts it, which is WKE-540's named scenarios; and nothing in the addon reads `qeSettings` yet (WKE-538 may show it).
- ~~The vault's reward shape is still unmeasured.~~ **Measured 2026-09-08 (§9)** and the module needed no change. ~~Three Vault-panel findings against it, filed as WKE-538 (M3-7).~~ **All three fixed in M3-7 (§6):** the keystone is named in words beside the gear instead of listed as an item at level 1; a row with a reward is presented as claimable whatever the reset did to its `progress`; and QE Live's 321 is shown next to the client's 305 with the setting that produced it. What is still open there: **the panel can only name the assumption when the companion recorded it.** A pasted export carries no `qeSettings`, and QE Live's own dialog defaults (`autoUpgradeVault` true) are not the companion's (both false since C-5), so a hand-pasted export and a companion one can disagree about an option's level with only one of them able to say why. Nothing infers the setting from the difference, which is the right refusal, but the reader of a pasted export gets two numbers and no reason.
- **A cached fixture is shared mutable state.** `spec/helpers/replay.lua` memoises each transcript, so one test's mutation followed the table into every test after it and produced a committed golden that did not match the transcript (§7, 2026-09-06). Fixed by copying before mutating; the shape of the mistake is easy to repeat in the next spec that edits a snapshot.
- Ace3 comes from two sources (wowace SVN in CI, GitHub mirror locally); drift between them would show up as a CI-only failure.
- QE Live's schema is his to change; a moved schema fails loudly (version pin) rather than wrongly.
- The owner's character name and realm are in the committed QE fixture; owner said that is fine for a public repo of their own character, and may ask for scrubbing.
- Voulk may decline the PR; the map stays values-free (recommended) or QE-3 is built (owner's call).
- Paste path is download-then-paste until a copy button exists on QE Live. Worse, as of 2026-09-06 the live site has no download control either; the owner runs the fork locally until the PR lands. If Voulk declines everything, every user needs a local build or QE-3, which makes QE-2's outcome the biggest single risk to the product.
- SimC addon export format changes are outside our control; QE Live absorbs them, we only depend on its JSON.

---

- **R-2 has never been on a screen, and the bag half has never run in a client at all (2026-09-14, WKE-563).** The tooltip block, the cache and both bag adapters are asserted headlessly over the owner's own week and against the shipped sources of Blizzard's container frames and Baganator's API, read under `.luals/` and `C:\World of Warcraft\_retail_\Interface\AddOns`. What no test here can say: whether the block reads well under Pawn's own block on a real tooltip; whether the corner widget lands where the owner expects it in Baganator's window or collides with what he already has in that corner; whether `RequestItemButtonsRefresh` redraws an open bag promptly enough to feel live; and whether 424 keys is a build the owner notices at login. The human-required step on the issue is the answer to the first two: sync, reload, hover, screenshot.
- **The item-ID-and-level fallback can join a bag item to an unowned row of the same item at the same level (2026-09-14, WKE-563).** `byItemLevel[itemID .. "@" .. level]` is what an Adventure Guide hover needs, because a journal link's bonus IDs are not a document's. It is asked only after the exact key misses, and the cache now gives every piece in the bags an exact-key entry, so a bag hover never reaches it; a hover on an item that is neither held nor walked still could. Nothing on the owner's own week does, and the join is the same one the loot map has used since M3-10, but it is a join by item and level rather than by identity and is written down as such.

## 12. Working rules (also in CLAUDE.md, shipped in 514)

Branch per issue (`lp-<n>-<slug>`), PR to `main`, never commit to `main` directly, merge your own PR only when every CI check is green. Read the whole Linear issue including its "Working in this repo" section. Verify the issue's premise against the code before building; a wrong premise is reported in the PR and the right thing is built instead - scaling down is fine, widening is not. Never write a measured figure you did not read from a tool. Every guard proven red. Every client read secret-guarded; nothing in combat; no network; **Lootpath never computes a healer value.** In-game steps are the owner's: write the capture, hand it over as human-required, build against the transcript. Report in every PR: what shipped; premise check; measured figures with the tool; guards and how each went red; CI results; left undone, deliberately. **And update this file**: §0 every session without exception, plus whichever numbered section holds the decision, verified fact, refuted premise, or closed issue. A session that only talked still updates §0 if the talk moved anything.

**One worktree per issue, always** (decision 2026-09-06, after two sessions collided in the shared checkout). Before the first edit: `git worktree add C:\Code\lootpath-<n> -b lp-<n>-<slug> origin/main`, then `fetch-libs.ps1` (and `fetch-annotations.ps1` for the LuaLS gate) inside it, because those directories are gitignored. Work, commit, push and open the PR from there; never edit `C:\Code\lootpath` itself while another session may be active; never `git add -A` in a directory you did not create; remove the worktree after the merge. The exact commands are in CLAUDE.md, and every ai-ready issue's "Working in this repo" section points at them.

**Captures and the Encounter Journal.** Captures only read, with one recorded exception: `capture journal` changes the Adventure Guide's view state (tier, instance, difficulty, loot filter, M+ preview level) because the API has no other way to ask for loot, restores what has a getter, and says what it could not restore (§7, 2026-09-06). Nothing any capture calls acts on the character, its items or its money, and no capture calls what a namespace walk finds.
